# CLAUDE.md — W-CONF

Working instructions for coding sessions in this repository. This is the code-relevant subset of the project instructions. The publishing protocol is deliberately not here; it never applies inside a coding session.

## Hard rules

- **Devnet only.** No mainnet writes, at any phase, for any reason. ⚠ ~~The target network is the Confidential Devnet, not the general devnet.~~ **STRUCK 2026-09-01 — falsified by this project's own grade-A row on 2026-08-16 and carried in the HARD RULES for two weeks.** **No dedicated Confidential Devnet is needed or used, and whether one exists elsewhere was never searched** — the prerelease-documented one had a dead faucet, the canonical xrpl.org page names no dedicated network, and all three required amendments are enabled on **standard Devnet**, where everything this project has executed ran. ⚠ **`CLAIMS.md` E-26's own sentence says *"there is no dedicated Confidential Devnet"*, which is a corpus-wide negative its evidence cell does not support** — `amendments-2026-08-16.md:57` records the existence search as never run (P-12), `wss://s.devnet.rippletest.net:51233`, network_id 2. Everything this project has ever executed ran there. ⚠ **Found by a fresh reader of the README who could not open this file** and flagged the contradiction from the README's text alone (E-222). **The rule that survives is the first sentence**, and the target is standard Devnet.
- **Never assert amendment status from memory or a previous session.** Run `tools/check-amendments.ts` and date-stamp the answer.
- **Never claim more security than is delivered.** The standing prohibitions live in `CLAIMS.md`. Read them before writing a README, a comment, a commit message or a log line.
- **A named gap is a correct output; a substituted source is a corrupted one.** If a primary source is unreachable, record it in `CLAIMS.md` and move on. Do not backfill from secondary coverage and do not quietly drop the question.

## Review gates — scale to irreversibility

- Architecture, trust boundaries, cryptographic design, anything to be published: full adversarial rotation before implementation.
- Benchmark harnesses, measurement code, throwaway spikes: **build immediately**. The artifact self-verifies against test vectors and a review round adds nothing. `tools/check-amendments.ts` is in this category.

**Two rounds is the right number for a throwaway spike, and the second round reviews the FIXES.** Learned 2026-08-18 and worth not rediscovering. Round one found a defect the code had. Round two — run against the post-fix branch, because the fixes themselves had been seen by nobody — found a **false-pass mode in the regression test round one had written**, plus two sentences that overstated what the fixes established. Fixing something and describing the fix are separate acts with separate failure modes, and only a fresh reviewer catches the second kind. A third round would review the fixes to the fixes; that is where it stops paying.

⚠ **Apply a fix against the FINDING's own citation list, THEN GREP FOR THE PHRASE.** The first half was learned 2026-08-26 at the cost of a whole extra round; **the second half was learned 2026-08-27 by doing the first half correctly and still missing four sites** (E-168). `REVIEW-025c` recorded all eleven of its findings FIXED and `REVIEW-026` found four were not — every miss the same shape: *a fix applied to the instance the adjudicator was looking at, rather than to the phrase*. One sentence was corrected on the ed25519 test a leg cited and left standing on the identical secp256k1 sentence; another was fixed in a doc comment and left in **the section banner directly above it, inside the line range the leg had named**. ⚠ **A leg's list is line numbers. A corrected sentence is a string, and strings have copies.** `REVIEW-023c` said *"all seven upheld … every one is fixed"*; round two found that **three fixes had not reached sites the findings named by file and line** — two in the architecture document, one an entire `CLAIMS.md` row never touched. The adjudication had summarised *"nine live sentences"* and named six in prose; the leg's report listed all nine with paths. **A sweep re-derived from a summary is E-141's shape one layer up.** Tick the leg's list, not your own recollection of it.

⚠ **Do not commit your own self leg while a repo-access leg is reading the tree.** `REVIEW-022`'s lesson was *don't let files move under a leg*. The neighbouring one, learned 2026-08-26: **don't let your own answers appear in the tree a leg is reading.** Write the self leg to a scratchpad and commit it after the legs return. On the day this was learned the independence survived only because the Codex leg noticed the checkout was not the frozen commit and read the subject through `git show <frozen>:<path>` instead.

Record rejected reviewer claims with reasons so later sessions do not relitigate settled decisions.

## Preconditions before a build milestone

The spec exists, the architecture has passed adversarial review, and TASK ZERO is complete with its gaps recorded.

**Status 2026-08-18, revised late the same day.** GAP-1 and GAP-3 are closed. ADR-0001 §(a)'s threat-model question is **ruled** (issue #1: a hostile same-host process is in scope), which answers REVIEW-003b's MEDIUM and settles §(e)'s nonce half — implemented, reviewed twice, and executed on Devnet. The FFI blocker is closed (issue #2, path A, merged). **§(e)'s keygen half is now ruled too** (REVIEW-012, adjudicated REVIEW-012c), and the three long-missing primary documents are resolved — rippled 3.3.0 and the XLS-0096 text obtained, the Trail of Bits May 2026 revision recorded as an unobtainable gap (B-13).

**Status 2026-08-27 — B-16 is RULED, BUILT, EXECUTED and REVIEWED TWICE. `REVIEW-024` ruled it; `REVIEW-025` found eleven things wrong with the build, none of them the construction; `REVIEW-026` found that `REVIEW-025c` had OVERSTATED ITS OWN FIXES (E-168). All four of §7's conditions are met.** `REVIEW-024`, three independent legs (Gemini 9.0, Codex 8, self leg 7.5), **all ruling Option B**: the issue #1 ruling extends to the ed25519 signing nonce and the residual is **not** accepted. The RFC 8032 nonce prefix is hedged — 32 fresh CSPRNG bytes, tagged-hashed under a fixed DST and **XORed** over it, the same idiom site 1 uses — through `ed25519_dalek::hazmat::raw_sign` with the scalar, the public key, the challenge transcript and the signature equation untouched. **B-14 is now closed on both halves.**

**Status 2026-08-27, later the same day — B-21 is BUILT and REVIEWED TWICE, and building it proved B-19 WRONG (E-171).** `zeroize` is a direct `wconf-signer` dependency; `sha512_zeroizing`, `fresh_aux` and `OneShotAux` carry `Zeroizing`. 267 tests, three green runs, `make check-zeroize-bounds` is the falsifier — now **four parts**. `REVIEW-027` (three legs) and `REVIEW-028` (round two, the fixes). ⚠ **Eight leg-readings across three rounds, NOT ONE defect in the construction** — every finding was evidence, a gate, or a sentence. ~~⚠ **STILL OPEN: no Devnet run on the default signing path since B-21 landed**~~ — **EXECUTED at HEAD, `EF2018FA13AF7DCECD1D7562DE0BDF9A870688FAD6E4E564323B2AA1386FD336` (E-176).** `REVIEW-027` finding 8 is closed. ⚠ **The script now writes a receipt PER RUN and refuses to overwrite one from another commit** — E-169's mechanism, blocked rather than warned about.

**Status 2026-08-27, later still — B-22 is RULED, BUILT and REVIEWED TWICE, and round two found the build had NOT implemented its own ruling.** The key-derivation path wipes: `sha512half_zeroizing`, the ed25519 private key, both derivation closures, `derive_scalar`'s every rejected candidate, `root`/`intermediate`/`sum`, both heap seed decoders and their internals, and two `Copy`-source defects in `keygen.rs`. **268 tests at the time, three green runs, all six gates PASS. `make check-zeroize-bounds` is the falsifier — ~~now FIVE parts~~ five parts AT B-22; it is SIX as of B-23** (**274 tests as of B-24**), the fifth still pinning **thirteen named sites** — ⚠ **one was RETIRED by B-24, not lost: `Zeroizing(entropy)`'s `Copy`-source guard is gone because the producer now hands out a wiping type, so there is no bare source to guard** — with three controls that each run through the real detector. ⚠ **Corrected by the commit that falsified it** — a count in a status block is a claim about the CURRENT tree, and B-23 is the next commit after this sentence was written. `REVIEW-029` (the decision) and `REVIEW-030` (round two, the fixes, **two external legs** — which is what B-21's round two lacked).

**Five things B-22 established which a session will otherwise get wrong:**

- ⚠⚠ **WRAPPING A `Copy` TYPE IN A ZEROIZING WRAPPER DOES NOT WIPE THE SOURCE, AND THIS SESSION HIT IT FOUR TIMES.** `Zeroizing(entropy)` on a `[u8; 16]`, `Zeroizing(*sk.as_bytes())` on a `[u8; 32]`, and `Zeroizing::new(sha512half(&m))` — **the last of these was written INTO THE COMMIT THAT FIXED THE FIRST**, four hundred lines away, and found by both round-two legs (E-178, E-181). ⚠ **A wrapper around a `Copy` value copies; the original binding stays live.** The fix is either to bind and wipe the source, or — better — to build the value *into* the wiping buffer so no bare temporary ever exists.
- ⚠⚠ **THE RULING SAID THREE SCALARS WERE IN SCOPE, THE BUILD WIPED NONE OF THEM, AND A COMMENT CALLED THAT "THE CRATE'S BOUNDARY, NOT AN OMISSION".** Both round-two legs found it independently; a compile probe then showed `k256::NonZeroScalar` **and** `k256::Scalar` both implement `Zeroize`, so nothing had prevented it (E-181). ⚠ **This is precisely what `CLAUDE.md`'s two-round rule predicts — the construction was sound and the DESCRIPTION was hiding a real gap.** A session that reads its own fix report instead of the ruling will ship this.
- ⚠⚠ **A `Zeroizing` WRAPPED AROUND AN EXISTING BINDING HAS NO COMPILE-TIME FALSIFIER, AND THAT IS MEASURED (E-179).** Reverting `sha512half_zeroizing`'s **return type** breaks the build; reverting `Zeroizing::new(base58check_decode(..))` to the bare call gives **zero errors**, because `Zeroizing<T>` derefs to `T` and every use site compiles identically. ⚠ **Applying the revert machinery to those sites would have shipped a false pass of E-164's shape.** A source check is the honest instrument, and its guarantee is over a **NAMED SET** — a gate cannot prove no unlisted site exists.
- ⚠⚠ **A SOURCE-CHECK GATE THAT COUNTS A NEEDLE ANYWHERE FALSE-PASSES ON A COMMENTED-OUT LINE, and its controls must run through the REAL detector (E-182).** Part 5 shipped with both defects and both round-two legs found both: commenting out a production line still satisfied its needle, and the two controls re-ran the same count / checked an unrelated absence. ⚠ **Third instance in this repository of a gate built to catch false passes false-passing** (E-165, E-168). **Strip comments, and make every control call the same function the real check calls.**
- ⚠⚠ **A COUNT WAS WRONG AGAIN — SEVENTH TIME — AND IT WAS INSIDE THE ROW ABOUT MEASURING RATHER THAN ASSUMING.** *"2 compile errors"* is **one**: `grep -c '^error'` counted rustc's *"could not compile … due to 1 previous error"* **summary line**. ⚠ **`grep -c '^error'` IS NOT AN ERROR COUNT.** `CLAIMS.md` E-179, corrected by E-181.

**Status 2026-08-28 — B-23 is BUILT, EXECUTED and REVIEWED, and round one found it had NOT fully implemented its own ruling. The fixes are B-24 and THEY are unreviewed.** ⚠ **This heading previously read *"the seed's owned lifetime wipes"* flatly, and `REVIEW-031b` was right that it was false** — four decoded `[u8; 16]` arrays were bare, including in the entry point every profile load calls. **B-24 fixed the producer.** What wipes now: `raw`, `RawProfile::account_seed`, the `seed` local, `ask_secret`'s return, and `read_line`'s buffer. **274 tests, three green runs, all six gates PASS.** `make check-zeroize-bounds` is **SIX parts**; part 6 pins **ten checks**, and its count is now **derived from the list** because restating it in prose drifted twice (E-199). Devnet `3386E9F5…D3A48` at `6efc8ab` for B-23, and **`50627A0B…2B980`, validated, clean tree at `9878046` for B-24** — ⚠ **re-run because B-24 changed the decode path every profile load takes.** ⚠⚠ **AND B-22's ROUND TWO IS EXACTLY WHAT HAPPENED AGAIN.** `REVIEW-031` ran three legs with **deliberately differed packets** — Gemini inline, Codex read-only repo, self — and upheld **nine findings**, rejecting one on a measurement. ⚠ **The worst was not a defect but a PROCESS failure: `REVIEW-030b` had ALREADY reported the bare entropy, and `REVIEW-030c` answered the comment the finding quoted instead of the defect it named (E-194).** ⚠ **A finding is not discharged by correcting the prose it cited.** **Round two — reviewing B-24's fixes — is OWED and not run.**

**Status 2026-08-28, later the same day — B-24 is REVIEWED (`REVIEW-032`, three legs) and round two found a LIVE SECRET UNWIPED. The fixes are B-25.** ⚠⚠ **`keygen::finish()` never wiped `sk` — the DERIVED ELGAMAL SECRET — and the comment beside it said `sk` was *"a `k256` type this crate does not own"*. It is `crate::scalar::Scalar`, thirty lines away, `Copy` with no `Drop`, and `grep -n Zeroize signer/src/scalar.rs` returned nothing (E-200).** ⚠ **That is E-181's shape written by the session that had E-181 in front of it — the rule was carried, in these words, and it was not self-executing.** **Fixed by the producer: `Scalar` is no longer `Copy` and is `ZeroizeOnDrop`, so `sk_A`, `r`, `ρ` and every blinding factor wipe — measured first, ZERO compile errors.** Devnet `63D81873…B663E8`, validated, clean tree at `8acb334`. **278 tests** — ⚠ **this said 281 before anyone ran it, which is the TWELFTH instance and it happened inside the milestone whose subject is hand-restated counts (E-201). 274 at B-24, plus four: two `Scalar` wiping tests, the both-entry-points KAT, and the error-variant test. Arithmetic, checked against `cargo test`** — **three green runs, all six gates PASS**; part 6 is **THIRTEEN checks** — ⚠ **and this said *"with ten controls"*, which was the THIRTEENTH stale count: it printed twelve. B-26 made the number DERIVED and it is eighteen now (E-203).** ⚠ **The second HIGH: part 6's decoder needles matched the SIGNATURE only, so B-24's headline fix could be undone in the body with the gate still green (E-201).**

**Status 2026-08-28, later still — B-25 is REVIEWED (`REVIEW-033`, ROUND THREE, three legs) and `REVIEW-033c` ruled CONVERGENCE REACHED. The fixes are B-26.** ⚠⚠ **The HIGH is that `dropping_a_scalar_overwrites_its_bytes` — THE ONLY REAL FALSIFIER B-25 HAD — was UNDEFINED BEHAVIOUR.** It took a `*const u8` from a **shared** reference, then called `ManuallyDrop::drop(&mut held)`, which retags **Unique**, then read through the stale pointer. ⚠ **Its own docstring said *"a test that is undefined behaviour proves nothing"* — about the version it replaced.** **BOTH external legs found it and NEITHER could run Miri; the self leg could.** Measured under both borrow models (E-202). ⚠⚠ **THE REMEDY WAS MEASURED, AND ONE OF THREE SOUND CANDIDATES WAS REJECTED FOR PASSING**: a `Zeroize::zeroize`-and-read-back version is Miri-clean and **passes with `Drop::drop` emptied**, because it exercises `Zeroize` and the property is `Drop`. **A sound replacement is not automatically a falsifier.** **279 tests, three green runs, all six gates PASS**; part 6 carries **eighteen controls, counted by the helper that runs them**. **Devnet `79F16C9A…58A59A`, validated, clean tree at `080a1a8`** — ⚠ **re-run because the `U256` `diff` wipe is inside `reduce_wide_mod`, which produces `r`, `ρ` and `sk_A`.**

**Five things `REVIEW-033` established which a session will otherwise get wrong:**

- ⚠⚠ **A GATE BUILT TO CATCH FALSE PASSES FALSE-PASSED FOR THE FOURTH ROUND RUNNING, AND FOR THE THIRD TIME THE REASON WAS CONTROLS THAT REPLAY THE LAST ROUND'S ATTACK.** `single_decode_findings` missed **five** more: a `pub fn` helper, `fn name (` with a space, a closure **called twice**, a decode in a `for` loop, and a second decode hidden between `"/*"` and `"*/"` string literals. ⚠ **Its helper sweep matched `\nfn ` while `_fn_body` TEN LINES ABOVE handled both spellings — the two disagreed and only the looser one was a gate.** The fix had been written against the finding's list, not the property.
- ⚠⚠ **THERE IS NO "SAFE DIRECTION" WHEN A FUNCTION FEEDS TWO CONSUMERS WITH OPPOSITE POLARITY, AND THAT SENTENCE HAS NOW BEEN RETRACTED THREE TIMES IN THREE ROUNDS.** `find_missing` wants a needle PRESENT, so over-stripping is safe; `single_decode_findings` enforces a **MAXIMUM**, so over-stripping is the **unsafe** direction. ⚠ **A safe-direction argument is only as good as the list of consumers, and the list grew after the argument was written.** The stripper now **skips string literals** rather than arguing about them, which closes the hiding attack and the raw-string synthesis with one mechanism (E-203).
- ⚠⚠ **A COUNT DERIVED FROM SOURCE STRUCTURE IS NOT DERIVED FROM BEHAVIOUR — THIRTEENTH INSTANCE.** *"Ten controls"* over twelve: reading the file counts nine `print("  CONTROL` sites, **four of which are one `print` inside a loop**. ⚠ **The thirteen checks were right because they came from `len(B23_SITES)`.** Controls are now incremented by `_p6_control`, the helper that runs them.
- ⚠⚠ **THE RESIDUAL A DOCSTRING SAID WAS "NAMED IN PART 6" WAS NAMED NOWHERE.** `grep` found the citation and nothing it pointed at. ⚠ **A citation to a section that does not exist reads exactly like a disclosure.** `what_part_6_does_not_say()` now exists and prints in the gate's output — and **the two shapes a lexical check CANNOT catch are asserted-uncaught controls: if either is ever caught the gate FAILS**, because the disclosure would then be stale.
- ⚠⚠ **B-25's REASON FOR LEAVING THE `U256` RESIDUAL WAS A FALSE DICHOTOMY, AND ALL THREE LEGS FOUND IT.** *"Removing `Copy` to add a destructor would rewrite the routine"* — ⚠ **wiping does not require a destructor, and only a destructor requires removing `Copy`.** One line, zero compile errors, `cargo test --lib` unchanged. ⚠ **E-181's and E-200's shape, written into the file that documents the fix for those two.** The `diff` half is closed; ⚠ **`acc` is still a residual and is NOT the same one-liner** — it is returned by value, so wiping it is a change of ownership.

⚠ **`CLAUDE.md`'s two-round rule is NOT amended, and the condition matters.** The handover made amendment conditional on round three finding a **construction defect**; it found none, and `REVIEW-033b` affirmed the construction positively. ⚠ **What round three DID establish is the condition under which a third round is owed: round two's HIGH was a live secret unwiped behind a false comment, and round three's is an aliasing violation in a TEST. When the findings move from the construction to the INSTRUMENTS THAT CHECK IT, that is convergence and it stops.** A third round is owed when round two's stopping condition never fired — not by default.

**Five things `REVIEW-032` established which a session will otherwise get wrong:**

- ⚠⚠ **A COMMENT NAMED THE WRONG CRATE AND THAT IS WHY A SECRET WENT UNWIPED.** The `k256` in `finish()`'s comment belongs to `compressed_public_key`, eight lines down; **the neighbouring function's dependency was read into this one's comment**, and it then answered *"why is this not wiped?"* for every later reader — including **two passes by the self leg**. ⚠ **The instrument that catches this is not a rule, it is one `grep -n Zeroize` on the file the comment names.** E-181 was closed by exactly that probe on `k256::Scalar` and the probe was not re-run one file over.
- ⚠⚠ **A NEEDLE THAT PINS A SIGNATURE DOES NOT PIN A WIPE.** `let mut out = [0u8; 16]; … Ok(Zeroizing::new(out))` keeps the return type and restores E-178 — **measured, both needles still counted 1**. ⚠ **B-24 had no compile-time falsifier and said so; the source pin was the whole warrant, and it was blind to the milestone's likeliest regression.** Needles now span **signature AND construction AND return**.
- ⚠⚠ **CONTROLS THAT REPLAY THE LAST ROUND'S ATTACK PROVE THE GATE CATCHES THE LAST ATTACK.** `single_decode_findings` ran `REVIEW-031b`'s exact mutation and missed **four** others — including a second decode **inside `derive_raw` itself**, through the *other* decoder. ⚠ **It grepped one spelling; it now counts every decoder by name** and covers the wrappers and helpers. **Ask what the control does NOT run.**
- ⚠⚠ **VERIFY A TEST BY BREAKING IT — INCLUDING ONE YOU WROTE FIVE MINUTES AGO.** `a_scalar_is_not_copy`, written in this fix round, **false-passed under the complete regression**: a blanket `impl<T> NotCopy for T` always applies, so the `Copy` bound was never consulted. ⚠ **Deleted rather than repaired** — `Copy` and `Drop` are mutually exclusive, so the property was already covered. ⚠ **And a MARKER TRAIT CAN LIE**: `ZeroizeOnDrop` still passed when `Drop::drop` was emptied, which is why the byte-level test exists beside it.
- ⚠⚠ **`Copy` AND `Drop` ARE MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE, SO "MAKE IT WIPE" MEANS "REMOVE `Copy`" — AND THAT COST NOTHING.** Removing `Copy` from `Scalar` gave **zero** compile errors because every caller already took `&Scalar`. ⚠ **Measured before the comment was written, which is the rule E-196 was created by breaking.**

⚠ **B-25's residual is NAMED, not closed:** `reduce_wide_mod`'s `U256` accumulator and its 512 per-iteration `diff` values are bare, and are deliberately not fixed — `U256` is the constant-time arithmetic type and removing `Copy` would rewrite the routine whose constant-time argument is why it is written that way. **Same species as B-21's `Sha512` block buffer.**

**Five things B-23 established which a session will otherwise get wrong:**

- ⚠⚠ **A COPY CAN BE DELETED RATHER THAN WIPED, AND THAT IS THE STRONGER FIX.** The profile was parsed **twice**, and the first parse built a `serde_json::Value` holding the seed — a copy `Zeroize` cannot reach at all. ⚠ **The check it fed reads FIELD NAMES**, so `BTreeMap<String, IgnoredAny>` skips every value in place and **the seed is never allocated a second time**. **B-22's *build it into the wiping buffer* generalises to *do not make the copy*** (E-183).
- ⚠⚠ **THE B-22 REQUEST CALLED `:162` AN OPEN TECHNICAL QUESTION AND IT TOOK THREE LINES.** `#[serde(deserialize_with = ..)]` over `String::deserialize(d).map(Zeroizing::new)` — no feature, no fork, no `unsafe`. ⚠ **And it is a MOVE, so E-178 lands the OPPOSITE way**: `String` is not `Copy`, nothing is duplicated, no source binding survives. **This is B-14's shape again — an unpriced blocker that dissolved on contact** (E-184).
- ⚠⚠ **E-168, THIRD INSTANCE, AND THIS TIME THE RULING'S OWN LIST WAS INCOMPLETE.** `REVIEW-029c` named four sites by line; the idiom at `holder.rs:309` **has three copies**, and the other two are `onboarding.rs:537`/`:615`, **both master-seed prompts**. ⚠ **This file already carried the rule in these words and it still took a deliberate grep. The rule is not self-executing** (E-185).
- ⚠⚠ **`String::new()` + `read_line` GREW THE BUFFER, so the seed was copied through a chain of allocations and every earlier one was freed unwiped.** ⚠ **Wrapping the final buffer would have wiped the LAST LINK AND LEFT THE REST** — E-178's shape with a heap allocation — **and it would have read as a complete fix.** Reserved up front instead. **A bound, not a guarantee** (E-186).
- ⚠⚠ **FIVE OF SEVEN REVERTS COMPILE CLEAN, AND THAT WAS MEASURED BEFORE THE GATE WAS WRITTEN.** Only `account_seed`'s type and `ask_secret`'s return break the build — both `(type)`, both **coupled** to a second site, so reverting both halves together compiles. ⚠ **The other five are a SOURCE check, because a revert that compiles proves nothing** (E-188). ⚠ **The gate REFUSED its own extension loudly** — the first time in this repository a gate failed rather than false-passed (E-190).

⚠ **Two counts and one test reach less far than their names suggest, all three stated at the site.** Part 6's header said *"5 of 7"* over **eight** needles before commit — E-141's eighth instance, in the part written to avoid it (E-189). And `the_two_seed_dispatches_agree` catches the two dispatches **drifting**, **not** a revert to the double decode, which is behaviourally identical and passes it — **E-164 a third time** (E-191).

⚠ **B-23's residuals are NAMED, not closed:** `serde_json`'s escape-decoding scratch, `read_to_string`'s growth if the file changes mid-read, and a pasted line over the 4096-byte reservation. **P-5 governs all of it — this narrows a window and removes no channel.** ⚠ **Both `REVIEW-029` legs rejected scoping any of this by profile regime** — *"already on disk"* is a **disclosed** residual, not a reason to keep an **avoidable** memory one.

**Three things B-21 established which a session will otherwise get wrong:**

- ⚠ **`ed25519-dalek`'s `zeroize` FEATURE WAS NEVER OFF, and three documents said it was.** `std = ["alloc", ..]`, and `alloc` lists **`"zeroize/alloc"` without the `?` sigil** that `serde?/alloc` two lines above it carries — so the historical `features = ["std"]` already resolved to `alloc,std,zeroize`. **E-167's nine-day story describes something that did not happen**, and B-19's falsifier — *"reverting the manifest line fails to COMPILE"* — is **false**: it compiles. ⚠ **A CARGO FEATURE AND AN OPTIONAL DEPENDENCY CAN SHARE A NAME, and `dep/feat` without `?` enables the feature.** `cargo tree -f '{p} {f}'` is what shows it; reading the manifest is not. ⚠ **Three `REVIEW-025` legs and a `REVIEW-026` round accepted it — and round two corrected that exact feature list** (finding 10: *"the manifest has `hazmat` too"*) **without asking what `std` pulls in.** The word that made the row false was on the line being corrected.
- ⚠⚠ **A COUNT IN A FIX REPORT IS A CLAIM WITH NO FALSIFIER, AND THIS SESSION GOT ONE WRONG FOUR TIMES RUNNING (E-172, E-175).** *"Fixed at all four live sites"* → six → nine, each found by the next sweep, each correction asserting a new number. This file already carried three clauses of advice about it — *tick the leg's list* (E-141), *a leg's list is line numbers, a corrected sentence is a string* (E-168), and this session's own *grep the diff you just wrote for the claim you just falsified* (E-172). **All three are advice, and advice is what kept failing.** ⚠ **`check-zeroize-bounds` part 4 is what ended it**: it scans every live file for the retracted phrases, fails on any lacking a correction marker, exempts `docs/reviews/` and carries a control that must detect an injected one. **E-137 and E-141 were counts corrected by counts and both recurred.** ⚠ **The corollary lands on this file's own rule: *a gate that reports a number is claiming what the number counts* applies to ADJUDICATIONS too.**
- ⚠ **A GATE NOBODY RAN IS A CLAIM, and this is the purest instance the project has.** `CLAIMS.md` E-165 and E-168 already said *verify a gate by breaking it* — about two gates that **existed**. B-19's gate had never been built, only described, in the register row, the decision §5 and a test's doc comment. It was found only because B-21 needed a real one and included the B-19 arm **on the strength of that sentence**. ⚠ **The new gate carries a control in BOTH directions** — an empty feature set that must fail, a comment edit that must still build — because `check-test-support-gate` and `revert-sweep.py` each false-passed before they worked (E-165).

**Four things the BUILD established which a session will otherwise get wrong:**

- ⚠ **A REVERT PASSED, and reading the test would never have found it (E-164).** `REVIEW-024` §6 item 3 — *a successful all-zero draw stays hedged* — had a test named for it, and reverting `fresh_aux` to treat a zero draw as an omission **left all 263 tests green**. The test drove `Hedge::FixedAux([0u8; 32])`, which enters one function *after* the decision is made. **Third instance of one failure mode in this file**: `aux_is_exactly_one_field_of_entropy`, then `one_implementation_not_two` (E-161), then this. **A test named for a property, one layer away from where the property breaks.**
- ⚠ **A GATE BUILT TO CATCH FALSE PASSES FALSE-PASSED, and a control naming `0u8` was certified "gated" (E-165).** `check-test-support-gate.ts` matched the compiler's output against each symbol name — which matches the **echoed source line**. Both `REVIEW-025` external legs found it. Its sibling: `revert-sweep.py` accepted **any** nonzero exit as *"the test failed"*. **Both are fixed and both were re-verified by breaking them**; the fixed sweep immediately caught four stale expected-test names the old rule could not see.
- ⚠ **`0xED` IS NOT SUFFICIENT DEVNET EVIDENCE, and neither is `tesSUCCESS`.** A conforming verifier cannot tell a hedged signature from a deterministic one. The script re-derives the signing serialisation **from the submitted blob**, computes what pure RFC 8032 would give over those same bytes with `ripple-keypairs`, and asserts the blob's `TxnSignature` **differs** while both verify under the same key. ⚠ It also **rebuilds the signer and records `builtFromCommit`** — the first run executed whatever binary was at `target/debug/`, which both legs called out.
- ⚠ **TWO FALSE-PASS GATES, AND ROUND TWO FOUND A THIRD MODE IN THE SAME TWO ARTIFACTS (E-165, E-168).** The `revert-sweep` had **no baseline**, so it certified a mutation whenever an expected test was in the failure set — *including when that test was already failing for an unrelated reason*. It now proves the unmodified target green first and requires a **newly** failing expected test. ⚠ **A gate that reports a number is claiming what the number counts**, and both of this session's gates got that wrong before they got it right.
- ⚠ **FOUR DOCUMENTS CITED A DEVNET TRANSACTION NO COMMITTED RECEIPT RECORDS (E-169).** The run was repeated from a clean tree *after* the prose was written; the receipt was overwritten and **nothing re-read the prose against it**. **A receipt is only evidence if the prose is checked against it, and nothing gates that.**
- ⚠ **A LEG GRADED A HIGH DEFECT THAT DOES NOT EXIST, from its own excerpt of a file the packet supplied IN FULL (E-170).** `restore()` is the first statement in the loop the leg said omits it. **E-160 was an adjudicator's excerpt bounding a leg; this is a leg excerpting for itself.** ⚠ **The point beneath two other false premises was still worth taking** — discarding a whole report because its diagnosis is wrong loses the real weakness underneath.
- ⚠ **The MASK has no outside check and cannot get one.** `@noble/curves` controls the *arithmetic* — the KAT generator refuses to emit unless its unmasked arm reproduces `ed25519.sign` byte for byte — but **no third party computes a W-CONF-hedged signature**, so the combiner rests on two readings of the same prose, both this project's. Stated in the test rather than argued away.

**Four things that ruling established which a session will otherwise get wrong:**

- ~~⚠ **`signer/src/xrpl.rs` STILL SIGNS ED25519 WITH PURE RFC 8032** … **Ruled is not built.**~~ **STRUCK 2026-08-27 — IT IS BUILT AND EXECUTED (E-163).** All eight of §6's gates have run, ending in a validated Devnet transaction on the default path with `SigningPubKey` decoded out of the submitted blob (`5A8CF0B4…`). *"The signer hedges ed25519"* is the accurate sentence now. ⚠ **The caveat that replaces it is one step along and is LIVE: built and executed is not REVIEWED.** §7 item 4 wants two rounds and the second reviews the fixes; `REVIEW-025c` is round one.
- ⚠ **B-14's reason for deferring this was FALSE, and that is what unblocked it.** *"Rolling one is worse than the gap"* was right; *"randomising it needs hand-rolling a signature scheme"* was not. `SigningKey::try_sign` **already is** `ExpandedSecretKey::raw_sign::<Sha512>`, and the nonce prefix enters at one line. A B-14 leg asserted `SigningKey::sign_with_rng`, which `ed25519-dalek` 2.2.0 does not have — **a wrong API surface produced a wrong price and deferred a live-path question for a week.**
- ⚠ **A successful draw of THIRTY-TWO ZERO BYTES stays hedged; only a REPORTED entropy failure omits the mask** and degrades to byte-identical RFC 8032. This is `REVIEW-018` §4a's distinction running the other way, and a session that assumes zeros recover RFC 8032 will write a false test.
- ⚠ **THREE-LEG UNANIMITY WAS CHEAP, and the packet is why.** Both external legs ruled from crate source *the adjudicator selected and pasted in*, so agreement is one reading distributed rather than three confirmations. Proved by a leg getting it wrong in the direction the packet steered: Codex reported `raw_sign_byupdate` as `pub(crate)` and unavailable, and it is `pub` at `hazmat.rs:210` — **a line the packet's excerpt omitted.** `CLAUDE.md`'s own rule, landing on the person who wrote it: **an excerpt is a diff by another name** (E-160).

**Status 2026-08-25 — architecture §6 step 7 is BUILT AND EXECUTED, under an AMENDED B-15.** `facility/` is the facility: the Dedicated Account, the single conversion, supplier enrolment, drawdowns, repayments, the balance book (TASK ZERO §9's R-5 / F-8 requirement) and retirement in tranches. **81 tests, a real type-check (`bunx tsc --noEmit`), and four gates.** ⚠ **The count read 63 until 2026-08-26 and was stale even at `REVIEW-023`'s tree, which runs 77.** **28 validated facility transactions on Devnet.** ⚠ **NOT under B-15's step-7 conditions**: Option A — loopback — was ruled, taken and then **costed**, and it is not payable on this hardware (E-148: two runs, ~2.5 hours, 6.9 GB of node store, `complete_ledgers: empty` throughout, one OOM kill and one hour pegged at 1663% CPU). The project owner **amended** the ruling to extend `REVIEW-019`'s named risk acceptance to the demo (**E-151**). `tapFailHard` — the half that removes the *consequence* of a wrong read **at the ledger**, and ⚠ **not at the node the blob was submitted to** (F-28) — was set on every facility movement; **loopback, the half that removes the *risk*, was not**, and every run record carries `underB15Conditions: false` to say so.

**Three things step 7's build established that a session will otherwise get wrong:**

- ⚠ **Option A's real cost is a full Devnet sync, and there is NO rehearsal network.** `signer/src/ledger.rs` hardcodes `DEVNET_NETWORK_ID = 2` and *"refuses every network but Devnet, at any phase, for any reason"*, so a local standalone ledger would have to claim `network_id` 2 to drive the signer — a local ledger presenting itself as Devnet, which is the substitution this project forbids. That was not in the decision request's cost table and it is now in architecture §6 step 7.
- **`tapFailHard` is verified at source AND EXECUTED, both arms.** rippled 3.3.0 `Transactor.cpp:1645-1651` discards a `tec` under the flag and `NetworkOPs.cpp:1833` stops it being kept or relayed. On Devnet 2026-08-25 the same signed bytes gave `applied=false`, **absent from the validated ledger, no `Sequence` consumed** with the flag — and `applied=true`, on the ledger, `Sequence` advanced, without it. ⚠ **The arms are separated by three ledger closes**, because submitting them back to back cannot distinguish *"A discarded, B applied"* from *"A applied, B a duplicate"*.
- ⚠ **Splitting sign-from-submit is what makes "no path omits the flag" true.** The first draft had one `signAndSubmit` that did both, so every caller submitted each blob **twice** and the second submission could only fail. Found by reading the file against its callers; **no test could see it**, because the fake node answers `tesSUCCESS` to every submit.

~~⚠ **B-15's step-7 gate is still shut**~~ — **ANSWERED 2026-08-25**: loopback for the facility's reads, `tapFailHard` set by whatever submits, which is **not** the signer. ⚠ **The ruling had no adversarial review**, and it is not one of `REVIEW-019`'s three options as stated. **Nothing forces a separate submitter to set the flag**, so that half is a convention rather than a property. `docs/decisions/DECISION-REQUEST-b15-step7-ledger-source-2026-08-24.md`.

**Three things step 7's opening established that a session will otherwise get wrong:**

- **Building a transaction type can DELETE a capability.** `REFUSED_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` had exactly one producer — the arms that refused ConvertBack — so building it made a documented refusal code unreachable. The variant was **removed**, because a code no client can receive is a client handling an answer that never arrives (E-130). An operation this signer does not build is now refused **before a grant can be minted for it**.
- **Our own facility walkthrough had a false disclosure sentence, and `claims-check` had already passed it.** *"Public: the aggregate change"* — but `MPTAmount` is a plaintext field on **every individual** ConvertBack, so retiring a facility in tranches publishes **every tranche size** (F-24). Two ConvertBacks read 95 and 191 straight off their validated transactions.
- **`tesSUCCESS` was not accepted as the evidence, and that is the standard now.** All four of XLS-0096 §10.5's state changes were checked against ledger state before and after — public balance, `ConfidentialOutstandingAmount`, version, and the **decrypted** remainder. A `tesSUCCESS` alone does not distinguish a correct homomorphic subtraction from a lucky one.

**Status 2026-08-20 — architecture §6 step 6 is BUILT AND EXECUTED.** Real key generation and onboarding. **Issue #11 is discharged**: `lsfDisableMaster`, a regular key and a signer list each — on their own — flip the recommended key source to `standalone` and make the ruling's condition-2 warning mandatory, and the disclosures reach the holder **before the seed is asked for**. ⚠ **Issue #10 is NOT discharged and no code discharges it**: the derivation is specified in one repository document and is not a published standard, so a holder who has their seed and not this repository cannot reconstruct their key — which is the entire benefit the ruling trades a permanent decryption credential for. **Publishing an XLS informational draft is a Desktop-project decision, not a coding-session one.**

**Three things step 6 established that a session will otherwise get wrong:**

- **The ElGamal key now has TWO named sources**, and a profile declares which. `derived-from-master-seed` is the ruled construction and carries **no ElGamal secret in the profile at all**; `standalone` is the library's CSPRNG in `elgamal_sk`/`elgamal_pk` — which is what every profile before this milestone silently was. An absent `elgamal_key_source` reads as `standalone` and is **not** upgraded.
- **A registered key is not "unrecoverable", and saying so is a defect.** The protocol has no rotation and no recovery (P-10) — that is about **XLS-96**. A *derived* key is reconstructible from the master seed under the W-CONF derivation, which is the only thing deriving buys, and the confirmation said the opposite for one commit (E-125). ⚠ These are two sentences about two layers and merging them is how the banner also came to claim XLS-0096 governs XRPL account keys (E-126).
- **`from_parts` accepted a public key that was not `sk·G`.** Now `try_from_parts`, which recomputes it. The failure it allowed is the worst one this protocol offers: register a key nobody holds the secret for, encrypt every balance to it, and have every step succeed (E-120).

**ADR-0001 is `ACCEPTED` as of 2026-08-18** (`REVIEW-013c`). The last item — re-weigh Option 2 (browser WASM) on risk alone — was run: two independent legs, **both ruling Option 3**, both handed the strongest arguments against it. **Option 3 is confirmed on risk alone**, which is what rev 3 asked for and what the ADR previously could not do.

**Three things that rotation established, which a session will otherwise get wrong:**

- **The build-cost table was INVERTED, not merely stale.** Option 2 (browser WASM) is now the **cheapest** option — three published npm packages, no patching, no vendoring, no build step (E-53) — and Option 3 is the **most expensive**: a vendored C fork, an FFI binding, a Rust binary, OS-mediated IPC, a separate confirmation UI. **Option 3 was chosen anyway, on risk.** Do not repeat "Option 2 is expensive"; it is not.
- **An argument this project made three times was FALSE.** "Before the keygen ruling, an Option 2 page compromise did not touch spending authority" — false; the ADR's own table says Option 2's page can spend. The correct statement is that the keygen ruling **removes Option 2's escape hatch** (the browser can no longer delegate signing and hold only an ElGamal key, because the derivation input is the master seed) and **welds a rotatable exposure to an unrotatable one**. Use that form.
- ~~**`Accepted` unblocks architecture §6 step 7 and nothing else.** Steps 4 and 6 have their own gates, still shut.~~ **Both gates are now open.** Step 4 was built 2026-08-18 against A4–A9; step 6 was built and executed 2026-08-20. ⚠ **Step 7's own gates are not** — B-15 requires loopback, corroboration or a `tapFailHard` submitter, ADR-0002 is `Proposed`, and E-117's custody cost is unpriced.

**Rulings closed 2026-08-20** (see `docs/reviews/`): **B-14** — the transaction's ECDSA nonce is **hedged**, because the issue #1 ruling extends to it (`REVIEW-018`, E-109); ~~⚠ **secp256k1 only, ed25519 is B-16 and is the live path.**~~ **B-16 IS RULED TOO — 2026-08-27, `REVIEW-024`, E-158 — and NOT BUILT** (see the 2026-08-27 status block above). **B-15** — one ledger endpoint stands for this artifact **as a named risk acceptance** with a mandatory disclosure, and is a **gate on step 7** (`REVIEW-019`, E-110). **ADR-0002 round two** produced **revision 3**, which splits a Decision that asserted what the ADR reopened two sections later (`REVIEW-017c`, E-111) — **Decision A, native XLS-66 rejected, is settled and is what building proceeds on; Decision B, an XLS-65 `Vault` on the funding side, is open and gates the funding side only.**

⚠ **ADR-0002 is STILL `Proposed` — but on item 4 alone, which is research and not a decision.** ~~Its item 2 is a scope question that the ADR reserves for whoever owns scope~~ — **discharged 2026-08-20 in rev 4 (E-115), and NOT on the scope question.** Blocker 1 makes the hybrid a hard fork: `Vault` share tokens cannot be confidential, so it forces **native share accounting XOR confidential investor positions**, and the accounting is commodity while the confidentiality is the whole premise. ⚠ **The funder count therefore stops mattering** — one funder or twenty, all are confidential MPT holders and the architecture does not fork. ⚠ **Reversal condition:** if `lsfMPTCanHoldConfidentialBalance` ever becomes settable on a `Vault` share issuance, re-argue it. A leg argued the question dissolved on *structural* grounds instead; that was checked and rejected (E-112).

**Not blockers of ADR-0001, but still open:**

- **The Bulletproof half of the randomness story** is issue #8, where the blocking question is not which construction but whether determinism is acceptable at all when the scalars provide the zero-knowledge property. Tracked separately; REVIEW-010c named keygen as the *last packet*, and it has landed.
- **The backlog is now ONE ruling, not three.** Sigma nonces: implemented and executed. **`r`/`ρ`: implemented 2026-08-18** in `signer/src/randomness.rs`, reproducing all nine published vectors from a second implementation written from the prose (E-75), with the A4–A9 ledger in `signer/src/store.rs` (E-77). ~~**Keygen (REVIEW-012c) is still ruled and not implemented.**~~ **BUILT AND EXECUTED 2026-08-20** — `signer/src/keygen.rs` and `signer/src/onboarding.rs`, reproducing both published vectors from an implementation written against the ruling's prose, with a Devnet account holding a confidential position under a derived key (E-118). **The randomness backlog is now empty except for issue #8.** ~~⚠ **`tools/` still calls `mpt_generate_blinding_factor`** — the harnesses were not migrated, and `r`/`ρ` is implemented *in the signer*, not everywhere (E-76).~~ **CLOSED 2026-08-24 (E-132).** Eight transaction-building harnesses derive through `tools/wconf-randomness.ts`, which reproduces the same nine published vectors **before it will derive anything**, and a Send built the migrated way was accepted on Devnet. ⚠ **FIVE call sites reach upstream's generator deliberately**, each with its reason at its own call site, and **`tools/check-blinding-sources.ts` fails if that list is ever wrong**. ⚠ **The count has been wrong twice in two days, in both directions** — **four** from a name-based sweep that missed a harness which submits to Devnet (E-137), then **nine** from counting files the gate flagged rather than call sites, two of which were the gate itself and a dead entry its own stale check was hardcoded to skip (E-141). **A sweep recorded in prose is a claim with no falsifier — and a gate that reports a number is making a claim about what the number counts.**
- **The `r`/`ρ` store amendments A1–A3 were reviewed 2026-08-18 and BOTH LEGS RULED THE SIGNER MAY NOT BE BUILT AGAINST THEM.** Six defects. They are corrected by **A4–A9**, in the same document (`REVIEW-010-RULING-r-and-rho-2026-08-18.md`) — build against those. A4–A9 are adjudicated and **not themselves reviewed**, and that is a deliberate stop under this file's own two-round rule: the next artifact is the implementation and a test that fails if it is not followed, not a third packet.

**The randomness packet, for the record, is HALF ruled.** `r` and `ρ` were ruled 2026-08-18 (REVIEW-010, adjudicated in REVIEW-010c): structured fresh CSPRNG entropy, with `sk_A` and every long-lived secret forbidden from the preimage. **Ruled, implemented in the signer 2026-08-18, and implemented everywhere 2026-08-24** — ~~but `mpt_generate_blinding_factor` is still what the `tools/` harnesses call.~~ **The Bulletproof half is open** and is issue #8, where the blocking question is not which construction but whether determinism is acceptable at all when the scalars provide the zero-knowledge property. Do not treat these as one item.

**The architecture document** (`docs/architecture/ARCHITECTURE-2026-08-18.md`, **revision 6**) is explicitly provisional: it records what the rulings so far oblige and marks what is still open. ~~It does **not** license building application logic — that still waits on ADR-0001 reaching `Accepted`.~~ **ADR-0001 is now `Accepted`, so §6 step 7 is open.** §5 of that document is the authority on which gates remain shut — ~~`r`/`ρ` (A4–A9)~~ **built**, ~~onboarding that branches on ledger state (#11)~~ **built and executed 2026-08-20**, and **publication of the keygen derivation (#10), which is open and which no code discharges.**

**Status 2026-08-19 — §6 step 2 is built and executed.** The signer reads its own sequence number, confidential balance, `ConfidentialBalanceVersion`, registration state, the issuer and auditor keys and the destination's `HolderEncryptionKey` from a node, at one pinned validated index, and **re-reads before signing**. Nine fields left the holder profile, which now carries secrets and policy only and **refuses any of the nine by name**. Executed on Devnet (E-101).

**Two things that milestone established which a session will otherwise get wrong:**

- **A wrong ledger read is a CONFIDENTIALITY break, not a rejected transaction.** `tecBAD_PROOF` is a `tec`, and a `tec` is *applied to the ledger* — so a substituted destination key makes the signer publish the amount encrypted under the substituter's key, permanently. The signer does not submit, so it cannot set the `tapFailHard` that would prevent it. `CLAIMS.md` **F-23**, read at source, **not executed**. This is why TLS is mandatory for non-loopback endpoints and why B-15 exists.
- **`amount_search_to` now bounds the BALANCE as well as the confirmation.** The balance is decrypted from the ledger, so a balance above the bound is a balance the signer refuses to check an amount against. One earlier test's scenario became unreachable through `prepare` as a result and was moved rather than deleted (E-104).

**Step 2 was built before step 6 deliberately, and it was the right order.** Issue #11 requires detecting `lsfDisableMaster`, a regular key or a signer list — that *is* a ledger read. `AccountSecurity` reads all three today; **nothing branches on them yet**, and saying otherwise claims work that does not exist.

Remaining gaps are listed in `docs/task-zero/TASK-ZERO-2026-08-13.md` §0 and in the open GitHub issues.

Start with `GETTING-STARTED-CLAUDE-CODE.md`. The first three sessions are gap closure, not building.

## Stack

Bun, TypeScript strict, TanStack Start, Elysia, Tailwind + shadcn/ui, monorepo.

⚠ **NONE OF THAT EXISTS YET, and this line has always read as though it did.** There is no `apps/`, no `web/`, no React and no Elysia in `package.json`. What exists is `signer/` (Rust), `facility/` (TypeScript, no framework) and `tools/`. ⚠⚠ **AND THIS SAID *"It is architecture §6 step 8 and step 8 was written on 2026-08-26"*, WHICH WAS FALSE — CORRECTED 2026-08-28 BY GREPPING THE DOCUMENT IT NAMED.** `ARCHITECTURE-2026-08-18.md` §6 has **SEVEN** numbered steps, §7 follows step 7 directly, `grep -n "step 8"` over it returns **nothing**, and revision 8 is recorded as *"§6 step 7 only"*. ⚠ **So the web tier had no scope, no falsifier and no gate in the authority document, and this line told every new session otherwise.** The sentence was right that the web tier *"was never built rather than being behind schedule"* and wrong about why: **there was no step, not a step nobody had reached.** ⚠ **A SCOPE NOTE NEEDS A RE-CHECK DATE EXACTLY AS MUCH AS AN AMENDMENT CLAIM DOES** — this file's own lesson about the `xrpl.js` blocker paragraph, one section up, landing one section down. `CLAIMS.md` **E-204**.

⚠ **CORRECTED TWICE MORE ON 2026-08-28, BOTH TIMES BY THE COMMIT THAT FALSIFIED THE PREVIOUS SENTENCE — WHICH IS THE POINT.** **(1) §6 step 8 now EXISTS**: `ARCHITECTURE-2026-08-18.md` was **revision 9** then, and is revision 12 since 2026-10-20, when step 9's first review was recorded; step 8 carries its scope, the four ruled decisions, what it may touch, what it may never touch, its gate and its *Falsified if:* clause, and `ADR-0003` is the decision record. **(2) THE STACK LINE ABOVE IS NOW TRUE, AND THE DATE IT BECAME TRUE IS 2026-08-28.** `web/api/` is Elysia; `web/app/` is TanStack Start with React 19, Vite and Tailwind 4; `bun test web/api` was **89 tests** on 2026-08-28 and is **129** on 2026-09-09 — ⚠ this read as a live figure and was stale by 40; `make check-web-readonly` is the seventh gate. ⚠ **The two paragraphs above are kept rather than deleted because they are the evidence for E-204** — *"the web tier has no scope"* was true when written and false two hours later, and *"there is no `web/`, no React, no Elysia"* was true at the start of this session and false by the end of it. **This is the project's own lesson landing twice on the note that records the lesson, in one day.**

⚠ **WHAT IS BUILT IS READ-ONLY.** ⚠⚠ ~~*"and that is the whole reason it needed no rotation"*~~ **STRUCK 2026-09-02 — the rotation ran (`REVIEW-036`) and ruled that reasoning unsound** (E-230). Read-only bought the **signing** property and not the disclosure one. The browser talks HTTP to a local Elysia process; that process reads a rippled node and sends the signer **`Request::Status` and nothing else**. **Signing stays in `wconf-signer`.** ⚠ **`Pair` and `Prepare` are the requests that carry authority, and `tools/check-web-readonly.ts` asserts that `web/api` constructs neither** — ⚠ **that scan covers `web/api`, not the whole tier**; `web/app` is covered by a separate assertion that nothing under it reaches the socket at all. This said *"anywhere under `web/`"* until 2026-09-02, and it was the THIRD site of one phrase (E-168), which was **broken seven ways against the real tree** before it was believed. ⚠⚠ **`ADR-0003` HAS NOW HAD ITS ROTATION — `REVIEW-036`, 2026-09-02, four legs with differed packets — AND ALL FOUR RULED THE CENTRAL CLAIM UNSOUND AS STATED (`CLAIMS.md` E-230).** ~~*"a read-only tier moves no trust boundary"*~~ **RETRACTED.** Both premises are false against source: **"reads public state"** — `Store::len()` counts *"a cancelled confirmation, a failed proof, an aborted attempt"* (`signer/src/store.rs:753-756`), which public ledger history cannot reveal; **"holds no credential"** is true and is **not** the same property as *conveys nothing a credential produces*. ⚠⚠ **The boundary that moved is a DISCLOSURE boundary: the tier re-serves, unauthenticated and to any local uid, the product of two resources the project protects with `0700`/`0600` mode bits — the signer's socket and the decrypted-balance snapshot** (E-228). ⚠ **What survives, and it is what the milestone needed: the tier adds no SIGNING authority**, and every leg affirmed it. **The ADR proved that and wrote it as a universal about authority.** ~~⚠ **`ADR-0003` is revision 2, still `Proposed`, on ONE item: the issuer route is a RULING handed to the project owner with four options priced — a coding session may not choose.**~~ **Ruled 2026-09-05, option 2: the disclosure is removed and `ADR-0003` is `Accepted` at revision 3 (E-241).** ⚠ This sentence stood unstruck until 2026-10-18. Two gate defects were found and fixed: **a falsifier condition named in architecture §6 step 8 (*"the API binds to anything but loopback"*) had NO falsifier** (E-227), and **two socket detectors disagreed with the narrower wired to the whole-tier count** — the pre-fix gate **measured** exiting 0 with a real second UDS client (E-226).

**The holder signer is a separate Rust binary, not part of the web tree.** ADR-0001 puts proof generation and transaction signing in a daemon on the holder's machine; a browser page cannot invoke a local binary, so the two live side by side. Do not try to fold the signer into the TanStack/Elysia application.

⚠ **CORRECTED 2026-08-26. This said the two *"talk over `127.0.0.1` with an origin allowlist and a session token"*, and that is the design REVIEW-003b KILLED.** Architecture §3 rule 3: *"Persist no bearer secret in the browser. ADR-0001 rev 2's pasted-token design is **withdrawn**."* An origin allowlist constrains **pages**, not **processes**, any local process can forge an `Origin` header, and the issue #1 ruling puts a hostile same-host process in scope — so a value-shaped interface plus a leaked token is a **spend oracle**.

**What the signer actually speaks is a Unix domain socket** (`signer/src/ipc.rs`), chosen because architecture §3 item 4 prefers OS-mediated IPC. Grants are scoped, short-lived and **bound to the connection**; closing the connection revokes them.

⚠ **The consequence for the web tier, and it is the first thing architecture §6 step 8 has to answer: a browser page CANNOT REACH THE SIGNER AT ALL.** `ipc.rs`: *"A browser page cannot connect at all. No web API opens a Unix socket."* That is the point — it closes the XSS-to-spend-oracle path by construction rather than by a header check. **Any step-8 design that gets a browser signing confidential transactions is a trust-boundary change and needs a full adversarial rotation before implementation, not after.**

~~**Known blocker, verified 2026-08-12:** `xrpl.js@5.0.0` and `ripple-binary-codec@2.8.0` contain no Confidential MPT transaction types or fields. You cannot serialise or sign these transactions with the published JS stack. Two pieces of work precede any application logic:~~

~~1. Patch or generate `definitions.json` with the types and field codes in TASK ZERO §3. Self-verifying against a node's `server_definitions` RPC.~~
~~2. Bind to `mpt-crypto` for proof generation. No binding, no WASM target and no published package exists. This is unscoped work and may be the largest single item in the project.~~

**STRUCK 2026-08-26 — BOTH ITEMS ARE CLOSED, and this paragraph told every new session that two pieces of work preceded any application logic when neither did.** It was already stale when the facility was built against the very packages it says do not exist.

- **Item 1 is closed by upstream, not by us.** `ripple-binary-codec@2.9.0` (2026-08-13) ships **all five** Confidential MPT transaction types at codes 85–89 and **all 18** confidential fields, every code matching TASK ZERO §3 exactly — 18/18, zero mismatches (**E-30**). Re-confirmed in `node_modules` on 2026-08-26: `ConfidentialMPTConvert`, `…ConvertBack`, `…Send`, `…MergeInbox`, `…Clawback`, with `ZKProof`, `BlindingFactor`, `HolderEncryptionKey`, `IssuerEncryptionKey`, `ConfidentialBalanceSpending` and `ConfidentialOutstandingAmount` all present. **No patched `definitions.json` was ever needed.**
- **Item 2 is closed twice over.** `@xrplf/mpt-crypto@0.1.0` **is published** and reproduces an accepted transaction's context hash byte for byte (**E-53**); the signer additionally links a **vendored C fork** directly through `signer/build.rs`, and the pristine upstream verifier accepts the proofs it produces (**E-80**). The JS client path is three published packages with **no patching, no vendoring and no build step**.

⚠ **`xrpl.js` is the one half that has NOT been re-checked.** E-30 recorded it at 5.0.0 (2026-06-05) not yet bundling the types, and **this project does not depend on it** — `package.json` carries `ripple-binary-codec`, `ripple-keypairs`, `ripple-address-codec` and `@xrplf/mpt-crypto` directly. If a future session needs `xrpl.js`, **check it rather than inheriting this sentence**; a named gap is a correct output.

⚠ **What this staleness cost, recorded because it is the project's own pattern:** the paragraph sat in the file every session reads *first*, asserting a blocker, while `facility/` was built and executed on Devnet against the packages it says are unusable. **A blocker note needs a re-check date exactly as much as an amendment claim does** (P-3's habit, applied one level out).

## Keys

~~Never write ElGamal keygen code before the Cryptography Reviewer has ruled on ADR-0001 §(e).~~ **Ruled 2026-08-18, implemented and executed 2026-08-20.** The construction lives in `docs/reviews/REVIEW-012-RULING-keygen-2026-08-18.md` and **only** there — do not paraphrase it into the ADR or the architecture document. `signer/src/keygen.rs` is an *implementation* of that prose and says so; where the two disagree, the ruling wins. Derivation from the XRPL master seed is upheld, with five conditions.

**Three things about that ruling that a coding session will otherwise get wrong:**

- **Its reduction is TOTAL and has no abort path**, unlike the sigma-nonce and `r`/`ρ` rulings, which both say *abort on zero, never retry*. That is correct there, where the input is fresh entropy and an abort costs one transaction. Keygen's input is a **fixed seed**, so an abort would be permanent — that holder could never participate. Do not "fix" it back.
- **Condition 2 is not cosmetic.** Onboarding must disclose that the master seed becomes a permanent decryption credential, and the signer must **warn** when the account carries `lsfDisableMaster`, a regular key or a signer list (E-69). For those holders the master seed is not spending authority, and §(e)'s argument for derivation does not hold.
- **The registration PoK must always be passed a non-NULL `context_id`.** The library accepts NULL and then emits an account-unbound, replayable proof (F-19). rippled cannot reach that path; our signer can.

~~When keygen is written, validate it against~~ **Done 2026-08-20.** mpt-crypto's own tests — `test_pok_sk`, `test_elgamal`, `test_elgamal_verify` — are C programs over **random** keys, so `signer/tests/keygen_interop.rs` drives their *properties* with a derived key. **Necessary, not sufficient:** a constant `sk_A = 1` passes all three, and **that claim is now itself a test** — `a_constant_sk_of_one_passes_every_upstream_check` runs it and asserts they pass, which is what makes the KAT load-bearing rather than decorative. The derivation's own KAT is `tools/spike-keygen-2026-08-18/t-keygen-construction.ts` and `signer/tests/keygen_kat.rs`.

## Testing

Every capability claim needs a test that fails if the claim is false.

For confidentiality specifically, **a demo of the happy path is not evidence of confidentiality.** Two harnesses are required:

- **Observer harness** — shows exactly what a full-ledger observer sees. Its first test is finding F-1 in `CLAIMS.md`: recompute the canonical `EncZero` independently and check whether freshly-initialised and post-merge balances are publicly identifiable as zero.
- **Issuer-view tool** — a single CLI command (`tools/issuer-decrypt-balance.ts`) that reads one account's `sfIssuerEncryptedBalance` and decrypts it given the issuer key. Not a standalone application. Showing what the issuer sees is the honest counterpart to showing what the public sees.

No count is carried now — the search is self-referential and every mention of it adds one (E-236). Re-run `git grep -c tapFailHard | sort -t: -k2 -rn`. **The translation for publication is `docs/VOCABULARY.md`.** The real names are:
