1:# CLAUDE.md — W-CONF
3:Working instructions for coding sessions in this repository. This is the code-relevant subset of the project instructions. The publishing protocol is deliberately not here; it never applies inside a coding session.
5:## Hard rules
7:- **Devnet only.** No mainnet writes, at any phase, for any reason. ⚠ ~~The target network is the Confidential Devnet, not the general devnet.~~ **STRUCK 2026-09-01 — falsified by this project's own grade-A row on 2026-08-16 and carried in the HARD RULES for two weeks.** **No dedicated Confidential Devnet is needed or used, and whether one exists elsewhere was never searched** — the prerelease-documented one had a dead faucet, the canonical xrpl.org page names no dedicated network, and all three required amendments are enabled on **standard Devnet**, where everything this project has executed ran. ⚠ **`CLAIMS.md` E-26's own sentence says *"there is no dedicated Confidential Devnet"*, which is a corpus-wide negative its evidence cell does not support** — `amendments-2026-08-16.md:57` records the existence search as never run (P-12), `wss://s.devnet.rippletest.net:51233`, network_id 2. Everything this project has ever executed ran there. ⚠ **Found by a fresh reader of the README who could not open this file** and flagged the contradiction from the README's text alone (E-222). **The rule that survives is the first sentence**, and the target is standard Devnet.
9:- **Never claim more security than is delivered.** The standing prohibitions live in `CLAIMS.md`. Read them before writing a README, a comment, a commit message or a log line.
12:## Review gates — scale to irreversibility
14:- Architecture, trust boundaries, cryptographic design, anything to be published: full adversarial rotation before implementation.
23:Record rejected reviewer claims with reasons so later sessions do not relitigate settled decisions.
25:## Preconditions before a build milestone
29:**Status 2026-08-18, revised late the same day.** GAP-1 and GAP-3 are closed. ADR-0001 §(a)'s threat-model question is **ruled** (issue #1: a hostile same-host process is in scope), which answers REVIEW-003b's MEDIUM and settles §(e)'s nonce half — implemented, reviewed twice, and executed on Devnet. The FFI blocker is closed (issue #2, path A, merged). **§(e)'s keygen half is now ruled too** (REVIEW-012, adjudicated REVIEW-012c), and the three long-missing primary documents are resolved — rippled 3.3.0 and the XLS-0096 text obtained, the Trail of Bits May 2026 revision recorded as an unobtainable gap (B-13).
31:**Status 2026-08-27 — B-16 is RULED, BUILT, EXECUTED and REVIEWED TWICE. `REVIEW-024` ruled it; `REVIEW-025` found eleven things wrong with the build, none of them the construction; `REVIEW-026` found that `REVIEW-025c` had OVERSTATED ITS OWN FIXES (E-168). All four of §7's conditions are met.** `REVIEW-024`, three independent legs (Gemini 9.0, Codex 8, self leg 7.5), **all ruling Option B**: the issue #1 ruling extends to the ed25519 signing nonce and the residual is **not** accepted. The RFC 8032 nonce prefix is hedged — 32 fresh CSPRNG bytes, tagged-hashed under a fixed DST and **XORed** over it, the same idiom site 1 uses — through `ed25519_dalek::hazmat::raw_sign` with the scalar, the public key, the challenge transcript and the signature equation untouched. **B-14 is now closed on both halves.**
35:**Status 2026-08-27, later still — B-22 is RULED, BUILT and REVIEWED TWICE, and round two found the build had NOT implemented its own ruling.** The key-derivation path wipes: `sha512half_zeroizing`, the ed25519 private key, both derivation closures, `derive_scalar`'s every rejected candidate, `root`/`intermediate`/`sum`, both heap seed decoders and their internals, and two `Copy`-source defects in `keygen.rs`. **268 tests at the time, three green runs, all six gates PASS. `make check-zeroize-bounds` is the falsifier — ~~now FIVE parts~~ five parts AT B-22; it is SIX as of B-23** (**274 tests as of B-24**), the fifth still pinning **thirteen named sites** — ⚠ **one was RETIRED by B-24, not lost: `Zeroizing(entropy)`'s `Copy`-source guard is gone because the producer now hands out a wiping type, so there is no bare source to guard** — with three controls that each run through the real detector. ⚠ **Corrected by the commit that falsified it** — a count in a status block is a claim about the CURRENT tree, and B-23 is the next commit after this sentence was written. `REVIEW-029` (the decision) and `REVIEW-030` (round two, the fixes, **two external legs** — which is what B-21's round two lacked).
63:- ⚠⚠ **A COMMENT NAMED THE WRONG CRATE AND THAT IS WHY A SECRET WENT UNWIPED.** The `k256` in `finish()`'s comment belongs to `compressed_public_key`, eight lines down; **the neighbouring function's dependency was read into this one's comment**, and it then answered *"why is this not wiped?"* for every later reader — including **two passes by the self leg**. ⚠ **The instrument that catches this is not a rule, it is one `grep -n Zeroize` on the file the comment names.** E-181 was closed by exactly that probe on `k256::Scalar` and the probe was not re-run one file over.
81:⚠ **B-23's residuals are NAMED, not closed:** `serde_json`'s escape-decoding scratch, `read_to_string`'s growth if the file changes mid-read, and a pasted line over the 4096-byte reservation. **P-5 governs all of it — this narrows a window and removes no channel.** ⚠ **Both `REVIEW-029` legs rejected scoping any of this by profile regime** — *"already on disk"* is a **disclosed** residual, not a reason to keep an **avoidable** memory one.
86:- ⚠⚠ **A COUNT IN A FIX REPORT IS A CLAIM WITH NO FALSIFIER, AND THIS SESSION GOT ONE WRONG FOUR TIMES RUNNING (E-172, E-175).** *"Fixed at all four live sites"* → six → nine, each found by the next sweep, each correction asserting a new number. This file already carried three clauses of advice about it — *tick the leg's list* (E-141), *a leg's list is line numbers, a corrected sentence is a string* (E-168), and this session's own *grep the diff you just wrote for the claim you just falsified* (E-172). **All three are advice, and advice is what kept failing.** ⚠ **`check-zeroize-bounds` part 4 is what ended it**: it scans every live file for the retracted phrases, fails on any lacking a correction marker, exempts `docs/reviews/` and carries a control that must detect an injected one. **E-137 and E-141 were counts corrected by counts and both recurred.** ⚠ **The corollary lands on this file's own rule: *a gate that reports a number is claiming what the number counts* applies to ADJUDICATIONS too.**
94:- ⚠ **TWO FALSE-PASS GATES, AND ROUND TWO FOUND A THIRD MODE IN THE SAME TWO ARTIFACTS (E-165, E-168).** The `revert-sweep` had **no baseline**, so it certified a mutation whenever an expected test was in the failure set — *including when that test was already failing for an unrelated reason*. It now proves the unmodified target green first and requires a **newly** failing expected test. ⚠ **A gate that reports a number is claiming what the number counts**, and both of this session's gates got that wrong before they got it right.
110:- ⚠ **Option A's real cost is a full Devnet sync, and there is NO rehearsal network.** `signer/src/ledger.rs` hardcodes `DEVNET_NETWORK_ID = 2` and *"refuses every network but Devnet, at any phase, for any reason"*, so a local standalone ledger would have to claim `network_id` 2 to drive the signer — a local ledger presenting itself as Devnet, which is the substitution this project forbids. That was not in the decision request's cost table and it is now in architecture §6 step 7.
119:- **Our own facility walkthrough had a false disclosure sentence, and `claims-check` had already passed it.** *"Public: the aggregate change"* — but `MPTAmount` is a plaintext field on **every individual** ConvertBack, so retiring a facility in tranches publishes **every tranche size** (F-24). Two ConvertBacks read 95 and 191 straight off their validated transactions.
120:- **`tesSUCCESS` was not accepted as the evidence, and that is the standard now.** All four of XLS-0096 §10.5's state changes were checked against ledger state before and after — public balance, `ConfidentialOutstandingAmount`, version, and the **decrypted** remainder. A `tesSUCCESS` alone does not distinguish a correct homomorphic subtraction from a lucky one.
122:**Status 2026-08-20 — architecture §6 step 6 is BUILT AND EXECUTED.** Real key generation and onboarding. **Issue #11 is discharged**: `lsfDisableMaster`, a regular key and a signer list each — on their own — flip the recommended key source to `standalone` and make the ruling's condition-2 warning mandatory, and the disclosures reach the holder **before the seed is asked for**. ⚠ **Issue #10 is NOT discharged and no code discharges it**: the derivation is specified in one repository document and is not a published standard, so a holder who has their seed and not this repository cannot reconstruct their key — which is the entire benefit the ruling trades a permanent decryption credential for. **Publishing an XLS informational draft is a Desktop-project decision, not a coding-session one.**
127:- **A registered key is not "unrecoverable", and saying so is a defect.** The protocol has no rotation and no recovery (P-10) — that is about **XLS-96**. A *derived* key is reconstructible from the master seed under the W-CONF derivation, which is the only thing deriving buys, and the confirmation said the opposite for one commit (E-125). ⚠ These are two sentences about two layers and merging them is how the banner also came to claim XLS-0096 governs XRPL account keys (E-126).
128:- **`from_parts` accepted a public key that was not `sk·G`.** Now `try_from_parts`, which recomputes it. The failure it allowed is the worst one this protocol offers: register a key nobody holds the secret for, encrypt every balance to it, and have every step succeed (E-120).
134:- **The build-cost table was INVERTED, not merely stale.** Option 2 (browser WASM) is now the **cheapest** option — three published npm packages, no patching, no vendoring, no build step (E-53) — and Option 3 is the **most expensive**: a vendored C fork, an FFI binding, a Rust binary, OS-mediated IPC, a separate confirmation UI. **Option 3 was chosen anyway, on risk.** Do not repeat "Option 2 is expensive"; it is not.
145:- **The backlog is now ONE ruling, not three.** Sigma nonces: implemented and executed. **`r`/`ρ`: implemented 2026-08-18** in `signer/src/randomness.rs`, reproducing all nine published vectors from a second implementation written from the prose (E-75), with the A4–A9 ledger in `signer/src/store.rs` (E-77). ~~**Keygen (REVIEW-012c) is still ruled and not implemented.**~~ **BUILT AND EXECUTED 2026-08-20** — `signer/src/keygen.rs` and `signer/src/onboarding.rs`, reproducing both published vectors from an implementation written against the ruling's prose, with a Devnet account holding a confidential position under a derived key (E-118). **The randomness backlog is now empty except for issue #8.** ~~⚠ **`tools/` still calls `mpt_generate_blinding_factor`** — the harnesses were not migrated, and `r`/`ρ` is implemented *in the signer*, not everywhere (E-76).~~ **CLOSED 2026-08-24 (E-132).** Eight transaction-building harnesses derive through `tools/wconf-randomness.ts`, which reproduces the same nine published vectors **before it will derive anything**, and a Send built the migrated way was accepted on Devnet. ⚠ **FIVE call sites reach upstream's generator deliberately**, each with its reason at its own call site, and **`tools/check-blinding-sources.ts` fails if that list is ever wrong**. ⚠ **The count has been wrong twice in two days, in both directions** — **four** from a name-based sweep that missed a harness which submits to Devnet (E-137), then **nine** from counting files the gate flagged rather than call sites, two of which were the gate itself and a dead entry its own stale check was hardcoded to skip (E-141). **A sweep recorded in prose is a claim with no falsifier — and a gate that reports a number is making a claim about what the number counts.**
150:**The architecture document** (`docs/architecture/ARCHITECTURE-2026-08-18.md`, **revision 6**) is explicitly provisional: it records what the rulings so far oblige and marks what is still open. ~~It does **not** license building application logic — that still waits on ADR-0001 reaching `Accepted`.~~ **ADR-0001 is now `Accepted`, so §6 step 7 is open.** §5 of that document is the authority on which gates remain shut — ~~`r`/`ρ` (A4–A9)~~ **built**, ~~onboarding that branches on ledger state (#11)~~ **built and executed 2026-08-20**, and **publication of the keygen derivation (#10), which is open and which no code discharges.**
156:- **A wrong ledger read is a CONFIDENTIALITY break, not a rejected transaction.** `tecBAD_PROOF` is a `tec`, and a `tec` is *applied to the ledger* — so a substituted destination key makes the signer publish the amount encrypted under the substituter's key, permanently. The signer does not submit, so it cannot set the `tapFailHard` that would prevent it. `CLAIMS.md` **F-23**, read at source, **not executed**. This is why TLS is mandatory for non-loopback endpoints and why B-15 exists.
159:**Step 2 was built before step 6 deliberately, and it was the right order.** Issue #11 requires detecting `lsfDisableMaster`, a regular key or a signer list — that *is* a ledger read. `AccountSecurity` reads all three today; **nothing branches on them yet**, and saying otherwise claims work that does not exist.
165:## Stack
173:⚠ **WHAT IS BUILT IS READ-ONLY.** ⚠⚠ ~~*"and that is the whole reason it needed no rotation"*~~ **STRUCK 2026-09-02 — the rotation ran (`REVIEW-036`) and ruled that reasoning unsound** (E-230). Read-only bought the **signing** property and not the disclosure one. The browser talks HTTP to a local Elysia process; that process reads a rippled node and sends the signer **`Request::Status` and nothing else**. **Signing stays in `wconf-signer`.** ⚠ **`Pair` and `Prepare` are the requests that carry authority, and `tools/check-web-readonly.ts` asserts that `web/api` constructs neither** — ⚠ **that scan covers `web/api`, not the whole tier**; `web/app` is covered by a separate assertion that nothing under it reaches the socket at all. This said *"anywhere under `web/`"* until 2026-09-02, and it was the THIRD site of one phrase (E-168), which was **broken seven ways against the real tree** before it was believed. ⚠⚠ **`ADR-0003` HAS NOW HAD ITS ROTATION — `REVIEW-036`, 2026-09-02, four legs with differed packets — AND ALL FOUR RULED THE CENTRAL CLAIM UNSOUND AS STATED (`CLAIMS.md` E-230).** ~~*"a read-only tier moves no trust boundary"*~~ **RETRACTED.** Both premises are false against source: **"reads public state"** — `Store::len()` counts *"a cancelled confirmation, a failed proof, an aborted attempt"* (`signer/src/store.rs:753-756`), which public ledger history cannot reveal; **"holds no credential"** is true and is **not** the same property as *conveys nothing a credential produces*. ⚠⚠ **The boundary that moved is a DISCLOSURE boundary: the tier re-serves, unauthenticated and to any local uid, the product of two resources the project protects with `0700`/`0600` mode bits — the signer's socket and the decrypted-balance snapshot** (E-228). ⚠ **What survives, and it is what the milestone needed: the tier adds no SIGNING authority**, and every leg affirmed it. **The ADR proved that and wrote it as a universal about authority.** ~~⚠ **`ADR-0003` is revision 2, still `Proposed`, on ONE item: the issuer route is a RULING handed to the project owner with four options priced — a coding session may not choose.**~~ **Ruled 2026-09-05, option 2: the disclosure is removed and `ADR-0003` is `Accepted` at revision 3 (E-241).** ⚠ This sentence stood unstruck until 2026-10-18. Two gate defects were found and fixed: **a falsifier condition named in architecture §6 step 8 (*"the API binds to anything but loopback"*) had NO falsifier** (E-227), and **two socket detectors disagreed with the narrower wired to the whole-tier count** — the pre-fix gate **measured** exiting 0 with a real second UDS client (E-226).
183:~~**Known blocker, verified 2026-08-12:** `xrpl.js@5.0.0` and `ripple-binary-codec@2.8.0` contain no Confidential MPT transaction types or fields. You cannot serialise or sign these transactions with the published JS stack. Two pieces of work precede any application logic:~~
186:~~2. Bind to `mpt-crypto` for proof generation. No binding, no WASM target and no published package exists. This is unscoped work and may be the largest single item in the project.~~
191:- **Item 2 is closed twice over.** `@xrplf/mpt-crypto@0.1.0` **is published** and reproduces an accepted transaction's context hash byte for byte (**E-53**); the signer additionally links a **vendored C fork** directly through `signer/build.rs`, and the pristine upstream verifier accepts the proofs it produces (**E-80**). The JS client path is three published packages with **no patching, no vendoring and no build step**.
195:⚠ **What this staleness cost, recorded because it is the project's own pattern:** the paragraph sat in the file every session reads *first*, asserting a blocker, while `facility/` was built and executed on Devnet against the packages it says are unusable. **A blocker note needs a re-check date exactly as much as an amendment claim does** (P-3's habit, applied one level out).
197:## Keys
207:~~When keygen is written, validate it against~~ **Done 2026-08-20.** mpt-crypto's own tests — `test_pok_sk`, `test_elgamal`, `test_elgamal_verify` — are C programs over **random** keys, so `signer/tests/keygen_interop.rs` drives their *properties* with a derived key. **Necessary, not sufficient:** a constant `sk_A = 1` passes all three, and **that claim is now itself a test** — `a_constant_sk_of_one_passes_every_upstream_check` runs it and asserts they pass, which is what makes the KAT load-bearing rather than decorative. The derivation's own KAT is `tools/spike-keygen-2026-08-18/t-keygen-construction.ts` and `signer/tests/keygen_kat.rs`.
209:## Testing
211:Every capability claim needs a test that fails if the claim is false.
215:- **Observer harness** — shows exactly what a full-ledger observer sees. Its first test is finding F-1 in `CLAIMS.md`: recompute the canonical `EncZero` independently and check whether freshly-initialised and post-merge balances are publicly identifiable as zero.
216:- **Issuer-view tool** — a single CLI command (`tools/issuer-decrypt-balance.ts`) that reads one account's `sfIssuerEncryptedBalance` and decrypts it given the issuer key. Not a standalone application. Showing what the issuer sees is the honest counterpart to showing what the public sees.
218:## ⚠ `tapFailHard` is this project's shorthand and is NOT a real name
220:**The string `tapFailHard` appears ZERO times in rippled.** It is used throughout this repository, including this file. ⚠ **This said *"~130 times across 35 files"* until 2026-09-04; it was **178 across 51** at `41b0255`. No count is carried now — the search is self-referential and every mention of it adds one (E-236). Re-run `git grep -c tapFailHard | sort -t: -k2 -rn`. **The translation for publication is `docs/VOCABULARY.md`.** The real names are:
225:**The code is correct and was never wrong** — `facility/src/submit.ts:90` sends `fail_hard: true`. The problem is the prose, which reads exactly like an API name and **escaped into a public upstream filing on 2026-08-26** (`XRPL-Standards#609`, corrected the same day, E-156). Nothing gates project vocabulary at the repository boundary, and a reader grepping rippled for `tapFailHard` finds nothing.
231:## Capturing upstream feedback
241:⚠ **FILING is governed by `docs/UPSTREAM-FILING-PROTOCOL.md`, ruled 2026-09-01, and `make check-filing` enforces the mechanical half.** Five rules: an adversarial review runs **BEFORE** filing and gates it; a prose word cap **the gate prints** with one concrete ask; **no positives-only filings to a bug tracker**; no negative claim without its recorded search; plain English and short sentences. ⚠ **The 2026-09-01 audit found every claim in the six open filings still standing at upstream HEAD — so the research was sound and the ORDER OF OPERATIONS was the defect.** A review that runs after submission cannot prevent anything; it can only produce a public correction.

     1	/**
     2	 * The hosted demo's diagrams, at the owner's request of 2026-10-22.
     3	 *
     4	 * ⚠ **Markup, not images.** The policy's `style-src 'self'` refuses inline
     5	 * styles, and a diagram drawn in markup reflows on a phone, follows dark mode,
     6	 * and is text the gate reads like any other sentence. So every label is a
     7	 * claim: each restates a sentence already on the page it sits on, or a row the
     8	 * page cites. The words *decrypt* and *plaintext* are not used, for check 8b's

    14	import type { ReactNode } from "react";
    15	
    16	type Tone = "public" | "hidden" | "issuer" | "plain";
    17

    20	  hidden: "border-emerald-500 bg-emerald-50 text-emerald-950 dark:border-emerald-600 dark:bg-emerald-950/40 dark:text-emerald-100",
    21	  issuer: "border-violet-400 bg-violet-50 text-violet-950 dark:border-violet-500 dark:bg-violet-950/40 dark:text-violet-100",

    27	  hidden: "hidden from the public",
    28	  issuer: "the issuer can read it",
    29	};
    30	
    31	function Figure({ caption, children }: { caption: string; children: ReactNode }) {
    32	  return (
    33	    <figure className="my-4">
    34	      {children}
    35	      <figcaption className="text-xs opacity-70 mt-2">{caption}</figcaption>
    36	    </figure>
    37	  );
    38	}

    40	/** A box. `sub` is a short description; `field` is a ledger field name, set in code. */
    41	function Node({ tone = "plain", title, sub, field, children, dashed = false }: {
    42	  tone?: Tone; title: string; sub?: string; field?: string; children?: ReactNode; dashed?: boolean;
    43	}) {
    44	  return (
    45	    <div className={`rounded-md border ${dashed ? "border-dashed" : ""} ${TONE[tone]} p-2 text-xs leading-snug`}>
    46	      <div className="font-semibold">{title}</div>
    47	      {field && <code className="block text-[10px] opacity-70 break-all mt-0.5">{field}</code>}
    48	      {sub && <div className="text-[11px] opacity-80 mt-0.5">{sub}</div>}
    49	      {children && <div className="mt-1">{children}</div>}
    50	    </div>
    51	  );
    52	}

    54	/** An arrow with its label: across on a wide screen, down on a phone. */
    55	function Edge({ label, tone, always }: { label: string; tone?: Tone; always?: "down" }) {
    56	  const arrow = always === "down"
    57	    ? <span aria-hidden="true">↓</span>
    58	    : <><span aria-hidden="true" className="hidden sm:inline">→</span><span aria-hidden="true" className="sm:hidden">↓</span></>;
    59	  return (
    60	    <div className="flex sm:flex-col items-center justify-center gap-1 text-center text-[11px] leading-tight px-1">
    61	      <span className="text-base opacity-70">{arrow}</span>
    62	      <span className={tone && tone !== "plain" ? `rounded border px-1 ${TONE[tone]}` : "opacity-80"}>{label}</span>
    63	    </div>
    64	  );
    65	}
    66	
    67	function Down({ label, tone }: { label: string; tone?: Tone }) {
    68	  return <Edge label={label} tone={tone} always="down" />;
    69	}

    71	export function Legend({ tones = ["public", "hidden", "issuer"] }: { tones?: Exclude<Tone, "plain">[] }) {
    72	  return (
    73	    <div className="flex flex-wrap gap-2 text-[11px] mt-1" aria-label="Key to the colours">
    74	      {tones.map((t) => (
    75	        <span key={t} className={`rounded border px-1.5 py-0.5 ${TONE[t]}`}>{TONE_WORD[t]}</span>
    76	      ))}
    77	    </div>
    78	  );
    79	}

## Emphasis

`docs/EMPHASIS-DISCIPLINE.md` is the rule and `make check-emphasis` is the gate,
built 2026-09-09. `make break-emphasis-gate` is the mutations that verify it and
`make break-emphasis-gate-self-test` verifies those.

⚠⚠ **Three adversarial rounds ran on this gate — `REVIEW-043`, `-044`, `-045` —
and the rotation is CLOSED at three, which DEPARTS from the two-round rule's
own extension clause.** That clause owes a further round whenever the last one's
stopping condition did not fire, and round three's did not. **The rule is not
amended; this artifact is an exception with a stated reason**: all three rounds
found defects in hand-written parsing of a format that has a reference
implementation, and round three deleted that layer rather than patching it a
seventh time. `REVIEW-045d` carries the three conditions that reopen it, and
`CLAIMS.md` E-264 is the ruling.

**One `⚠⚠` per row at most; `⚠` means a retraction or a correction, not
"important"; three `⚠` per row at most; a `⚠` starts a sentence; bold under 20%
of a row; no narrative paragraph over 800 characters.**

⚠ The density grew from **0 to 2.1 per KB** with no rule ever written, and the
session that measured it was writing at **4.0**. Enforced forward from a cutoff;
history is left as written, because this register keeps what it believed.

⚠ **This file is DENSER than the register the rule was argued from — 2.70 ⚠ per
KB against 2.13 — and was never sampled** (`CLAIMS.md` E-252). It has no row
ids, so it has no cutoff and gets **rule 6 only, as a ratchet** at eleven
over-length paragraphs. A twelfth fails the gate. ⚠ **This said thirteen until
2026-09-09**, when `REVIEW-045` made rule 6 answer through `cmark-gfm` instead
of a hand-written splitter and the true count turned out to be lower. Rules 1 and 3 to 5 do not
reach this file at all.

## Reporting

At the end of each milestone: what runs, what does not, what you learned that was not in the spec, and what should change in the plan as a result. The last item is the Compound step and it is not optional.

## Personas

`docs/personas/` is canonical. `.claude/agents/` is generated from it — regenerate whenever a persona changes or the two will drift.
# CLAIMS register

⚠ **`tapFailHard` in this document is THIS PROJECT'S SHORTHAND and appears zero times in rippled** — the real names are `fail_hard` (the RPC parameter) and `TapFailHard` (the internal apply flag). See [`docs/VOCABULARY.md`](docs/VOCABULARY.md). The code always used the real one.

**The register.** Every capability claim in a README, demo, post or conversation gets an entry here with the test result or primary citation that supports it. **Before publishing anything, diff the new claims against this register.**

**Human-gated checklist, not automation.** CI cannot judge whether prose overclaims. Do not add a CI job and call it solved.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Navigate** | [`docs/CLAIMS-INDEX.md`](docs/CLAIMS-INDEX.md) — one line per row, generated. ⚠ Do not hand-edit it; `make check-claims-index` fails if it drifts. |
| **Statuses** | `SUPPORTED` evidence attached · `PENDING` asserted, evidence not attached, **may not be published** · `BLOCKED` never claim · `RETRACTED` was claimed, found wrong — **keep the row** |
| **Sections** | [Standing prohibitions](#standing-prohibitions--never-claim) · [Established](#established) · [Pending](#pending--asserted-not-yet-supportable) · [Findings](#findings--verified-file-upstream-before-publishing) · [Retracted](#retracted) |
| **History** | [Revision history](#revision-history) — moved to the end 2026-09-04. ⚠ It was **5,358 characters of unbroken prose above the first heading**, so nothing in this file was visible without scrolling past it. |

---

## Standing prohibitions — never claim

| # | Claim | Why |
|---|---|---|
| P-1 | That this work makes XRPL quantum-safe or quantum-resistant | It does not |
| P-2 | That an application-layer PQ scheme protects funds at the ledger level | An adversary who breaks secp256k1 signs a valid classical transaction and the network accepts it |
| P-3 | That any amendment is live or enabled, without a dated `feature` RPC check | Amendment state is a reading, not knowledge |
| P-4 | That a Confidential MPT demo hides identities | It hides amounts and balances. Sender and receiver remain public |
| P-5 | That anything here is trustless, non-custodial, decentralised, audited, production-ready or secure | None of it is; ADR-0001 names the trusted parties |
| P-6 | That anything is compliant with regulation in a named jurisdiction | Not a compliance project |
| P-7 | That analysis of published work is original research | Cite Cenk, Malhotra, Akinyele, Chen, Xie, Qian by name where the work is theirs. **Add Tess Dore (2026-08-16):** the compact sigma protocol, the twisted-ElGamal analysis and the Bulletproofs++ analysis are Dore & Cenk, RippleX Research — Dore is *not* an XLS-0096 author and is easy to omit by copying E-1's list |
| P-8 | Any benchmark number without hardware, parameter set and methodology | Includes re-quoting XLS-0096 §14.2's "laptop-class CPU" timings — see B-1 |
| P-9 | That holders are private from the issuer | The issuer's decryption mirror is mandatory. No configuration makes this false |
| P-10 | That XLS-96 supports key rotation or key recovery | It supports neither, at any level |
| P-11 | That confidential transfers hide *who* transacts, or *when*, or *how often* | Only amounts are hidden. The counterparty graph and event timing are fully public |
| P-12 | **Any claim that a source does NOT say something — *nothing documents it*, *no such field*, *not exported*, *nobody has called it out*, *I could not find it* — without the search that would falsify it recorded alongside** | It is an assertion about a whole corpus and one search settles it. **Three graded rows failed this way on 2026-08-26** (E-155, E-156), two of them **positives**: U-15 quoted a sentence XLS-0096 has never contained; U-12 said a property was *"not called out anywhere I found"* when the spec states it **twice**, once in wording identical to the benefit claimed as an original reading. ⚠ **This shape survives review because it does not look like a claim** — a quotation has quotation marks, a benchmark visibly wants a methodology, and *"nobody has documented this"* reads as modesty. Gated by `tools/check-negative-claims.ts` |

---

## Established

| # | Claim | Evidence | Grade |
|---|---|---|---|
| E-1 | XLS-0096 authors: Cenk, Malhotra, Akinyele, Chen, Xie, Qian; status Draft, created 2026-01-15. ⚠ **Superseded upstream on 2026-09-03.** XLS-0096 PR #604, *"Update and finalize XLS-96"*, merged as `33ea4ca66` and set `status: Final`. It reads `Final` at `58ccd3047` and at `master` `47fa15357`, read on 2026-10-18 (E-322) | Spec header, at the time; the header at `9fbf7ef8e` (Draft) and at `33ea4ca66`, `58ccd3047` and `master` (Final) for the supersession | A |
| E-2 | Transaction types 85 Convert, 86 MergeInbox, 87 ConvertBack, 88 Send, 89 Clawback | `transactions.macro` @ 3.3.0, matches spec | A |
| E-3 | The Send `ZKProof` is a fixed-length bundle of a compact sigma proof plus an aggregated Bulletproof, enforced at preflight | `Protocol.h:466–471`; `ConfidentialMPTSend.cpp` length check | A |
| E-3a | Those components are 192 B and 754 B, totalling 946 B | **GAP-6 closed 2026-08-16.** `secp256k1_mpt.h:313` (192), `mpt_protocol.h:35` (754) at commit `48a3cfe` = `0.4.0-rc4`; identical at `1.0.4`. Independently **executed**: `test_bulletproof_agg` prints 754 B for m=2, 688 B for m=1. **Confirmed invariant in the participant count 2026-08-17: the `ZKProof` is 946 B at n=3 and 946 B at n=4**, as the structure implies — the sigma proof is six scalars regardless of how many recipients share the ciphertext randomness | **A** |
| E-4 | Total Send payload is **1210 B without an auditor and 1276 B with one — both now MEASURED.** The conflicting 1177 B is **wrong for the wire** | **RESOLVED 2026-08-17 by measurement.** Three `ConfidentialMPTSend` transactions serialised and accepted on standard Devnet. Each encrypted-amount field carries a **full 66-byte ElGamal pair**, not a bare 33-byte `C₂`: `SenderEncryptedAmount` 66, `DestinationEncryptedAmount` 66, `IssuerEncryptedAmount` 66, `AmountCommitment` 33, `BalanceCommitment` 33, `ZKProof` 946 = **1210 B**. The whole signed blob is **1423 B**. **Both documents were partly right and the reason matters (→ F-13):** `C₁` *is* shared, and this is **enforced by the protocol, not chosen by our harness** — a distinction REVIEW-004b HIGH-03 was right to demand and which the original evidence could not support, since it amounted to the harness observing its own input. Settled by negative control (REVIEW-004c §3): a Send built with independent per-participant randomness is refused at three layers — `mpt_get_confidential_send_proof` returns `-1` and generates nothing, `mpt_verify_send_proof` returns FAIL, and **rippled returns `tecBAD_PROOF`** where the shared-`C₁` control returns `tesSUCCESS`. The ledger nonetheless transmits `C₁` **once per participant**. The paper's 1177 is the size this would be if `C₁` were sent once, which rippled 3.3.0 does not do. The 99 B is exactly the 3 redundant 33-byte `C₁` copies at n=4. Our arithmetic was right; the paper's assumption about the wire was not. **The auditor case was measured on 2026-08-17**, closing the n=3 limitation the claims-check gate had flagged: an issuance carrying `sfAuditorEncryptionKey` was built and a four-participant Send accepted. Payload **exactly 1276 B** — `SenderEncryptedAmount` 66, `DestinationEncryptedAmount` 66, `IssuerEncryptedAmount` 66, **`AuditorEncryptedAmount` 66**, `AmountCommitment` 33, `BalanceCommitment` 33, `ZKProof` 946. Signed blob **1492 B** (n=3: 1423). The arithmetic prediction was confirmed rather than merely assumed, and **the paper's 1177 for n=4 is now refuted against a direct n=4 measurement rather than against a hypothetical** | **A on both figures, measured** |
| E-5 | **A confidential transaction costs exactly 10× the network's standard base fee.** The widely-quoted "100 drops" is that ratio against a `base_fee` of 10 and is **network-dependent, not absolute** | **Mechanism executed 2026-08-17 (REVIEW-004c §5), closing the "arithmetic not verified" gap that had stood since 2026-08-13.** Measured by ladder on Devnet where `fee` RPC reports `base_fee = 1`: `ConfidentialMPTSend` returns `telINSUF_FEE_P` at 1, 5 and 9 drops and `tesSUCCESS` at **10**, on both zero-amount and non-zero arms; a standard `AccountSet` from the same account is accepted at **1 drop**. Ratio 10:1, executed. `kConfidentialFeeMultiplier = 9` (`Protocol.h:492`); `Transactor::calculateBaseFee` still unread, but the result no longer depends on reading it. **Publish the ratio, never the absolute number** | **A on the ratio, executed; the "100 drops" figure is B and network-dependent** |

| E-23 | ~~ElGamal decryption bounded at 1,000,000; above that permanently undecryptable through the API~~ **CORRECTED 2026-08-16 — see F-7.** Decryption brute-forces the discrete log over a **caller-supplied window** `[range_low, range_high]`. 1,000,000 is a *documented default*, not a bound. | E-22 | (a) A `ConfidentialMPTSend` of zero is **valid**. (b) It is indistinguishable from a non-zero send — **survived THREE falsification checks under a controlled rerun, NOT proven** | **Tested 2026-08-17, then RE-TESTED under control the same day after REVIEW-004 (see REVIEW-004c §2).** (a) is **Grade A, executed**: zero-amount Sends return `tesSUCCESS` and validate. (b) remains unprovable by this method; what can be said is that three attempts to break it failed. ⚠ **"Four checks" was wrong and is retired: the old check 1 was validity, which IS limb (a), so the count used the validity claim as evidence for the indistinguishability claim.** Both review legs missed this; the Gemini leg found it. Limb (b) rests on three checks, not four. **[1] Randomisation**, the sharpest test: two zero Sends from the same account at the same balance differ in **all six** observables — `SenderEncryptedAmount`, `DestinationEncryptedAmount`, `IssuerEncryptedAmount`, `AmountCommitment`, `BalanceCommitment`, `ZKProof`. Re-run with zero sends **first**, since a zero send does not move the balance: **all four arms proved at balance 80**, n=2 per arm. Result: signed blob **1423 B all four**, ZKProof **946 B all four**, `AffectedNodes` **3 all four**, identical fields mutated on identical node types (`ConfidentialBalanceInbox`+`IssuerEncryptedBalance` on the destination; `ConfidentialBalanceSpending`+`ConfidentialBalanceVersion`+`IssuerEncryptedBalance` on the sender), version **+1 all four**. **Fee threshold measured on both arms**, not deduced: a full ladder gives `telINSUF_FEE_P` at 1 and 9 drops and `tesSUCCESS` at 10 for **both** zero and non-zero — identical. **Metadata is NOT constant and E-51 was wrong about it**, but the variation tracks *destination state*, not amount: at matched destination state the arms are byte-identical (1219/1219, then 1225/1225 once the destination has merged). **No asymmetry attributable to the amount was found.** Zero sends still mutate the sender's spending ciphertext, because subtracting `Enc(0, r)` changes `C₁` — so "nothing changed" is not observable either. **Never write "confirmed": four passing checks are not a proof of indistinguishability, and an observer with resources this session did not model may still separate them** | **(a) A, executed. | E-33 | **Generating a `ConfidentialMPTSend` proof requires the sender's ElGamal secret key `sk_A` as a witness.** `sk_A` appears in the witness `w = (m, r, b, ρ, sk_A)`; balance linkage is proved as `B₂ − b·G = sk_A·B₁` precisely because the historical randomness `r_b` is unavailable to the prover. 2-special soundness extracts `sk_A`; knowledge soundness holds in the ROM under DL. **ADR-0001's central premise is confirmed.** `ConvertBack` shares the relation; `Clawback` requires `sk_iss` instead | `cmpt-compact-sigma.pdf` §2.3, §2.4 Eq. 1, Thm 4.2 (Relations 5–6), Thm 4.4, Remark 4.5 — Dore & Cenk, RippleX Research, 2026-05-12. Read in full 2026-08-16 | A |
| E-34 | **Nonce reuse in a compact-form sigma proof leaks `sk_A`, and is undetectable from the proofs.** Compact form transmits `(e, z…)` not the commitments, so reused nonces yield proofs that verify normally while allowing an observer holding both to extract the witness. ⚠ **That mitigation line is FALSE and was falsified by two independent cryptography reviewers on 2026-08-17 (REVIEW-005a, REVIEW-005b, adjudicated in REVIEW-005c §1).** `statement` does not include `TransactionContextID` as the paper defines it, while the Fiat–Shamir challenge does — so an ordinary resubmission at a changed sequence or `CB_S_Version`, with cached ciphertexts, yields two proofs sharing first-round commitments under distinct challenges. That is Theorem 4.2's extraction condition exactly. **The formula as written causes the failure it is quoted to prevent.** The correct requirement is that the nonce preimage must refine the challenge preimage — every byte that can influence the challenge, other than the commitments, must appear injectively in the derivation. See F-17 | `cmpt-compact-sigma.pdf` §6.1, Remark 6.1. **Direct input to ADR-0001 §(e) and the Rust signer — ranks above TOB-RIPCTX-5, since it needs only a coding error, not an adversary with local measurement** | A |
| E-35 | Compact-form proofs **cannot be batch-verified** — commitments are reconstructed per-proof from each proof's own challenge, leaving no cross-proof linear structure. Verification is O(n) multi-scalar multiplications per proof (11 at n=4), with no amortisation across a ledger | `cmpt-compact-sigma.pdf` Remark 6.2. Constrains any throughput claim | A |
| E-36 | XLS-96 **deliberately rejected twisted ElGamal.** Under compact form its size advantage shrinks from 292 B to an irreducible **64 B** — structural, arising from three scalar witnesses vs five — judged insufficient against specification, implementation and audit costs | `cmpt-compact-sigma.pdf` §A, Tables 5–6, §A.5. The scheme choice was quantified, not incidental — relevant background for W-PQ | A |
