| E-53 | **`@xrplf/mpt-crypto@0.1.0` is published and it works.** A hex-in/hex-out TypeScript API over a vendored WASM build, zero runtime dependencies, published 2026-08-16T15:26:21Z. Installed from npm, it reproduces the context hash of the `ConfidentialMPTConvert` this session got accepted **byte for byte**, and a proof it generates verifies under an independently built native library. It exports all four proof generators, all four context hashes, ElGamal encrypt/decrypt, Pedersen commitments and homomorphic add/subtract. **It does not export keypair generation** | Executed 2026-08-17: `bun add @xrplf/mpt-crypto@0.1.0`, cross-checked against the native build and against on-chain values. **Supersedes E-32 and closes the last item in TASK ZERO §5's rescope.** The JS client path is three published packages — `@xrplf/mpt-crypto` + `ripple-binary-codec@2.9.0` + `ripple-keypairs` — with **no patching, no vendoring and no build step**. **Executed end to end, same day — for `ConfidentialMPTConvert` ONLY.** ⚠ **Scoped 2026-08-17 after REVIEW-004b MEDIUM-02.** The package exports all four proof generators, but only `Convert` was run through it; `Send`, `ConvertBack`, `Clawback` and `MergeInbox` used the native `mptool` and are **PENDING execution via npm**. A second `ConfidentialMPTConvert` whose every piece of proof material came from the npm package — context hash, Schnorr proof, blinding factor, both ciphertexts — was accepted and validated on Devnet, hash `CA37BC1B95E861F13C374F43B702F7B2C4C5A0DEDD633CB8D253A8A985C916E7`, account `rDssjNf9sYnYn1y1EinhgNS3ZQ3EfppTKZ`, MPTAmount 321. **This was added because the first version of this row rested on component agreement alone** — a matching context hash is not a transaction the ledger accepted. ElGamal keys still came from `mpt_generate_keypair`; the package does not export keygen, and deriving one from an XRPL seed is what ADR-0001 §(e) is under review for | A, executed |
| E-54 | **Upstream is building native libraries for Rust and Python consumers.** `.github/workflows/build-native-libs.yml` exists to produce a self-contained static archive "so downstream consumers … can statically link one file", naming **the `mpt-crypto-sys` Rust crate and xrpl-py's CFFI** binding; `cmake/BundleStatic.cmake` reasons in detail about symbol collision with `secp256k1-sys` and `rustsecp256k1_v0_*`. `CMakeLists.txt` gained `MPT_CRYPTO_BUNDLE_STATIC` and `MPT_CRYPTO_BUILD_SHARED` for the same purpose | Read at `85dbac7`, 2026-08-17: `CMakeLists.txt:60–140`, `cmake/BundleStatic.cmake:11–21`, `:99–102`, `.github/workflows/build-native-libs.yml:10`, `.github/scripts/build-native-libs.sh:43`, `:97`. **Bears directly on ADR-0001's Rust signer**: the FFI substrate is being built upstream rather than by us. **Whether `mpt-crypto-sys` is actually published is a GAP** — see GAP-11; crates.io refused the API query and no substitute source was used | A on the intent, GAP on the availability |
| E-55 | **Under WASM, sigma-proof randomness comes from the host.** OpenSSL's `RAND_bytes` — the source of the HKDF-Extract salt in E-48 — bottoms out in `getentropy`, which Emscripten routes to **`crypto.getRandomValues`** in a browser and **`node:crypto.randomFillSync`** under Node. Both are CSPRNGs; the point is that the browser case is a host-supplied primitive, which is a different trust story from a native `/dev/urandom` | Read from the generated glue of a locally built module, 2026-08-17: `initRandomFill` in `mpt_crypto.js`, plus the single `getentropy` import in `mpt_crypto.wasm`. **Relevant to ADR-0001, which puts proving in a local daemon rather than a browser** — that choice also decides which RNG signs for the nonce salt | A |
| E-56 | **F-9 was bypassed for local macOS compilation; it remains blocking for the documented contributor path.** mpt-crypto's sources carry no build-system-dependent code, so the unresolvable `conan.lock` can be sidestepped by compiling the nine sources directly with clang against a locally built secp256k1 `v0.7.1` and a system OpenSSL 3 — about fifteen minutes. ⚠ **Narrowed 2026-08-17: both review legs independently called the original "not a blocker" an over-generalisation from a single environment, and they were right.** | Executed 2026-08-17; `tools/spike-2026-08-17/build-native.sh`, and the resulting library generated every proof in E-45 to E-53. **One host, one OS, one compiler, one undocumented ad-hoc script.** That is evidence this spike was unblocked, **not** that the documented Conan path is non-blocking for a clean machine, CI, Linux, or the downstream Rust/Python consumers of E-54. F-9 is **not** downgraded. Only re-scope it after a clean-machine reproduction | **A on "this host was unblocked"; the general claim is withdrawn** |
| E-57 | **GAP-11 closed. The Rust path for ADR-0001's signer already exists upstream and is published.** `mpt-crypto-sys` (low-level FFI) and `mpt-crypto` (safe wrappers) are both on crates.io at `0.1.0-beta.1`, published **2026-06-08** from **`XRPLF/xrpl-rust`** | Queried 2026-08-17 with a User-Agent header. **The 2026-08-17 "refusal" that opened GAP-11 was a missing User-Agent on our side, not a crates.io policy blocking the data** — the gap was correctly recorded rather than backfilled, and closed on a proper retry. ⚠ **Both are `beta.1` and neither has been republished since 2026-06-08, while the C library moved `0.4.0-rc4` → `1.0.4` → `85dbac7` in the same period. Check which mpt-crypto revision the crates pin before scoping the signer** — a stale pin is the obvious risk and was not checked | A on existence and provenance; the version-drift question is untested |
| E-58 | **The auditor view is executed, not inferred.** With an issuance carrying `sfAuditorEncryptionKey`, the auditor's ElGamal key decrypts a holder's `sfAuditorEncryptedBalance` to the exact plaintext balance, matching what the holder reads with their own key and what the issuer reads from the issuer mirror. Read 500 immediately after the Convert, then **re-derived independently as 475 after a 25-unit Send** — the three views track each other through a balance change, which is the stronger statement | Executed 2026-08-17 on issuance `00453A7DD67EB7E60002C33AAD59E7F544C136A8D30F5D52`. **Three parties decrypt the same balance: holder, issuer, auditor — verified twice, at two different balances.** Reinforces P-9 and P-4 — this is a confidentiality model with mandatory issuer visibility and optional third-party visibility, and neither is hideable from the holder's side | A |
| E-59 | **The two harnesses CLAUDE.md requires now exist and both self-verify.** `tools/observer-harness.ts` holds **no keys and links nothing from mpt-crypto** — no vendored C library, no WASM module. It recomputes the canonical `EncZero` from three public inputs using general-purpose primitives only, `@noble/hashes` and `@noble/curves`. *(Corrected 2026-08-17: an earlier wording said "links no cryptography library", which was false — it links two.)*, and `--assert-encrypted-zero` **exits non-zero unless F-1 is demonstrated on live ledger state**. `tools/issuer-decrypt-balance.ts` is the single command CLAUDE.md specifies, taking its key from the environment or a file and never from argv | Executed 2026-08-17. **The reimplementation is cross-checked, not asserted**: it reproduces an on-chain `sfConfidentialBalanceSpending` byte-for-byte, and a perturbed-AccountID control matches nothing on the same object — so the match is evidence rather than coincidence. Negative controls pass: wrong key → exit 1, absent key → exit 2, unknown flag → exit 2, missing key file → exit 2 with no path in the message. ⚠ **The rejection-sampling retry branch is NOT covered by any test** — it fires with probability ~2⁻¹²⁸, so the live cross-check does not exercise it, and this row should not be read as validating it. The issuer tool read the same balance, 475, under **three different keys** (issuer, auditor, holder) from three different mirrors. **This is the first artifact in the project that would fail if a confidentiality claim were false**, which is what CLAUDE.md's testing rule asks for. **Extended 2026-08-18 with a second assertion, `--assert-no-r-reuse`** (E-63): it scans an account's confidential transactions for a `C₁` appearing in more than one transaction — reuse of the ElGamal randomness `r` — and, where found, recovers the difference between the two hidden amounts with no key. **Discriminates in both directions, executed**: exit 1 on the account whose transactions deliberately reused `r`, with the difference recovered from all three participant ciphertexts; exit 0 on two accounts that did not. It correctly does **not** flag `C₁` repeating *within* one Send, which is by design (E-4/F-13). This is a self-check as much as an audit — our own signer could regress into it and the ledger would not complain | A |
| E-52 | **The issuer-view claim is executed, not inferred.** Given only `sfIssuerEncryptedBalance` from the ledger and the issuer's ElGamal secret key, both holders' confidential balances decrypt exactly — 150 and 100, matching what each holder decrypts with their own key. There is no configuration in which a holder is private from the issuer | Executed 2026-08-17 against `account_objects` output for both holders. Confirms TASK ZERO §4's finding by execution and is the honest counterpart to the observer harness required by CLAUDE.md | A |
| E-60 | **A `ConfidentialMPTSend` whose sigma nonces this project derived — not mpt-crypto's — was accepted on Devnet.** The nonce derivation is the construction REVIEW-005c §2 specified: HKDF-SHA-512, `TransactionContextID` bound in, one encoder shared between the nonce preimage and the Fiat–Shamir challenge, wide 64-byte reduction, no rejection sampling, abort on zero, length framing throughout, per-proof-type domain and count separation. ⚠ **Scoped to the sigma proof.** The 946-byte `ZKProof` is 192 B of sigma proof plus a 754 B aggregated Bulletproof; the Bulletproof's randomness is untouched and remains REVIEW-005c §6's open item | **Executed 2026-08-18 on standard Devnet (`network_id` 2, rippled 3.3.0).** `tesSUCCESS`, validated, transaction `EFFBB35CA522DE5A3A4B8FF68FD411298CBB828E04F66B98A8A13F9D37574DD9`; sender's confidential balance moved 40 → 35, decrypted from ledger state after the fact. **Second transaction 2026-08-18 with the HEDGED build, after issue #1 was ruled: `8361C090B2657B23A803B340C32016E02DEA05EF280F59074D50CB793763A199`, `tesSUCCESS`, validated, balance 25 → 20.** Recorded because adversarial review (REVIEW-011c §4) found that the first transaction used the *deterministic* build, so the configuration that became the default had local verification but no ledger acceptance. Both arms are now executed. **Acceptance on chain is not the only evidence, and was not the first**: before submission the PRISTINE upstream library at `85dbac7` verified our proof locally, which is what establishes that the Fiat–Shamir challenge bytes are unchanged. **That local verifier is rippled's verifier, and this is now evidenced rather than asserted** — a second review round flagged the original wording as unsupported, since rippled 3.3.0 ships `0.4.0-rc4` (E-14) and we vendor `1.0.4`. `git diff 0.4.0-rc4 1.0.4 -- src/ include/` is **empty**: the five commits between the tags touch CI, workflows and CMake only, zero library code. `vendor-verify.sh` asserts it on every run. Generator: `tools/spike-2026-08-18/`, patch `wconf-nonces.patch` against pinned commit `85dbac73d033241b73f0565ae7cce86d07005c70`, which **is** tag `1.0.4`, the newest tagged release. **Adversarially reviewed 2026-08-18 in two rounds** — first two independent legs (`WCONF-PATCH.md` §7, `docs/reviews/REVIEW-009-...`): one HIGH defect found and fixed — unchecked `size_t` on the participant count, a heap OOB write this patch's own refactor introduced — plus three LOW and two evidence gaps, all closed. **Neither leg found a defect in the wide reduction or the Fiat–Shamir transcript**, the two things most likely to be wrong. The transaction above was submitted before that review; the accepted proof is unaffected by the fixes, which bound an input range and cleanse buffers rather than changing any derived value — re-verified after the fixes by the same harnesses. **A second Codex round then reviewed the fixes themselves** and returned `fix-before-merge` with five blockers, including a **false-pass mode in the regression test** written for the HIGH finding; all five are fixed and recorded in `WCONF-PATCH.md` §8 | **A, executed** |
| E-61 | **Path A is installable, and cheaper than issue #2 costed it: the Rust FFI crate does not need forking.** `mpt-crypto-sys`'s `build.rs` resolves its library in three ordered steps — (1) the `MPT_CRYPTO_LIB_DIR` environment variable, (2) a `vendor/lib/<target>/` directory, (3) download the pinned upstream release tarball — and links a **prebuilt dynamic library** rather than compiling the C. A patched C library therefore propagates into the Rust signer path via one environment variable, which is step 1 and wins over the other two | Read at `mpt-crypto-sys@0.1.0-beta.1` (`build.rs`; `cargo:rustc-link-lib=dylib=mpt-crypto`), downloaded from crates.io 2026-08-18. Step 3 was checked rather than assumed and **does work** — the `0.3.0-rc2` asset is a real 20 MB tarball of dylibs for six platforms — so `MPT_CRYPTO_LIB_DIR` is a genuine override, not the only functioning path. Step 2 is unusable from the published crate, which ships `vendor/include/` but **no `vendor/lib/`**. ~~**Not yet executed through Rust** — no Rust signer exists and none was built this session~~ — **a Rust signer now exists and links the patched library (E-80), and it does NOT use this crate.** ⚠ **The route E-61 describes was not taken, and that is a correction to this entry's framing rather than a refutation of it.** `mpt-crypto-sys` pins `0.3.0-rc2` and declares seven functions that no longer exist at `1.0.4` (F-18), so binding to it means either a silently old prover or a loud failure. The signer declares its own `extern "C"` surface against the header of the commit it actually links and pins that header's digest at build time. **This entry's grade stays B**: its claim about `build.rs`'s resolution order is still read-not-executed, because nothing executed it. See F-18 for what the override collides with | **B — read, not executed; and the route was not taken** |
| E-62 | **The nonce construction reproduces from its own prose, and draws no entropy through the tree's only entropy route.** Thirteen known-answer vectors generated by the patched C are reproduced exactly by an **independent TypeScript implementation written from the specification text**, not from the C control flow — the method REVIEW-005c §5 credits for leg A's T-2. Eight separation properties assert what the framing is for: context, statement length, domain, count, the hedge flag, and **the `sk_offset` that differs between provers** each change every nonce. Calls to `RAND_bytes` during sigma proof generation are **exactly 1**, at one call site, in the default build, and **0** under `-DWCONF_HEDGE=0`. ⚠ **Corrected 2026-08-18 — the default flipped and this row was briefly false.** Issue #1 ruled the same-host adversary in scope, which made hedged the default; the row had said 0-by-default. **And the count is no longer a discriminator:** upstream's own `generate_deterministic_nonces` also draws exactly one `RAND_bytes` per proof (its Extract salt, `mpt_internal.h:262` at `85dbac7`), so "exactly 1" is consistent with an *unpatched* build. Before the flip, "0" could only be true if upstream's derivation had been replaced. What still discriminates: `vendor-verify.sh`'s assertion that no call site reaches upstream's function, and the deterministic arm's byte-identical reproducibility in `nonce-crosscheck.ts` §2 — the first proves the **tree** is patched, the second proves the **KDF** is ours. Neither proves the *default binary* was built from that tree; closing that is a recorded follow-up (REVIEW-011c §4) | Executed 2026-08-18. `tools/spike-2026-08-18/t1-vectors.ts` (21 checks) and `build-t9.sh` (both arms, counted by overriding `RAND_bytes` for all statically linked callers). ⚠ **The entropy claim is scoped to `RAND_bytes`, deliberately.** Adversarial review noted the override cannot see `RAND_priv_bytes`, `RAND_bytes_ex`, a direct `getentropy`/`getrandom`, or `arc4random`. All seven were grepped at `85dbac7`: the sole occurrence of any is `getentropy` inside a comment this patch itself wrote, so `RAND_bytes` is the only entropy route in the tree **today** — what T-9 would fail to catch is a **new** route added later. ⚠ **The vectors pin the nonce derivation, not the transcript** — the transcript is what the cross-verification against the pristine upstream verifier tests, at n=3 and n=4. Neither alone is sufficient. Closes REVIEW-005b §6.1 items 1 and 4 | **A, executed, scoped as stated** |
| E-63 | **A passive full-ledger observer holding no keys recovered the difference between two confidential amounts, when the ElGamal encryption randomness `r` was reused across two Sends.** `C₁ = r·G` repeats on chain and is visible; subtracting the two `C₂` values annihilates the shared `r·pk_ᵢ` term, leaving `(m − m′)·G`, and a bounded discrete-log search returns the difference. ⚠ **This recovers the DIFFERENCE, not either amount, and it requires a signer bug — reusing `r`. It is NOT a break of XLS-96 as specified and must never be described as one** | **Executed on Devnet 2026-08-18** as a deliberate negative control. Two `ConfidentialMPTSend`s, amounts 7 and 3, one shared `r`, everything else differing. Both `tesSUCCESS`: `E1F1E41AF1ED0F1AA85D69487507B332279BB13DBF06045B3F2DB22B485A8085` and `77A9B8E515F4E918233444A358F0ED2593FE8504098767857BF25DD2A4BDBA77`. `C₁` byte-identical on chain; **`m − m′ = 4` recovered in 0 ms**. Three findings beyond the claim as written: **rippled does not reject a reused `r`** — both accepted first submission, so there is no ledger-side defence; the recovery works **independently from all three ciphertexts** (sender, destination and issuer), so corrupted randomness is not contained to the party that produced it; and the observer half is a **separate file holding no keys and linking no mpt-crypto**, using `@noble/curves` alone. Upgrades issue #3's central claim from asserted to executed. Generator: `tools/spike-randomness-2026-08-18/` | **A, executed** |
| E-64 | **`r` and `ρ` are caller-owned, so whatever the third packet rules for them needs no upstream change and has no FFI problem.** Both come from `mpt_generate_blinding_factor`, which the signer calls; the library never generates them itself. By contrast the six Bulletproof blinding scalars are library-owned. **The half with the demonstrated attack (E-63) is the half the project already controls** | Read at `85dbac7`: `mpt_utility.cpp` `mpt_generate_blinding_factor` is a bare `RAND_bytes` loop, exported in `utility/mpt_utility.h`, and is what every harness in `tools/spike-2026-08-17/` calls for both values. **Also recorded for the packet's consistency:** that function uses `do { RAND_bytes } while (!seckey_verify)` — **rejection sampling**, the exact pattern REVIEW-005c §2 ruled against for nonces | A |
| E-65 | **Deterministic Bulletproof blinding is reachable by the same Path A move, and needs no new information from the caller.** Six of the eight `generate_random_scalar` call sites are inside `secp256k1_bulletproof_prove_agg` and two inside a helper it calls. That prover already receives `values`, `blindings_flat`, `m` and `context_id` — witness and transaction context both — so a per-proof seed can be derived in place and threaded into the one helper via a signature change | Read at `85dbac7`: call sites at `bulletproof_aggregated.c:1153,1157,1406,1408,1413,1415,1731,1733`; signature at `:1292–1295`. ⚠ **Feasibility only — not attempted, and deliberately.** The Bulletproof scalars are **not** analogous to sigma nonces: they are blinding values providing the zero-knowledge property, so making them deterministic makes the range proof a deterministic function of witness and context. Whether that is acceptable is a design question for the packet, not a coding one. This row establishes only that the packet is **not blocked on feasibility** | **B — read, not executed** |
| E-66 | **The XLS-96 registration PoK IS bound to the account, so a registration proof is not copyable to another account. Issue #4 closed, and §(b)'s rogue-key defence is at least as strong as ADR-0001 claims.** `ConfidentialMPTConvert::preclaim` computes `getConvertContextHash(account, issuanceID, sequence)` from `ctx.tx[sfAccount]` — the submitting account — and passes it to `verifySchnorrProof`, which calls `mpt_verify_convert_proof(proof, pk, contextHash.data())`. The utility wrapper **rejects a NULL context hash** before forwarding, and `build_pok_challenge` folds it into the Fiat–Shamir challenge `SHA-256("CMPT_POK_SK_REGISTER" ‖ pk(33) ‖ T(33) ‖ context_id(32))`. The context hash itself is `sha512_half(u16(txtype) ‖ acc(20) ‖ iss(24) ‖ u32(seq) ‖ acc(20) ‖ u32(0))` — the account appears **twice**. The proof therefore binds **(account, issuance, sequence)**: not copyable across accounts, not replayable across issuances, not replayable at a different sequence | **Read at source 2026-08-18**, rippled tag `3.3.0` (commit `00a178f`) and mpt-crypto `85dbac7` = `1.0.4`: `ConfidentialMPTConvert.cpp:74,153–162`; `ConfidentialTransfer.cpp:366–375`; `mpt_utility.cpp:286–297,349–369,859–883`; `proof_pok_sk.c` `build_pok_challenge`. Full chain in `docs/task-zero/SOURCES-2026-08-18.md` §1. ⚠ **Read, not executed** — no negative control was run submitting a stolen registration proof from a second account. The chain is five links of source and the NULL guard is explicit, but the executed form would be stronger | **A, read** |
| E-67 | **XLS-0096 nowhere states that the ElGamal encryption randomness `r` must not be reused across transactions. The specification is SILENT, and that is a specification gap.** B-12 closed. The only "reuse" statement about randomness points the other way — §5.4 says the `AmountCommitment` *"intentionally reuses the ElGamal ciphertext randomness as its blinding factor"*, which is deliberate reuse **within** one transaction and reads as permission. `BlindingFactor` is described (§7.2, §10.3) with no freshness, sampling or distribution requirement; the failure conditions (§7.3.1, §10.4.1) check only 32-byte length and consistency with the ciphertexts; **§13.8 "Attack Surface & Mitigations" does not list randomness reuse**; and §A.7 "What prevents replay or proof reuse?" answers about *proof* reuse via transcript binding, not randomness. rippled 3.3.0 stores nothing that could detect it — `ConfidentialMPTConvert.cpp:179` and `ConfidentialMPTConvertBack.cpp:80` read the field and pass it to `verifyRevealedAmount` | **Obtained and read 2026-08-18** — `XRPLF/XRPL-Standards`, `XLS-0096-confidential-mpt/README.md`, 971 lines, the text this repository had never held. ⚠ **This is proving a negative.** Searched `reuse`/`re-use`/`reused`/`fresh`/`random`/`randomness`/`blinding`/`nonce`/`entropy`/`CSPRNG`/`unique`/`distinct`/`unpredictab`/`independent scalar`/`per-transaction`/`each transaction`/`MUST`, and read §§5.4, 7, 10, 13 and Appendix A directly. A statement in vocabulary none of that reaches would not have been caught. **Decides U-37's shape:** silence makes the specification gap fileable on its own, per B-12's own test | **A on the read, with the negative-proof caveat stated** |
| E-68 | **The keygen construction reproduces from its own prose, by two implementations, and its test is not a false pass.** The ruling's two known-answer vectors are reproduced by an implementation calling `HMAC-SHA-512` directly and by a second going through `@noble/hashes`' `hkdf()`; both agree on `PRK`, `OKM64`, `sk_A` and `pk_A`. **`PRK` and `OKM64` are leg B's executed values, recomputed independently during adjudication and reproduced exactly** — the check REVIEW-010c §3 exists to force, coming out the other way this time. 34 assertions pass, covering the vectors, cross-implementation agreement, scalar range, the **totality of the reduction** (`OKM = 0^64` yields `sk_A = 1`, not an abort), six separation properties in which every framing element changes `OKM64`, and three malformed-input refusals | **Executed 2026-08-18**: `tools/spike-keygen-2026-08-18/t-keygen-construction.ts`, 34 passed / 0 failed, exit 0. **Negative control run:** corrupting one byte of one vector makes it exit 1 with 33/1 — so the harness is not a false pass, which is the defect round two found in the nonce spike's own regression test. ⚠ **The construction is RULED, not implemented.** No signer exists and no keygen code is wired into anything; this test exercises published vectors and nothing else. ⚠ **Both implementations were written by the adjudicating session** — a weaker independence claim than two authors, and stated rather than hidden | **A on reproduction, executed** |
| E-69 | **An XRPL account's master seed need not be its spending authority, which is the fact that falsifies ADR-0001 §(e)'s central defence.** An account may authorise a regular key and **disable the master key**, or enforce multi-signing through a `SignerList`. In those configurations the master seed signs nothing — yet under the keygen ruling it still derives `sk_A` and therefore still decrypts the holder's entire confidential history, permanently, with no rotation. §(e) argues the marginal harm of seed compromise is small because *"seed compromise already means total loss of spending authority"*; **for these holders that premise is false and the confidentiality loss is the whole loss.** This is why the ruling's condition 2 obliges an onboarding disclosure and a signer warning | **Read at source 2026-08-18**, rippled tag `3.3.0` (`00a178f`): `asfDisableMaster` at `include/xrpl/protocol/TxFlags.h:421`; `lsfDisableMaster = 0x00100000`, commented *"True, force regular key"*, at `include/xrpl/protocol/LedgerFormats.h:142`; enforced at `src/libxrpl/tx/Transactor.cpp:1024`; `SetRegularKey.cpp` and `SignerListSet.cpp` present under `src/libxrpl/tx/transactors/account/`. ⚠ **Read, not executed** — no account was configured this way on Devnet and no Convert was attempted from one. Raised by the self leg of REVIEW-012, which flagged the claim as recalled; it was read before entering the ruling. **Both external legs missed it** | **A, read** |
import { createFileRoute } from "@tanstack/react-router";
import type { ReactNode } from "react";
import { Panel } from "../../components/Verdict";
import { SNAPSHOT } from "../data";
import { FacilityDiagram, LifecycleDiagram, LocalArchitectureDiagram, WhoSeesWhatDiagram } from "../Diagrams";

export const Route = createFileRoute("/how-it-works")({ component: HowItWorks });

const REPO = "https://github.com/nandanito/xrpl-confidential-positions";
/** A repository file at the build's own commit, so the link shows the text the gate passed with. */
const AT_BUILD = (path: string) => `${REPO}/blob/${SNAPSHOT.commit}/${path}`;

/**
 * The explainer: what confidential transfers are, and the example this project
 * built, for a reader who has never met XLS-96.
 *
 * ⚠ **No ledger value, by design.** Check 8 of the hosted gate forbids an
 * address, an issuance ID or a ciphertext on any page but the two ledger
 * pages, so this page names the demo accounts by label only.
 *
 * ⚠ **Every sentence restates a register row or an explainer document**, and
 * the ids under each panel name them. The words *decrypt* and *plaintext* are
 * avoided on purpose: the gate's no-issuer-figure check (8b) reads either one
 * followed by a digit in the same sentence as a figure, and this page states
 * none.
 */
function HowItWorks() {
  return (
    <>
      <Panel title="Confidential balances, in brief">
        <div className="text-sm leading-relaxed space-y-2">
          <p>
            The XRP Ledger is public: anyone can read every account and every transaction on it.
            Multi-Purpose Tokens (MPTs, XLS-33) are one of its kinds of token. Confidential Transfers
            (XLS-96) let an MPT holder keep their balance encrypted on that public ledger: hidden from
            the public, not from the token&rsquo;s issuer.
          </p>
          <p>
            Each confidential balance is stored as an ElGamal ciphertext: a number only the right key
            can read. A holder registers their own encryption key when they opt in.
          </p>
          <p>
            The ledger must still check that nobody spends more than they hold. So every transaction
            that spends from a confidential balance carries zero-knowledge proofs, which every validator
            checks without learning any hidden amount.
          </p>
          <p>
            XLS-96 is a final standard. When this page was written, on 2026-10-21, its amendment,
            ConfidentialTransfer, was enabled on Devnet and not on Mainnet or Testnet.
          </p>
        </div>
        <Sources>E-1, E-349; the README&rsquo;s terms; TASK ZERO §3</Sources>
      </Panel>

      <Panel title="The life of a confidential position">
        <LifecycleDiagram />
        <div className="text-sm leading-relaxed space-y-2 mt-4">
          <p>
            <strong>Convert</strong> (<code>ConfidentialMPTConvert</code>) moves tokens from the
            holder&rsquo;s public balance into their confidential inbox, and the amount is published. The
            first Convert also registers the holder&rsquo;s key, which can never be changed.{" "}
            <strong>Send</strong> (<code>ConfidentialMPTSend</code>) moves an amount from one confidential
            balance to another; the amount is hidden, the sender and receiver are not, and it too arrives
            in the receiver&rsquo;s inbox. <strong>ConvertBack</strong>{" "}
            (<code>ConfidentialMPTConvertBack</code>) moves tokens back to a public balance, and the amount
            is published, on every ConvertBack separately.
          </p>
          <p>
            <strong>Tokens in the inbox cannot be sent yet.</strong> A separate transaction,
            MergeInbox, moves the inbox into the spending balance, so a holder who converts must merge
            before their first Send.
          </p>
          <p>
            <strong>Only Send hides an amount.</strong> The amount is encrypted separately for the
            sender, the receiver, the issuer and any auditor. Send&rsquo;s proofs show that every copy is
            the same number, that the sender holds the key to their balance, and that neither the amount
            nor the sender&rsquo;s remaining balance is negative.
          </p>
          <p>
            Why are both ends public? The ledger shows that no tokens are created inside the
            confidential pool by keeping its total in plain view. The token records the total held in
            confidential form, and every Convert and ConvertBack changes it by a published amount.
          </p>
        </div>
        <Sources>E-9, E-33, F-24, F-27, P-4, P-11; TASK ZERO §3; WHAT-CONFIDENTIAL-MEANS</Sources>
      </Panel>

      <Panel title="What is hidden, and what is public">
        <div className="grid gap-4 sm:grid-cols-2 text-sm">
          <div>
            <h3 className="font-semibold mb-1">Hidden from the public</h3>
            <ul className="list-disc pl-5 space-y-1">
              <li>Each holder&rsquo;s balance, except in the zero cases under &ldquo;Public, always&rdquo;.</li>
              <li>The amount of each Send.</li>
            </ul>
            <p className="mt-2 opacity-80">Both are hidden from the public, not from the issuer.</p>
          </div>
          <div>
            <h3 className="font-semibold mb-1">Public, always</h3>
            <ul className="list-disc pl-5 space-y-1">
              <li>Who sends to whom, and when, so how often anyone transacts.</li>
              <li>Every amount converted in or out, each one separately.</li>
              <li>The token&rsquo;s total supply, and the total held in confidential form.</li>
              <li>
                That a balance is exactly zero, in three cases: when it is first set up, when a
                MergeInbox empties the inbox, and after an issuer clawback, which destroys the balance. A
                balance spent down to zero looks like any other.
              </li>
            </ul>
          </div>
        </div>
        <p className="text-sm leading-relaxed mt-4">
          The public numbers also bound the hidden ones. If a holder converts in 500, receives nothing
          and converts back 400, anyone can work out that the other 100 is either still held or has left
          by confidential sends. No key is needed.
        </p>
        <Sources>P-4, P-9, P-11, F-24, F-1, E-211, R-1, R-7</Sources>
      </Panel>

      <Panel title="Who can read a balance, and who can spend it">
        <WhoSeesWhatDiagram />
        <ul className="text-sm leading-relaxed list-disc pl-5 space-y-1">
          <li>The holder can read their own balance, with their key.</li>
          <li>
            The issuer can always read every balance. The protocol keeps a second copy of each balance,
            encrypted to the issuer&rsquo;s key, and no setting turns it off.
          </li>
          <li>An auditor can too, if the issuer names one. Only one auditor is supported.</li>
          <li>
            This was run, not inferred: the holder, the issuer and the auditor each read the same
            balance, and again after it changed.
          </li>
          <li>
            Spending needs the holder&rsquo;s key: a Send&rsquo;s proof cannot be made without it. So the
            issuer cannot spend a holder&rsquo;s balance, though it can destroy it and may be able to
            freeze it (see Limits). Where the key is derived from the account&rsquo;s master seed,
            whoever holds the seed holds the key too.
          </li>
        </ul>
        <Sources>P-9, E-58, E-11, E-33, E-118, E-119</Sources>
      </Panel>

      <Panel title="The example: a confidential receivables facility">
        <div className="text-sm leading-relaxed mb-4 rounded border border-black/10 dark:border-white/15 p-3">
          <p>
            <strong>The other pages do not show this facility.</strong> They read four Devnet test
            accounts that hold one demo token, made by this project while it was being built: Holder A,
            an active position; Holder B, whose key is derived from its master seed; Holder C, the
            account the project&rsquo;s ed25519 signing ran on; and Holder D, an early test account.
            &ldquo;What anyone sees&rdquo; and &ldquo;What the issuer sees&rdquo; show how those accounts
            look to an observer and to the issuer. The facility below ran separately, and its record is
            in the repository:{" "}
            <a className="underline" href={AT_BUILD("docs/explainer/FACILITY-WALKTHROUGH-2026-08-20.md")}>
              the facility walkthrough
            </a>.
          </p>
        </div>
        <div className="text-sm leading-relaxed space-y-2">
          <p>
            A bank pays a large buyer&rsquo;s suppliers early, against their invoices. How much a
            supplier draws says a lot about its cash position, so these numbers are commercially
            sensitive.
          </p>
          <p>
            This project built the core of that facility on XLS-96 and ran it on Devnet on 2026-08-25, in
            28 validated facility transactions besides setting up the token:
          </p>
          <FacilityDiagram />
          <p>
            The run shows that these transactions are well formed and that rippled accepts them. It read
            the ledger through a public Devnet node, not one on the same machine as the design requires,
            so it says nothing about that requirement.
          </p>
        </div>
        <div className="grid gap-4 sm:grid-cols-2 text-sm mt-4">
          <div>
            <h3 className="font-semibold mb-1">Anyone can see</h3>
            <ul className="list-disc pl-5 space-y-1">
              <li>The size of the float.</li>
              <li>Which suppliers are enrolled, and both public amounts of each enrolment.</li>
              <li>Who drew and repaid, on which days, and how often.</li>
              <li>The size of every retirement tranche.</li>
            </ul>
          </div>
          <div>
            <h3 className="font-semibold mb-1">Hidden from the public</h3>
            <ul className="list-disc pl-5 space-y-1">
              <li>Each supplier&rsquo;s limit.</li>
              <li>The size of each drawdown and repayment.</li>
              <li>What each supplier still owes.</li>
            </ul>
            <p className="mt-2 opacity-80">The bank, as issuer, sees all of it.</p>
          </div>
        </div>
        <p className="text-sm leading-relaxed mt-4">
          <strong>In short, it hides the shape of the book, not its size.</strong>
        </p>
        <Sources>E-146, B-18, E-151, F-24, F-26, R-1; the four accounts: E-208, E-118, E-176, E-101</Sources>
      </Panel>

      <Panel title="How the project runs on a holder's machine">
        <p className="text-sm leading-relaxed">
          This site only shows. The project itself runs on one machine: a Rust program,{" "}
          <code>wconf-signer</code>, holds the keys and signs, and everything else talks to it over a local
          socket that no web page can open.
        </p>
        <LocalArchitectureDiagram />
        <Sources>the README&rsquo;s &ldquo;How it fits together&rdquo;; E-146, B-15</Sources>
      </Panel>

      <Panel title="Limits" tone="warn">
        <ul className="text-sm leading-relaxed list-disc pl-5 space-y-2">
          <li>
            <strong>No key rotation or recovery in XLS-96.</strong> A registered key can never be changed.
            If a holder loses it, and any seed it can be re-derived from, the balance cannot be spent, and
            the only remedy is an issuer clawback, which destroys the balance. A key this project derives
            from the master seed can be re-derived from that seed, but the derivation is not a published
            standard. A draft standard, XLS-99, proposes rotation and recovery; on 2026-10-20 Mainnet,
            Testnet and Devnet did not know its amendment.
          </li>
          <li>
            <strong>The issuer can act without the holder&rsquo;s consent.</strong> If the token allows
            clawback, the issuer can destroy a holder&rsquo;s whole confidential balance; this
            project&rsquo;s tokens allow it. Unless the token&rsquo;s flags were made unchangeable when it
            was created, and this project&rsquo;s were not, the issuer can also turn on locking later and
            freeze balances. That second power was read in rippled&rsquo;s source, not run.
          </li>
          <li>
            <strong>Not audited.</strong> This is a research project on a test network. It is not
            audited, not production-ready (P-5) and not safe to put value behind.
          </li>
        </ul>
        <Sources>P-10, E-9, E-119, E-341, E-12, E-21, E-223, E-290, E-292, P-5</Sources>
      </Panel>

      <p className="text-xs opacity-60 mb-6">
        Each id under a panel, such as E-58, is a row in the project&rsquo;s claims register,{" "}
        <a className="underline" href={AT_BUILD("CLAIMS.md")}>CLAIMS.md</a>. Where this page and a
        row disagree, the row wins.
      </p>
    </>
  );
}

function Sources({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {
  return <p className="text-xs opacity-60 mt-3">Sources: {children}</p>;
}

# CLAIMS register

⚠ **`tapFailHard` in this document is THIS PROJECT'S SHORTHAND and appears zero times in rippled** — the real names are `fail_hard` (the RPC parameter) and `TapFailHard` (the internal apply flag). See [`docs/VOCABULARY.md`](docs/VOCABULARY.md). The code always used the real one.

**The register.** Every capability claim in a README, demo, post or conversation gets an entry here with the test result or primary citation that supports it. **Before publishing anything, diff the new claims against this register.**

**Human-gated checklist, not automation.** CI cannot judge whether prose overclaims. Do not add a CI job and call it solved.

## Standing prohibitions — never claim

| # | Claim | Why |
|---|---|---|
| P-1 | That this work makes XRPL quantum-safe or quantum-resistant | It does not |
| P-2 | That an application-layer PQ scheme protects funds at the ledger level | An adversary who breaks secp256k1 signs a valid classical transaction and the network accepts it |
| P-3 | That any amendment is live or enabled, without a dated `feature` RPC check | Amendment state is a reading, not knowledge |
| P-4 | That a Confidential MPT demo hides identities | It hides amounts and balances. Sender and receiver remain public |
| P-5 | That anything here is trustless, non-custodial, decentralised, audited, production-ready or secure | None of it is; ADR-0001 names the trusted parties |
| P-6 | That anything is compliant with regulation in a named jurisdiction | Not a compliance project |
| P-7 | That analysis of published work is original research | Cite Cenk, Malhotra, Akinyele, Chen, Xie, Qian by name where the work is theirs. **Add Tess Dore (2026-08-16):** the compact sigma protocol, the twisted-ElGamal analysis and the Bulletproofs++ analysis are Dore & Cenk, RippleX Research — Dore is *not* an XLS-0096 author and is easy to omit by copying E-1's list |
| P-8 | Any benchmark number without hardware, parameter set and methodology | Includes re-quoting XLS-0096 §14.2's "laptop-class CPU" timings — see B-1 |
| P-9 | That holders are private from the issuer | The issuer's decryption mirror is mandatory. No configuration makes this false |
| P-10 | That XLS-96 supports key rotation or key recovery | It supports neither, at any level |
| P-11 | That confidential transfers hide *who* transacts, or *when*, or *how often* | Only amounts are hidden. The counterparty graph and event timing are fully public |
| P-12 | **Any claim that a source does NOT say something — *nothing documents it*, *no such field*, *not exported*, *nobody has called it out*, *I could not find it* — without the search that would falsify it recorded alongside** | It is an assertion about a whole corpus and one search settles it. **Three graded rows failed this way on 2026-08-26** (E-155, E-156), two of them **positives**: U-15 quoted a sentence XLS-0096 has never contained; U-12 said a property was *"not called out anywhere I found"* when the spec states it **twice**, once in wording identical to the benefit claimed as an original reading. ⚠ **This shape survives review because it does not look like a claim** — a quotation has quotation marks, a benchmark visibly wants a methodology, and *"nobody has documented this"* reads as modesty. Gated by `tools/check-negative-claims.ts` |

## Established

| # | Claim | Evidence | Grade |
|---|---|---|---|
| E-1 | XLS-0096 authors: Cenk, Malhotra, Akinyele, Chen, Xie, Qian; status Draft, created 2026-01-15. ⚠ **Superseded upstream on 2026-09-03.** XLS-0096 PR #604, *"Update and finalize XLS-96"*, merged as `33ea4ca66` and set `status: Final`. It reads `Final` at `58ccd3047` and at `master` `47fa15357`, read on 2026-10-18 (E-322) | Spec header, at the time; the header at `9fbf7ef8e` (Draft) and at `33ea4ca66`, `58ccd3047` and `master` (Final) for the supersession | A |
| E-2 | Transaction types 85 Convert, 86 MergeInbox, 87 ConvertBack, 88 Send, 89 Clawback | `transactions.macro` @ 3.3.0, matches spec | A |
| E-3 | The Send `ZKProof` is a fixed-length bundle of a compact sigma proof plus an aggregated Bulletproof, enforced at preflight | `Protocol.h:466–471`; `ConfidentialMPTSend.cpp` length check | A |
| E-3a | Those components are 192 B and 754 B, totalling 946 B | **GAP-6 closed 2026-08-16.** `secp256k1_mpt.h:313` (192), `mpt_protocol.h:35` (754) at commit `48a3cfe` = `0.4.0-rc4`; identical at `1.0.4`. Independently **executed**: `test_bulletproof_agg` prints 754 B for m=2, 688 B for m=1. **Confirmed invariant in the participant count 2026-08-17: the `ZKProof` is 946 B at n=3 and 946 B at n=4**, as the structure implies — the sigma proof is six scalars regardless of how many recipients share the ciphertext randomness | **A** |
| E-4 | Total Send payload is **1210 B without an auditor and 1276 B with one — both now MEASURED.** The conflicting 1177 B is **wrong for the wire** | **RESOLVED 2026-08-17 by measurement.** Three `ConfidentialMPTSend` transactions serialised and accepted on standard Devnet. Each encrypted-amount field carries a **full 66-byte ElGamal pair**, not a bare 33-byte `C₂`: `SenderEncryptedAmount` 66, `DestinationEncryptedAmount` 66, `IssuerEncryptedAmount` 66, `AmountCommitment` 33, `BalanceCommitment` 33, `ZKProof` 946 = **1210 B**. The whole signed blob is **1423 B**. **Both documents were partly right and the reason matters (→ F-13):** `C₁` *is* shared, and this is **enforced by the protocol, not chosen by our harness** — a distinction REVIEW-004b HIGH-03 was right to demand and which the original evidence could not support, since it amounted to the harness observing its own input. Settled by negative control (REVIEW-004c §3): a Send built with independent per-participant randomness is refused at three layers — `mpt_get_confidential_send_proof` returns `-1` and generates nothing, `mpt_verify_send_proof` returns FAIL, and **rippled returns `tecBAD_PROOF`** where the shared-`C₁` control returns `tesSUCCESS`. The ledger nonetheless transmits `C₁` **once per participant**. The paper's 1177 is the size this would be if `C₁` were sent once, which rippled 3.3.0 does not do. The 99 B is exactly the 3 redundant 33-byte `C₁` copies at n=4. Our arithmetic was right; the paper's assumption about the wire was not. **The auditor case was measured on 2026-08-17**, closing the n=3 limitation the claims-check gate had flagged: an issuance carrying `sfAuditorEncryptionKey` was built and a four-participant Send accepted. Payload **exactly 1276 B** — `SenderEncryptedAmount` 66, `DestinationEncryptedAmount` 66, `IssuerEncryptedAmount` 66, **`AuditorEncryptedAmount` 66**, `AmountCommitment` 33, `BalanceCommitment` 33, `ZKProof` 946. Signed blob **1492 B** (n=3: 1423). The arithmetic prediction was confirmed rather than merely assumed, and **the paper's 1177 for n=4 is now refuted against a direct n=4 measurement rather than against a hypothetical** | **A on both figures, measured** |
| E-5 | **A confidential transaction costs exactly 10× the network's standard base fee.** The widely-quoted "100 drops" is that ratio against a `base_fee` of 10 and is **network-dependent, not absolute** | **Mechanism executed 2026-08-17 (REVIEW-004c §5), closing the "arithmetic not verified" gap that had stood since 2026-08-13.** Measured by ladder on Devnet where `fee` RPC reports `base_fee = 1`: `ConfidentialMPTSend` returns `telINSUF_FEE_P` at 1, 5 and 9 drops and `tesSUCCESS` at **10**, on both zero-amount and non-zero arms; a standard `AccountSet` from the same account is accepted at **1 drop**. Ratio 10:1, executed. `kConfidentialFeeMultiplier = 9` (`Protocol.h:492`); `Transactor::calculateBaseFee` still unread, but the result no longer depends on reading it. **Publish the ratio, never the absolute number** | **A on the ratio, executed; the "100 drops" figure is B and network-dependent** |
| E-6 | `kTxMaxSizeBytes = megabytes(1)` — size is not a binding constraint | `Protocol.h`, executed | A |
| E-7 | Confidential MPTs are incompatible with a non-zero `TransferFee` | Spec §6.4; enforced at `MPTokenIssuanceSet.cpp:214` | A |
| E-8 | Confidential MPTs cannot be used with the DEX, escrow or checks | Spec §A.4; RippleX docs | A |
| E-9 | Holder, issuer and auditor encryption keys cannot be rotated once registered | `tecDUPLICATE` / `tecNO_PERMISSION` paths; docs concur | A |
| E-10 | A holder who loses their ElGamal key permanently loses access | Spec §A.6; docs | A |
| E-11 | Only one auditor is supported | Spec §A.12 | A |
| E-12 | Clawback burns the entire balance, decrementing both OA and COA, crediting no account | `ConfidentialMPTClawback.cpp:170–179` | A |
| E-13 | ~~`xrpl.js@5.0.0` and `ripple-binary-codec@2.8.0` contain no Confidential MPT types or fields~~ **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-16 → E-30.** True of the versions named; **`ripple-binary-codec@2.9.0`, published 2026-08-13, ships all five types and every confidential field** | Direct inspection, 2026-08-13 (2.8.0) and 2026-08-16 (2.9.0) | A |
| E-14 | mpt-crypto is C/C++; `0.4.0-rc4` in rippled 3.3.0. ~~no bindings, no WASM target, no published package~~ **PARTLY SUPERSEDED 2026-08-16 → E-31.** A WASM target exists in CI and exports proof generation for all four proving transactions. **No published package** — `@xrplf/mpt-crypto` returns `Not found` on the npm registry as at 2026-08-16 | Repo inspection; rippled changelog PR #7813; `.github/scripts/build-wasm.sh` at `1.0.4`; npm registry query | A |
## Emphasis

`docs/EMPHASIS-DISCIPLINE.md` is the rule and `make check-emphasis` is the gate,
built 2026-09-09. `make break-emphasis-gate` is the mutations that verify it and
`make break-emphasis-gate-self-test` verifies those.

⚠⚠ **Three adversarial rounds ran on this gate — `REVIEW-043`, `-044`, `-045` —
and the rotation is CLOSED at three, which DEPARTS from the two-round rule's
own extension clause.** That clause owes a further round whenever the last one's
stopping condition did not fire, and round three's did not. **The rule is not
amended; this artifact is an exception with a stated reason**: all three rounds
found defects in hand-written parsing of a format that has a reference
implementation, and round three deleted that layer rather than patching it a
seventh time. `REVIEW-045d` carries the three conditions that reopen it, and
`CLAIMS.md` E-264 is the ruling.

**One `⚠⚠` per row at most; `⚠` means a retraction or a correction, not
"important"; three `⚠` per row at most; a `⚠` starts a sentence; bold under 20%
of a row; no narrative paragraph over 800 characters.**

⚠ The density grew from **0 to 2.1 per KB** with no rule ever written, and the
session that measured it was writing at **4.0**. Enforced forward from a cutoff;
history is left as written, because this register keeps what it believed.

⚠ **This file is DENSER than the register the rule was argued from — 2.70 ⚠ per
KB against 2.13 — and was never sampled** (`CLAIMS.md` E-252). It has no row
ids, so it has no cutoff and gets **rule 6 only, as a ratchet** at eleven
over-length paragraphs. A twelfth fails the gate. ⚠ **This said thirteen until
2026-09-09**, when `REVIEW-045` made rule 6 answer through `cmark-gfm` instead
of a hand-written splitter and the true count turned out to be lower. Rules 1 and 3 to 5 do not
reach this file at all.

## Reporting

At the end of each milestone: what runs, what does not, what you learned that was not in the spec, and what should change in the plan as a result. The last item is the Compound step and it is not optional.

## Personas

`docs/personas/` is canonical. `.claude/agents/` is generated from it — regenerate whenever a persona changes or the two will drift.
## What XLS-96 hides, and what it does not

This section is about the **protocol**. Where it describes this project's own demonstration, it says so.

**Hidden.** Individual holder balances, and the amounts moved by confidential transfers.

**Public, always.** The sender and receiver of every transfer; the transaction type; the token; total supply and total confidential supply; the amount of every conversion into or out of confidential form; and the fact and timing of every transfer, so how often anyone transacts.

**Who can decrypt every balance.** The issuer, always: every confidential balance carries a mandatory encrypted mirror under the issuer's key. An auditor too, if one is named. This was executed, not inferred: holder, issuer and auditor each decrypted the same balance, at two different balances (E-58). Registered keys cannot be rotated (E-9), and only one auditor is supported (E-11). Whether naming an auditor can be undone is unknown; this project has not searched for it (P-12).

**What the issuer can do without consent.** If the issuance allows clawback, burn any holder's entire confidential balance; it is destroyed, not credited to anyone (E-12, E-21). This project's issuances allow it, so in every demonstration here the issuer can. And unless an issuance was created with its flags made immutable, which this project's were not, the issuer can also freeze balances by enabling `CanLock` and locking, which `DynamicMPT` allows after creation (E-290, E-292; read at source, not executed).

**Who can spend.** Only the holder of `sk_A`: a `ConfidentialMPTSend` proof cannot be made without it (E-33). Where `sk_A` is derived from the holder's XRPL master seed, whoever holds that seed holds it too. The demonstration does not show holder-only custody, because its facility driver plays every party and holds every seed. And a hostile process running as the same user can read `sk_A` out of memory.

**Lost keys.** If a holder loses `sk_A`, the balance cannot be spent, and XLS-96 has no rotation or recovery (P-10); the only remedy is issuer clawback, which destroys the balance. A draft standard, XLS-99, proposes holder key rotation and key-loss recovery; on 2026-10-20 its amendment was in rippled `develop` as not supported, and Mainnet, Testnet and Devnet did not know it (E-341). A key derived from the master seed under this project's own construction can be re-derived from that seed, but the derivation is not a published standard (issue #10).

**A zero balance can be visible.** Encrypted zero is written to a balance field in three cases: the spending balance on first initialisation, the inbox when `MergeInbox` empties it, and the whole position on clawback. Each is recognisable on sight, so enrolment is publicly visible (F-1). A balance spent down to zero holds a randomised encryption of zero and is not recognisable (E-211).

**In short, it hides the shape of the book, not its size.** The float, because converting it into confidential form publishes the amount, every retirement tranche, and every bank–supplier relationship with its timing, so how often each supplier draws, are public. Each supplier's limit and the sizes of its drawdowns and outstanding balance are not; this project once claimed more was hidden (R-1). The public perimeter still bounds the interior by arithmetic: convert in 500, receive nothing, convert back 400, and anyone knows the other 100 is either still held or has left by confidential transfer. [`WHAT-CONFIDENTIAL-MEANS`](docs/explainer/WHAT-CONFIDENTIAL-MEANS-2026-09-02.md) sets this out at length.

**What this is not:** non-custodial, trustless, private from the issuer, or anonymous. It is not audited, not production-ready, and not compliant with any regulation.

### Before opting in

A holder's first `ConfidentialMPTConvert` commits them three ways. The first and third are irreversible; the second lasts while any holder of the issuance has a non-zero confidential balance:

1. It registers an encryption key that **can never be changed**.
2. It blocks deleting their `MPToken`, and so locks its reserve, **while any holder of the issuance has a non-zero confidential balance**. XLS-0096 §7.2 and rippled's source agree: a holder's own deletion fails with `tecHAS_OBLIGATIONS` until the issuance's `ConfidentialOutstandingAmount` is zero, which no single holder controls. Read at XLS-0096 `58ccd30` and rippled 3.3.0, not executed, and not re-read at the 3.4.1 Devnet now runs (F-29, E-316).
3. It publishes the converted amount **in plaintext**.

---

## How it fits together

```
  ONE machine. The demonstration runs every role locally; that is a property
  of the demo, not of the design.
 ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │                                                                        │
 │   web/app          web/api             ┌────────────────────────────┐  │
 │   TanStack Start   Elysia              │  signer/  wconf-signer     │  │
 │   React 19  ──────▶ READ-ONLY   ──────▶│  Rust binary               │  │
 │   :3000      HTTP   :3011        UDS   │                            │  │
 │                        │    Status,    │   ElGamal secret  sk_A     │  │
 │                        │    and only   │   XRPL account key         │  │
 │   facility/            │    Status     │   proof generation         │  │
 │   TypeScript ──────────┼───────────────▶   transaction signing      │  │
 │   holds every          │          UDS  │   per-transaction          │  │
 │   party's seed         │    Pair,      │     confirmation UI        │  │
 │        │               │    Prepare    │   reads, never submits     │  │
 │        │               │               └─────────────┬──────────────┘  │
 └────────┼───────────────┼─────────────────────────────┼─────────────────┘
          │ submits       │ reads                       │ reads
          ▼               ▼                             ▼
          XRPL Devnet — network_id 2, rippled 3.4.1 when read on 2026-10-18
```

The signer answers three requests. `Pair` opens a grant: a named set of operations, valid for a limited time and only on that socket connection. `Prepare` asks for one transaction under a grant; the signer builds it, shows it to the holder and returns the signed bytes. `Status` carries no authority.

- **[`signer/`](signer/)** — a Rust binary on the holder's machine. Each instance holds one participant's `sk_A` and XRPL key; in the demonstration the facility writes each party's profile and starts one signer per party. It builds the proofs and signs, and shows what it is about to sign in its own window, parsed from the transaction it built, so a caller that lies in its request cannot get a different transaction confirmed. It refuses every network but Devnet. It reads the ledger and **never submits**, so whatever submits must set `fail_hard`: a failing confidential transaction applied as a `tec` lands on the ledger with its ciphertexts (F-23, E-149). The flag stops that at the ledger, not at the node the blob was handed to (F-28), and nothing forces a submitter to set it (B-15).
- **[`facility/`](facility/)** — the receivables facility: a Dedicated Account, supplier enrolment, drawdowns, repayments, an off-ledger balance book and retirement in tranches. It is a single-machine test driver that plays the bank, the Dedicated Account and every supplier, so it holds every seed and derives every party's `sk_A` from it. It sends the signer an operation, an issuance, a destination and an amount. The signer refuses a request that carries a seed, key or randomness field; that is a check over a named set of field names, not of what a value contains.
- **[`web/`](web/)** — a **read-only** viewer: `web/api` (Elysia, bound to loopback) and `web/app` (TanStack Start, React 19). It renders public ledger state, the observer's view, and an issuer page drawn from a snapshot that `tools/issuer-view-snapshot.ts` wrote; that page says whether the issuer's decryption succeeded, never the figure (E-241). It asks the signer for `Status` only, which [`tools/check-web-readonly.ts`](tools/check-web-readonly.ts) checks by reading `web/api`'s source; it cannot prove what is sent at run time.
- **[`tools/`](tools/)** — the observer harness; the issuer-view tools, which hold the issuer key (`issuer-view-snapshot.ts` writes the viewer's snapshot, `issuer-decrypt-balance.ts` prints a figure); the live amendment reader; and the repository's checks.

**The signer is a real boundary for the browser, and only for the browser.** No API available to a web page can open a Unix socket, so a script injected into the page cannot open the signer's socket; the most it can reach is `Status`, through `web/api`. That closes the path by construction rather than by an origin check, which any local process can forge. It does not stop an adversary with code execution on the holder's machine. The authority on the design is [`ARCHITECTURE-2026-08-18.md`](docs/architecture/ARCHITECTURE-2026-08-18.md).

---

## Run it

/**
 * What a full-ledger observer sees.
 *
 * ⚠ **A demo of the happy path is not evidence of confidentiality** — this is
 * the surface that says what is actually public, and its first duty is
 * `CLAIMS.md` **F-1**: a freshly-initialised or post-merge balance is
 * **publicly identifiable as exactly zero**, because `EncZero` is canonical and
 * recomputable by anyone from the holder's public key, the account id and the
 * issuance id. The observer harness proves that by recomputing it
 * independently; this view reports it.
 *
 * ⚠ **P-4 and P-11 govern every sentence here.** Sender, receiver, timing and
 * frequency are public. Only amounts are hidden.
 */

import type { Snapshot } from "../../facility/src/loopback.ts";
import { canonicalEncZero } from "../../tools/observer-harness.ts";

export interface ObserverView {
  account: string;
  issuanceId: string;
  /**
   * Public and plaintext. Not hidden by anything.
   *
   * ⚠ **A DECIMAL STRING.** `MPTAmount` is a UINT64 and a JS `Number` rounds
   * anything over 2^53 — the issuer path was fixed for this (E-215) and this
   * one was missed, which is the fix-the-instance-not-the-phrase shape again
   * (`CLAIMS.md` E-220).
   */
  publicAmount: string;
  registered: boolean;
  holderEncryptionKey?: string;
  confidentialBalanceVersion: number;
  /** ⚠ The whole point of F-1. */
  spending: CiphertextView | null;
  inbox: CiphertextView | null;
  issuance: {
    issuer: string;
    /** ⚠ PLAINTEXT on the issuance object. The sum of every hidden position. */
    /** ⚠ A decimal string, for the same reason as `publicAmount`. */
    confidentialOutstandingAmount: string;
    hasIssuerKey: boolean;
    hasAuditorKey: boolean;
  };
  whatIsPublic: string[];
  whatIsHidden: string[];
}

export interface CiphertextView {
  ciphertext: string;
  /** True when the ciphertext equals the canonical encryption of zero. */
  publiclyKnownZero: boolean;
  note: string;
}

/** Everything on an observer page that is readable with no secret at all. */
export const WHAT_IS_PUBLIC: string[] = [
  "The account's address, and every account it has transacted with (P-4).",
  "When each transaction happened, and how often (P-11).",
  "The public MPT balance.",
  "The issuance's ConfidentialOutstandingAmount: the total of all confidential balances.",
  "The holder's encryption key, once registered.",
  "The ConfidentialBalanceVersion, which counts changes to the balance.",
  "Whether a confidential balance is exactly zero, when it holds the canonical encryption of zero. " +
    "New, merged and clawed-back balances do; a balance spent down to zero does not (F-1, E-211).",
  "Each tranche size, when a position is retired in tranches: the amount is plaintext on every ConvertBack (F-24).",
];

/**
 * ⚠ **The complete list, and it is two items.** Anything not here is public.
 */
export const WHAT_IS_HIDDEN: string[] = [
  "The value of a confidential balance that is not the canonical encryption of zero, even when " +
    "that value is zero (E-211).",
  "The amount of each ConfidentialMPTSend.",
  "Both are hidden from observers, not from the issuer (P-9). Nothing else here is hidden.",
];

/** ⚠ F-1, stated where it is true rather than in a footnote. */
export const CANONICAL_ZERO_NOTE =
  "Publicly known to be exactly zero. This is the canonical encryption of zero, which anyone can " +
  "recompute from the holder's public key, the account and the issuance, with no secret (CLAIMS.md F-1).";

/**
 * ⚠⚠ **THIS SAID "Non-zero, and the amount is hidden from an observer" AND THAT
 * WAS FALSE (`CLAIMS.md` E-211).**
 *
 * Measured on Devnet 2026-08-28: holder `rDYMkRMh7o7QfkNaoGvtd7TgFzmt12FEM8`
 * decrypts to **0** through the issuer mirror, while its
 * `ConfidentialBalanceSpending` does **not** match the canonical `EncZero`.
 * **A balance can be exactly zero and not publicly identifiable as zero** —
 * F-1's canonical form is used on first-time init, merge reset and clawback, so
 * a balance that reached zero by SPENDING is a homomorphic result: a randomised
 * encryption of zero that matches nothing.
 *
 * ⚠ `tools/observer-harness.ts:277` had the correct hedge — *"not the canonical
 * zero (some non-zero value, or never reset)"* — and this tier flattened it into
 * a flat claim. **The correct source was one import away.**
 */
export const NOT_CANONICAL_NOTE =
  "Not the canonical encryption of zero, so an observer cannot tell the value. That is not the " +
  "same as saying it is non-zero: a balance that reached zero by spending looks exactly like this. " +
  "The issuer can still decrypt it (P-9).";

function classify(ciphertext: string | undefined, encZero: string | null): CiphertextView | null {
  if (!ciphertext) return null;
  if (encZero && ciphertext.toUpperCase() === encZero) {
    return {
      ciphertext,
      publiclyKnownZero: true,
      note: CANONICAL_ZERO_NOTE,
    };
  }
  return {
    ciphertext,
    publiclyKnownZero: false,
    note: NOT_CANONICAL_NOTE,
  };
}

export async function observerView(snap: Snapshot, account: string, issuanceId: string): Promise<ObserverView> {
  const mpt = await snap.mptoken(account, issuanceId);
  const iss = await snap.issuance(issuanceId);
  return viewOf(account, issuanceId, mpt, iss);
}

/**
 * The view, from answers already read. ⚠ The hosted snapshot read a position,
 * then called `observerView`, which read it AGAIN; a second answer that said
 * "not found" rendered as zeros under the state `present` (`REVIEW-059` R1-8).
 * A caller that has read the position passes it here instead of reading twice.
 */
export function viewOf(account: string, issuanceId: string, mpt: any, iss: any): ObserverView {
  const key = mpt?.holderEncryptionKey;
  const encZero = key ? canonicalEncZero(key, account, issuanceId) : null;

  return {
    account,
    issuanceId,
    publicAmount: mpt?.publicAmountRaw ?? "0",
    registered: Boolean(key),
    holderEncryptionKey: key,
    confidentialBalanceVersion: mpt?.confidentialBalanceVersion ?? 0,
    spending: classify(mpt?.confidentialBalanceSpending, encZero),
    inbox: classify(mpt?.confidentialBalanceInbox, encZero),
    issuance: {
      issuer: iss.issuer,
      confidentialOutstandingAmount: iss.confidentialOutstandingAmountRaw,
      hasIssuerKey: Boolean(iss.issuerEncryptionKey),
      hasAuditorKey: Boolean(iss.auditorEncryptionKey),
    },
    whatIsPublic: WHAT_IS_PUBLIC,
    whatIsHidden: WHAT_IS_HIDDEN,
  };
}
/**
 * The capability matrix — architecture §6 step 8's spine.
 *
 * **What each key source can and cannot do, and why.** Every cell carries a
 * citation, and `web/api/capability.test.ts` checks that each citation
 * RESOLVES AND EXISTS — that the named file, register row, ADR or ruling is
 * there.
 *
 * ⚠⚠ **IT DOES NOT CHECK SEMANTIC SUPPORT, AND THIS COMMENT SAID IT DID UNTIL
 * 2026-09-04** (*"says what the cell claims"*, `CLAIMS.md` E-239). **A citation
 * to an existing but irrelevant file passes.** That is the exact shape this
 * project claims discipline against — a gate asserting more than it checks —
 * and it was found by an adversarial leg asked for the worst overclaim in the
 * repository.
 *
 * ⚠ **This is DERIVED from the signer's own types, not restated from a
 * handover table.** `KeySource` in `signer/src/onboarding.rs:274` has exactly
 * two variants — `DerivedFromMasterSeed` and `Standalone` — and an absent
 * `elgamal_key_source` reads as `standalone` rather than being upgraded
 * (`holder.rs:53`). The browser-wallet row is the one that does **not** exist
 * in the code, and saying so is the point of the row.
 *
 * ⚠ **`CLAIMS.md` P-4, P-9 and P-11 govern every sentence here.** This system
 * hides amounts. It does not hide who transacts, with whom, when or how often,
 * and no configuration makes a holder private from the issuer.
 */

export type Verdict = "yes" | "no" | "conditional" | "unverified";

export interface Capability {
  verdict: Verdict;
  /** One sentence. Rendered as-is; it is a disclosure surface. */
  detail: string;
  /** Where the claim comes from. A file, a ruling, or a `CLAIMS.md` row. */
  citation: string;
}

export interface KeySourceRow {
  id: string;
  label: string;
  /** The token an operator writes into a holder profile, or null if none exists. */
  profileToken: string | null;
  existsToday: { built: boolean; where: string };
  decryptOwnBalance: Capability;
  proveAndSign: Capability;
  publicTransactions: Capability;
  /** The ruled disclosure this source obliges, if any. */
  ruledDisclosure: string | null;
}

/**
 * ⚠ **`REVIEW-012` condition 2 is RULED, not optional**, and it is the reason
 * the derived rows carry a disclosure the standalone row does not. It must
 * reach the holder **before the seed is asked for**.
 */
export const MASTER_SEED_DISCLOSURE =
  "The master seed becomes a permanent decryption credential. Anyone who ever " +
  "holds it can decrypt every confidential balance this account has held, past " +
  "and future. XLS-96 has no key rotation and no key recovery (P-10), so this " +
  "cannot be undone.";

/**
 * ⚠ **Issue #10 is OPEN and no code discharges it.** The derivation is
 * specified in one repository document and is not a published standard.
 */
/**
 * ⚠ **A NAMED GAP, and it is deliberately NOT one of the reasons the wallet row
 * says no.** `CLAIMS.md` E-206: whether a given wallet build's bundled codec can
 * serialise the XLS-0096 types was **not determined** by the checks of 2026-08-28
 * — the page-side SDKs (`@crossmarkio/sdk@0.4.0`, `@gemwallet/api@3.8.0`,
 * `xumm@1.8.0`) carry no codec dependency at all, so serialisation happens
 * inside the extension, or for Xaman in its backend, and none of the three
 * page-side packages names the codec version (checked 2026-08-28, E-206).
 *
 * ⚠ **It is excluded from the reasoning on purpose.** It is a version fact an
 * upstream release can close at any time; the three reasons the row DOES give
 * are structural, so no codec release closes them, and only a wallet that held
 * the viewing key and produced the proofs itself would (`CLAIMS.md` E-330).
 * A demonstration resting
 * on this one would be falsified by a release note.
 */
export const WALLET_CODEC_GAP =
  "Not determined: whether a given wallet build can serialise XLS-0096 transactions. The " +
  "page-side SDKs examined on 2026-08-28 carry no codec, so serialisation happens inside the " +
  "extension, or for Xaman in its backend, and none of them names the codec version (CLAIMS.md " +
  "E-206). This is not why a wallet cannot hold a confidential position: the reasons above are " +
  "structural, and this one could change with any release.";

export const DERIVATION_NOT_PUBLISHED =
  "The derivation is specified only in this repository and is not a published " +
  "standard. A holder with their seed but without this repository cannot " +
  "reconstruct their key. Issue #10 tracks this.";
