/**
 * The custody page, a simulation.
 *
 * ⚠ Rendered by the local tier's route and by the hosted build's route alike,
 * so the page has one body and the two cannot drift (`ADR-0004`; `CLAIMS.md`
 * E-141's shape is a second copy of one text).
 *
 * The capability matrix's custody row is not repeated here: the two findings
 * say the same thing, and the matrix page carries the row.
 */
import { Panel } from "../components/Verdict";

export function CustodyView({ sim }: { sim: { flows: any[] } }) {
  // A caveat that several flows share is printed once, numbered, and each flow points at it.
  const counts = new Map<string, number>();
  for (const f of sim.flows) for (const c of f.caveats as string[]) counts.set(c, (counts.get(c) ?? 0) + 1);
  const notes = [...counts].filter(([, n]) => n > 1).map(([c]) => c);
  return (
    <>
      <Panel title="This is a simulation" tone="warn">
        <p className="text-sm leading-relaxed">
          No custody platform is integrated behind this page: no MPC, no HSM, no Harmonize. It
          models a key-source shape, a standalone ElGamal key plus an external signer, to show which
          flows survive and which break.
        </p>
      </Panel>

      <Panel title="Two findings">
        <div className="text-sm space-y-5">
          <div>
            <h3 className="font-semibold mb-1">1. The derived key does not work with custodial key management</h3>
            <p className="opacity-85 leading-relaxed">
              The ElGamal secret is derived from the master seed, and a custody platform never releases
              the seed. So the account cannot derive its own ElGamal key or read its own confidential
              balance outside the platform. A platform that ran the derivation inside its own boundary
              would change that; it would need the derivation published as a standard, and it is not
              (issue #10).
            </p>
            <p className="opacity-85 leading-relaxed mt-2">
              A standalone ElGamal key gets around this, but then something outside the custody
              platform holds a permanent decryption key. Who that should be is an open question in
              this project (CLAIMS.md E-117).
            </p>
          </div>
          <div>
            <h3 className="font-semibold mb-1">2. Approval policies cannot read a confidential amount</h3>
            <p className="opacity-85 leading-relaxed">
              An approval workflow that holds only public data sees a proof, not a value, so a rule
              like <em>&ldquo;two approvers above $1M&rdquo;</em> has nothing to check. This is
              structural, not an integration detail: confidentiality and amount-based approval
              policies pull against each other. The ways out each cost something: give the policy
              engine the ElGamal secret, and it becomes another party that reads every balance;
              accept the holder&rsquo;s statement of the amount, and the policy rests on the
              holder&rsquo;s honesty; or prove the bound in zero knowledge, which this project
              has neither built nor costed.
            </p>
          </div>
        </div>
      </Panel>

      <Panel title="Which flows survive, and which break">
        <p className="text-sm mb-2 opacity-80">
          Each flow lists what it needs, and the verdict is computed from what a custody-shaped key
          source has.
        </p>
        {notes.map((c, i) => (
          <p key={c} className="text-sm mb-2 opacity-80">Note {i + 1}: {c}</p>
        ))}
        <div className="mb-4" />
        <div className="space-y-3">
          {sim.flows.map((f: any) => (
            // ⚠ A class, not a `style` attribute: the hosted build's Content
            // Security Policy blocks inline style attributes (ADR-0004).
            <div key={f.id} className={`border-l-2 pl-3 ${f.survives ? "border-[var(--color-public)]" : "border-[var(--color-warn)]"}`}>
              <div className="text-sm font-medium">
                {f.survives ? "survives" : "breaks"}: {f.label}
                {notes.map((c, i) => (f.caveats.includes(c) ? <span key={c} className="font-normal opacity-70"> (note {i + 1})</span> : null))}
              </div>
              {f.blockedBy.map((b: any) => (
                <p key={b.requirement} className="text-xs opacity-80 mt-1">{b.because}</p>
              ))}
              {f.caveats.filter((c: string) => !notes.includes(c)).map((c: string) => (
                <p key={c} className="text-xs opacity-70 mt-1">{c}</p>
              ))}
            </div>
          ))}
        </div>
      </Panel>
    </>
  );
}
# A confidential receivables facility on the XRPL — how it actually works

**Date:** 2026-08-20, **revised twice on 2026-08-25 and once on 2026-08-28**
(§5's zero-balance nuance for `CLAIMS.md` **E-211**, §6's build table for the
web tier, and the amendment reading re-dated) — §3 step 4 and §4's table
for `CLAIMS.md` **F-26** (a second false disclosure sentence in this document),
§3 step 9 and §4's table for **F-24**, and §6's build table after architecture
§6 step 7 was **built and executed**. **Devnet only.** Written as the reference
an app, an explainer or a post would be built *from* — not as any of those.

⚠ **The facility now runs, and the row in §6 carries a qualification that must
travel with it**: the run was **not** under `CLAIMS.md` B-15's step-7
conditions, because the ledger source was remote under an amended ruling
(E-151). It is evidence that the transactions are well-formed and that rippled
accepts them. It is **not** evidence about the loopback obligation.

> ## ⚠ Read this box before reusing anything below
>
> **This has not been through the project's publishing protocol.** That protocol
> is deliberately not part of a coding session, and nothing here is cleared for
> external use. Every capability sentence below is written against
> `CLAIMS.md`'s standing prohibitions, and the register is the authority if the
> two ever disagree.
>
> **The one sentence that must survive any rewrite:** this design hides
> **amounts**. It does not hide **who** transacts, **with whom**, **when**, or
> **how often**, and the issuer can decrypt every balance by construction. An
> explainer that leaves that out is not a simplification of this document; it is
> a different and false claim.

---

## 1. The commercial problem, in one paragraph

A large buyer's suppliers want to be paid early; a bank will advance against the
buyer's payables. That is receivables financing, and the numbers in it are
commercially sensitive in a specific way: **a supplier's drawdown level tells you
their cash position.** Put that on a public ledger in plaintext and you have
published every supplier's working-capital stress to their competitors and to
the buyer's other counterparties. Put it on a private ledger and you have given
up most of what a shared ledger was for.

**XLS-0096 Confidential MPTs are an attempt at the middle**: amounts encrypted
on a public chain, with a decryption path reserved for the parties who are
accountable for the book.

## 2. The shape

Three roles, **one issuance**, and every participant holds a confidential
balance in it.

```
      ┌────────────────────────┐
      │  Funding bank          │  issuer of the MPT
      │  · IssuerEncryptionKey │  ── can decrypt EVERY balance, always
      │  · AuditorEncryptionKey│  ── optional second party, same power
      └───────────┬────────────┘
                  │ funds publicly, once
                  ▼
      ┌────────────────────────┐
      │  Dedicated Account     │  an ORDINARY XRPL account
      │  holds the float,      │  (not an XLS-65 Vault — see §7)
      │  confidentially        │
      └───────────┬────────────┘
                  │  ConfidentialMPTSend — amounts hidden
        ┌─────────┼─────────┬─────────┐
        ▼         ▼         ▼         ▼
     supplier  supplier  supplier   ...        each with its own
        A         B         C                  HolderEncryptionKey
```

⚠ **"Dedicated Account", never "vault".** An XLS-65 `Vault` is a different
ledger object, it *cannot* convert to a confidential balance, and the word has
already nearly derailed one reading of the design.

## 3. The walkthrough

A worked example: a bank runs a **£50m** facility for **30 suppliers** of one
large buyer.

### Setup — public, and deliberately so

**Step 1 — the bank creates the issuance.** A Multi-Purpose Token flagged to
hold confidential balances. It sets:

- **`IssuerEncryptionKey`** — mandatory. The bank can decrypt every holder's
  balance. **No configuration removes this**, and any design that assumes
  otherwise is wrong about the protocol.
- **`AuditorEncryptionKey`** — optional. A trustee or supervisor with the same
  visibility.
- **`sfDomainID`** — gates the issuance to a permissioned domain, so only
  credentialled accounts can hold it.

⚠ **`TransferFee` must be zero on a confidential issuance.** Fees and interest
settle off-ledger or as separate confidential transfers. That is a protocol
constraint, not a design choice, and it shapes everything downstream.

**Step 2 — the bank creates a Dedicated Account.** An ordinary XRPL account.
It cannot be the issuer account: **an issuer cannot convert its own issuance
into a confidential balance.** The ledger refuses it outright — this is the
reason the design needs a separate account to hold the float, not a stylistic
preference.

**Step 3 — the bank funds it publicly** with the whole £50m float, as plaintext
MPT.

### The one plaintext moment

**Step 4 — one `ConfidentialMPTConvert`.** This does two things at once: it
registers the Dedicated Account's `HolderEncryptionKey`, and it moves the entire
float into confidentiality.

**The amount is public at this step, and that is the intent.** Facility size is
what the market and the funders are entitled to see.

⚠ **~~After this, no plaintext amount appears again until the facility is
retired or resized.~~ That sentence was FALSE and is struck (2026-08-25,
`CLAIMS.md` F-26).** Step 5 publishes two plaintext amounts **per supplier** —
the bank's `Payment` of the enrolment token, and the supplier's own
`ConfidentialMPTConvert`, whose `MPTAmount` is a plaintext field exactly as step
4's and step 9's are. On a thirty-supplier facility that is sixty plaintext
amounts between the float conversion and the first drawdown.

**What is true, and it is what the design actually buys:** no **drawdown** and
no **repayment** publishes an amount, and those are the numbers that carry the
commercial signal. The plaintext amounts are the enrolment tokens, which are
deliberately nominal and identical, and the facility-level ones — its size, and
each tranche of its retirement.

⚠ This is the **second** false disclosure sentence found in this document
(F-24 was the first), and like F-24 it survived the document's own
`claims-check`. Both were about *which* amounts are plaintext. A sentence of the
form *"no plaintext amount appears after X"* should be assumed wrong until every
transaction type between X and the end has been listed.

### Enrolling a supplier

**Step 5.** The bank pays the supplier a token amount of the MPT publicly, and
the supplier runs its own `ConfidentialMPTConvert` — which is what registers
their `HolderEncryptionKey`.

⚠ **Registration happens exactly once and cannot be repeated.** The protocol
requires the key when initialising and forbids it afterwards. A supplier with no
registered key **cannot receive a confidential amount at all** — there is no key
to encrypt to, so the transaction cannot be built, let alone rejected.

### Running the facility

**Step 6 — drawdown.** Dedicated Account → supplier, one `ConfidentialMPTSend`.
The amount is hidden. Received value lands in the supplier's **inbox**; a
`ConfidentialMPTMergeInbox` moves it into their spendable balance.

**Step 7 — repayment.** Supplier → Dedicated Account, the same transaction type,
amount hidden.

**Step 8 — several funders?** They are confidential holders in the same
issuance. The bank sends each their position confidentially, and yield as
further confidential transfers. **No shares and no share price** — positions are
denominated directly, which is a large part of why a `Vault` buys nothing here
(§7).

### Wind-down

**Step 9 — `ConfidentialMPTConvertBack`** when the facility retires or resizes.

⚠ **Public: each ConvertBack's OWN amount, in the clear — not merely the
aggregate change.** ~~Public: the aggregate change.~~ **Corrected 2026-08-25
(`CLAIMS.md` F-24), and the earlier sentence would have misled a reader in a way
that matters:** `MPTAmount` is a plaintext field on every individual
`ConfidentialMPTConvertBack` (XLS-0096 §10.3), so retiring a facility in tranches
publishes **every tranche size**, not just the net movement in
`sfConfidentialOutstandingAmount`. Two ConvertBacks executed on 2026-08-24 read
95 and 191 straight off their validated transactions.

This is symmetric with step 4: **`Convert` and `ConvertBack` are the two
transaction types in this design that publish an amount, and both publish it per
transaction.** It is the specification's own intent — §10.7's worked example
turns on it — and not a defect.

---

## 4. Who sees what

| | Facility size | Who is enrolled | Who drew, when, how often | **Amounts** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Anyone with a block explorer | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ for drawdowns and repayments — **but every `Convert` amount, every `ConvertBack` amount and every plaintext `Payment` is public** (F-24, F-26). That is the facility's size, each retirement tranche, **and both plaintext amounts of every enrolment** |
| A supplier | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | only their own |
| **The bank (issuer)** | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | **✅ everyone's** |
| **An auditor, if configured** | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | **✅ everyone's** |

**Three tiers of disclosure, and none of them requires publishing a position:**

1. **Public** — `sfConfidentialOutstandingAmount` on the issuance keeps the
   aggregate visible, alongside the transaction graph.
2. **The arranger** — the mandatory issuer mirror.
3. **A supervisor or trustee** — the optional auditor key.

⚠ **This is a statement about who can decrypt what. It is not a compliance
claim.** Nothing here asserts that the structure satisfies any named
jurisdiction's requirements.

## 5. ⚠ What this does NOT hide

The most important section in this document, and the one most likely to be cut
from a shorter version.

**It hides the amount of a confidential *transfer*, and balances. It hides nothing else** — and ⚠ **converting in or out publishes the amount in plaintext, every time.** Only a `Send` hides one.

- **The counterparty set is public.** Every supplier on the facility is
  identifiable.
- **Timing and frequency are public.** Every drawdown and repayment is a visible
  event with a timestamp.
- **Therefore drawdown *frequency* leaks.** A supplier moving from monthly to
  weekly draws is **legible as distress without a single amount being known** —
  which is a substantial part of the commercial signal the design set out to
  protect.
- **No holder is private from the issuer.** By construction, permanently.

**The proposed mitigation for the frequency leak — scheduled cover traffic, so
the Dedicated Account sends to every supplier on a fixed cadence whether or not
value moves — is `Proposed` and has been reviewed by nobody.** It rests on
zero-amount sends being indistinguishable from real ones, which this project has
tested three ways and **has not established**. Surviving three attempts to break
a claim is not a proof of it. Do not present cover traffic as a feature.

⚠ **One thing this section did NOT say, added 2026-08-28 (`CLAIMS.md` E-211):**
**a balance can be exactly zero and not publicly identifiable as zero.** F-1's
canonical `EncZero` is used on first-time init, merge reset and clawback — so a
freshly-enrolled supplier's balance **is** publicly known to be zero, while a
supplier who has drawn and fully repaid holds a *randomised* encryption of zero
that matches nothing. **The two look completely different to an observer and
are the same number.** ⚠ The converse is the trap: **a ciphertext that does not
match the canonical zero is NOT evidence of a non-zero balance**, and the web
tier shipped a sentence saying it was.

**And two protocol constraints that shape any product built on this:**

- **Keys cannot be rotated or recovered.** A lost key is a permanently lost
  balance; a compromised key is permanently compromised.
- **Clawback, where the issuance enables it, burns the holder's *entire*
  balance.** It cannot model partial recovery. It is a sanctions instrument,
  not a repayment mechanism. (It is available only if the issuance carries
  `lsfMPTCanClawback`; without that flag the attempt is refused — a
  qualification `claims-check` caught missing here.)

## 6. What is actually built, as of 2026-08-25

Honest status, because an explainer that blurs *demonstrated* into *designed* is
the failure this project exists to avoid.

| | Status |
|---|---|
| Issuance, enrolment, Convert, Send, MergeInbox on Devnet | **Executed.** Real transactions, validated |
| A local signer that generates the proofs and signs | **Built.** Reads the ledger itself, holds throwaway keys |
| `ConfidentialMPTConvertBack` (step 9) | ~~**NOT built.** Refused by name rather than guessed at~~ — **BUILT AND EXECUTED 2026-08-24.** Two on Devnet under a **derived** ElGamal key, both validated, with all four of XLS-0096 §10.5's state changes checked against ledger state before and after rather than inferred from `tesSUCCESS` (`CLAIMS.md` E-131). The field set is §10.3's, read at source |
| Real key generation and onboarding | **Built and executed 2026-08-20.** The ElGamal key is derived from the master seed under the ruled construction, and a Devnet account holds a position under one (`CLAIMS.md` E-118). ⚠ **The derivation is not a published standard**, so a holder with their seed and not this repository cannot reconstruct their key — that is the benefit the ruling trades for, and it is open (issue #10) |
| The facility as application logic | **BUILT AND EXECUTED 2026-08-25** — 28 validated facility transactions on Devnet: the Dedicated Account, the single conversion, three supplier enrolments, three drawdowns, two repayments, the merges and retirement in two tranches. Every party's decrypted balance matches the off-ledger book, and the issuance's plaintext `ConfidentialOutstandingAmount` is exactly the sum of the hidden positions. ⚠ **NOT under `CLAIMS.md` B-15's step-7 conditions**: the ledger source was **remote**, under the owner's amendment (E-151), because a loopback node proved unaffordable on the available hardware (E-148). `fail_hard` was in force on every facility **movement**; loopback was not. ⚠ **Not "every submission"** — the run's fail-hard control submits one arm **without** the flag, deliberately, and B-15's Part C says *"whatever submits"* with no control-arm exception, so the run record's `underB15Conditions` is `false` for that reason as well as for the remote source (`REVIEW-023` round two). **Nothing in this run is evidence about the loopback obligation** |
| An observer harness showing what the public sees | **Built**, and it is what §5's claims are checked against |
| A read-only web tier rendering all of the above | **Built and exercised 2026-08-28** — architecture §6 step 8, `web/`. It renders the observer view, the issuer view and a capability matrix. ⚠ **It never signs and holds no key**; signing stays in the CLI signer. ⚠ **It has had NO adversarial review** — `ADR-0003` is `Proposed`. See `WEB-TIER-DEMO-SCRIPT-2026-08-28.md` |

⚠ **Amendment status is a reading, not knowledge.** `ConfidentialTransfer`,
`PermissionedDomains` and `SingleAssetVault` were observed `ENABLED` on Devnet
at ~~**2026-08-24T14:42:31Z**~~ **2026-08-28T04:45:40.875Z**, alongside
`MPTokensV1`, `DynamicMPT` and `LendingProtocol`. **That reading expires.**
Re-check before relying on it.
(`SingleAssetVault` is required to *write* `sfDomainID` even though no `Vault`
object is ever created.)

⚠ **Everything here runs on Devnet with throwaway keys.** Nothing in this
project is secure, audited, non-custodial or production-ready, and nothing
should ever hold value.

## 7. Two designs that were considered and rejected

**Building on XLS-66, the lending protocol.** Rejected. Five independent
blockers, any one sufficient: a vault share token cannot be made confidential;
vault and broker pseudo-accounts can neither convert into nor receive a
confidential balance, because they cannot sign; every loan and vault value is a
plaintext ledger field; there are no revolving drawdowns, only discrete public
`Loan` objects; and value must leave confidentiality before it can enter a vault
at all. Evidenced on Devnet, not read from a specification.

**A hybrid — an XLS-65 `Vault` for the funding side, confidential transfers for
drawdowns.** Rejected, and the reason is worth stating because it is not the
obvious one. **A `Vault`'s share token cannot be confidential**, so the hybrid
forces a hard choice: *native share accounting* **or** *confidential investor
positions*, never both. The accounting is arithmetic an application can do; the
confidential position is the thing that does not exist elsewhere. And since
`TransferFee` is forced to zero and interest settles off-ledger anyway, the
share-price arithmetic was never going on-chain to begin with.

**A consequence worth noticing:** because every participant is a confidential
holder in one issuance, **the number of funders stops mattering to the
architecture.** One bank or twenty investors — the design does not fork.

Both rejections are recorded, with their evidence and their reversal conditions,
in `docs/decisions/ADR-0002-facility-as-application-logic.md`.

## 8. Where the claims in this document come from

Every capability statement above traces to `CLAIMS.md`, which grades each claim
by whether it was **executed**, **read at source**, or **inferred** — and which
carries a list of things this project may never claim. If a sentence here reads
as stronger than the register, the register wins and the sentence is a defect.

**Reusing this material:** the §5 disclosures are not optional context. A
version of this that keeps the walkthrough and drops "identities and timing are
public, the issuer decrypts everything, and the frequency leak is real and
unmitigated" is not a shorter explainer. It is a claim this project has spent
its whole life refusing to make.
74:| E-33 | **Generating a `ConfidentialMPTSend` proof requires the sender's ElGamal secret key `sk_A` as a witness.** `sk_A` appears in the witness `w = (m, r, b, ρ, sk_A)`; balance linkage is proved as `B₂ − b·G = sk_A·B₁` precisely because the historical randomness `r_b` is unavailable to the prover. 2-special soundness extracts `sk_A`; knowledge soundness holds in the ROM under DL. **ADR-0001's central premise is confirmed.** `ConvertBack` shares the relation; `Clawback` requires `sk_iss` instead | `cmpt-compact-sigma.pdf` §2.3, §2.4 Eq. 1, Thm 4.2 (Relations 5–6), Thm 4.4, Remark 4.5 — Dore & Cenk, RippleX Research, 2026-05-12. Read in full 2026-08-16 | A |
108:| E-58 | **The auditor view is executed, not inferred.** With an issuance carrying `sfAuditorEncryptionKey`, the auditor's ElGamal key decrypts a holder's `sfAuditorEncryptedBalance` to the exact plaintext balance, matching what the holder reads with their own key and what the issuer reads from the issuer mirror. Read 500 immediately after the Convert, then **re-derived independently as 475 after a 25-unit Send** — the three views track each other through a balance change, which is the stronger statement | Executed 2026-08-17 on issuance `00453A7DD67EB7E60002C33AAD59E7F544C136A8D30F5D52`. **Three parties decrypt the same balance: holder, issuer, auditor — verified twice, at two different balances.** Reinforces P-9 and P-4 — this is a confidentiality model with mandatory issuer visibility and optional third-party visibility, and neither is hideable from the holder's side | A |
168:| E-117 | ⚠ **Rejecting the XLS-65 `Vault` traded away a custody property, and ~~ADR-0002 does not say so~~ ADR-0002 has said so since `c34dcec` (E-256, E-295).** A `Vault`'s pseudo-account holds pooled assets **with no key** — nothing can move them outside the protocol's rules. The Dedicated Account that replaces it is an **ordinary XRPL account with an ordinary key**, so whoever holds that key can move the entire float. Rev 4 rejected the hybrid on blocker 1 (share tokens cannot be confidential) and that reasoning stands. ⚠ ~~*the comparison table counts only disclosure and accounting; it has no row for custody*~~ **— STRUCK 2026-09-09, and it was false from the commit that wrote this row.** `c34dcec` added **both** E-117 and the `Custody of the pooled float` row it says is missing, so the clause described a state its own commit had already ended and then read as live for twenty days. The real axis is sharper than the ADR states it: **confidential positions, or non-custodial pooling — one or the other, today.** This project picks confidentiality because that is its premise, and a design needing *"no single party can move the float"* would weigh it the other way | **Noticed 2026-08-20** while answering where a `Vault` *would* fit, which is a question the ADR never asks. ⚠ **Not a reversal of rev 4** — it is a cost rev 4 does not price. ⚠ **Both premises verified 2026-09-09, and the first is STRONGER than this row states it.** rippled 3.3.0 `src/libxrpl/ledger/helpers/AccountRootHelpers.cpp:565-604`, `createPseudoAccount`: `sfFlags` is set to `lsfDisableMaster \| lsfDefaultRipple \| lsfDepositAuth` at `:597`, and `sfSequence` to **0** under `featureSingleAssetVault` at `:588-593`, over the comment *"Pseudo-accounts can't submit transactions … an extra level of protection"*. So it is not merely that nobody holds a key: the master is disabled, deposit authorisation is on, and the sequence number cannot support a transaction. The second premise is `facility/src/facility.ts:129`, which runs `checkSeedControlsAddress` over `bank` and `dedicated` alike. ~~**What is live is the last clause and only it: the facility's float sits under one key and no review has asked who holds it**~~ **Discharged 2026-09-13 (E-294): `REVIEW-046` asked who holds it and found more holders than one key, and the owner ruled B-27, accepting the risk by name with a printed disclosure.** What was live was the last clause and only it — P-12 search, 2026-09-09, over ~~357~~ 204 files in `docs/reviews/` (E-292): the only `custod` matches inside `REVIEW-019`, `REVIEW-022` and `REVIEW-023` are boilerplate quotations of P-5 in ~~two REQUEST documents~~ three REQUEST documents and one leg (E-292), and no leg or adjudication in any of the three rounds weighs it. ⚠ **A decision request now exists** ~~and this clause is still live~~ (discharged 2026-09-13, below): `docs/decisions/DECISION-REQUEST-e117-custody-of-the-float-2026-09-11.md` (2026-09-11) states the question and prices the options, and it establishes that the seed confers a **second** power this row never names — permanent, unrotatable decryption of every per-supplier amount (E-266). A request is not a review, so nothing here is discharged. 197:| E-146 | **Architecture §6 step 7 is BUILT AND EXECUTED — `facility/`, the receivables facility as application logic.** ⚠ **Executed under E-151's amendment, so `underB15Conditions: false`** — the ledger source was remote and this run says nothing about the loopback obligation. **Reviewed in two rounds, three legs → E-150.** ~~and it is NOT EXECUTED~~ — **struck 2026-08-25.** The Dedicated Account, the single conversion, supplier enrolment, drawdowns, repayments, the balance book and retirement in tranches. What the build actually enforces, as distinct from what it describes: **`LoopbackLedger` is the only ledger read path** and refuses a non-loopback literal *before it connects* — ⚠ **unless the risk has been accepted by name** (E-151's hatch, which is how the executed run happened, and which this clause omitted until round two) — so a misconfigured facility cannot tell a remote host the shape of its reads on the way to failing; **`FailHardSubmitter` is the only submission path for a facility movement** and has no method that can omit `fail_hard`, with the method set pinned by a test so a new path cannot appear unnoticed — ⚠ **and `run-facility.ts` §1's fail-hard control makes exactly one submission outside it, without the flag, on every run.** That is the point of a control and it is also why `underB15Conditions` is `false` on every run this driver makes; **`FacilityBook`** is TASK ZERO §9's R-5 balance-tracking requirement, and reports search **widths** rather than seconds because P-8 forbids a benchmark number without hardware, parameter set and methodology. ⚠ **The loopback check is STRICTER than the signer's**: a numeric literal in `127.0.0.0/8` or `::1` only, because a name is resolved by something the process does not control — `REVIEW-016`'s two bypasses and round two's unterminated `[::1` are negative test inputs rather than prose. ~~⚠ **NOT EXECUTED means no facility transaction has been submitted to any ledger**, and every capability sentence about it is therefore about code and tests, not about rippled's answer~~ — **struck 2026-08-26.** ⚠ **This sentence sat at the end of the row whose own heading says BUILT AND EXECUTED**, and survived `REVIEW-023` C-6's sweep of nine denials because the sweep worked from a list rather than from a re-read. Round two's Gemini leg graded it HIGH and was right to: a register row that contradicts itself is worse than a wrong one, because both readings are citable. **28 validated facility transactions, 2026-08-25** — see **B-18** | **2026-08-25.** **81 tests** (`bun test facility tools`, 2026-08-26) ⚠ **not the 63 this row claimed until round two** — 63 was already stale at `REVIEW-023`'s own tree, which runs **77**, a real type-check (`bunx tsc --noEmit` — added because `bun build` is not one, E-145), `tools/check-loopback.ts` PASS, and `tools/check-blinding-sources.ts` extended to walk `facility/` and still reporting 5 call sites, **with its coverage of the new tree spot-checked by planting a call and watching it fail**. ⚠ **A demo of the happy path is not evidence of confidentiality**, and the unit tests drive fake nodes — what a fake node proves is that the code does what the test says. /bin/zsh -lc 'cat web/hosted/browser-check.ts' in $USER_TMP/T/r061a.NchKmT/repo
 succeeded in 47ms:
/**
 * The hosted demo in a real browser, under its real policy: step 4 of
 * `web/hosted/README.md`'s release procedure (`ADR-0004`).
 *
 *   bun run web/hosted/browser-check.ts <output root>
 *
 * Serves `<output root>/.vercel/output` the way its `config.json` says (every
 * response carries the first route's headers, the page routes rewrite to their
 * `index.html`, everything else is a file or a 404), opens each page in a
 * headless Chrome with a fresh profile, and FAILS on:
 *
 *   - any Content Security Policy violation, which is how a hash the gate
 *     computed and a script the browser runs would disagree;
 *   - any request to anything but the page's own origin;
 *   - any uncaught exception, or any console error other than the expected
 *     404 for a missing verdict file, whether the browser logged it or the
 *     page's own script did (`console.error`), on every page AND after the
 *     final client-side navigation (`REVIEW-059` R1-5: both were missed);
 *   - a ledger page whose D-H2 wording is missing from the rendered text, or
 *     present but not visible: every element of it that carries text must pass
 *     the browser's own `checkVisibility` with opacity and visibility, have a
 *     box, keep an effective opacity of at least 0.5 through its ancestors and
 *     be at least 8px, on the page and again after the client navigation
 *     (`REVIEW-059` R1-2, and R2-7: transparent text counted as rendered). ⚠
 *     Text coloured like its background, moved off screen or covered by
 *     another element is not detected;
 *   - a page whose script never ran, read from the footer's verdict slot,
 *     which only the page's own script changes;
 *   - a rendered link into the repository at any commit but the one
 *     `build-manifest.json` names, including one a script assembled after
 *     load, which no reading of the files finds (`REVIEW-060` A-5);
 *   - a client-side navigation that loads a new document or does not render.
 *
 * ⚠ **This observes ONE browser on ONE run.** It is evidence about what the
 * pages request, not a proof about every browser, and it says nothing about
 * what the host adds after upload (`ADR-0004`; the gate's own "does not check"
 * list, items 1 and 2).
 */

import { cpSync, existsSync, mkdtempSync, readFileSync, readdirSync, rmSync, statSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { tmpdir } from "node:os";
import { join } from "node:path";
import { HOSTED_PAGES } from "./pages.ts";
import { ADMITTED_URLS, LEDGER_PAGES, repoLinkCommit } from "../../tools/check-web-hosted.ts";
import { DH2_WORDING } from "../app/src/hosted/wording.ts";

const CHROME = process.env.WCONF_CHROME ?? "/Applications/Google Chrome.app/Contents/MacOS/Google Chrome";

const TYPES: Record<string, string> = {
  html: "text/html; charset=utf-8", js: "text/javascript; charset=utf-8", css: "text/css; charset=utf-8",
  json: "application/json", txt: "text/plain; charset=utf-8",
};

/** A local stand-in for the host, from the output's own config.json. */
export function serveOutput(root: string) {
  const base = join(root, ".vercel", "output");
  const config = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(base, "config.json"), "utf8"));
  // ⚠ This server models one shape: headers on the first route only, file
  // rewrites, and the filesystem. A configuration outside it would be served
  // as if it were that shape, so it is refused instead (`REVIEW-059` R1-5).
  for (const [i, r] of (config.routes ?? []).entries()) {
    const keys = Object.keys(r).filter((k) => !["src", "dest", "headers", "continue", "handle"].includes(k));
    if (keys.length || (i > 0 && r.headers !== undefined) || (r.handle !== undefined && r.handle !== "filesystem")) {
      throw new Error(`browser-check cannot model route ${i} (${JSON.stringify(r).slice(0, 120)}); the gate refuses it too`);
    }
  }
  const headers: Record<string, string> = config.routes[0].headers;
  const rewrites: [RegExp, string][] = config.routes.filter((r: any) => r.dest).map((r: any) => [new RegExp(r.src), r.dest]);
  return Bun.serve({
    hostname: "127.0.0.1",
    port: 0,
    fetch(req) {
      let path = new URL(req.url).pathname;
      for (const [re, dest] of rewrites) if (re.test(path)) { path = dest; break; }
      if (path === "/") path = "/index.html";
      const file = join(base, "static", path);
      if (!file.startsWith(join(base, "static")) || !existsSync(file) || !statSync(file).isFile()) return new Response("not found", { status: 404, headers });
      const ext = file.slice(file.lastIndexOf(".") + 1);
      return new Response(Bun.file(file), { headers: { ...headers, "Content-Type": TYPES[ext] ?? "application/octet-stream" } });
    },
  });
}

/** A minimal DevTools-protocol client over one WebSocket. */
class Cdp {
  private id = 0;
  private waiting = new Map<number, (v: any) => void>();
  readonly events: { method: string; params: any; sessionId?: string }[] = [];
  private constructor(private ws: WebSocket) {
    ws.onmessage = (m) => {
      const msg = JSON.parse(String(m.data));
      if (msg.id !== undefined) this.waiting.get(msg.id)?.(msg);
      else this.events.push(msg);
    };
  }
  static async connect(url: string): Promise<Cdp> {
    const ws = new WebSocket(url);
    await new Promise((ok, no) => { ws.onopen = ok; ws.onerror = no; });
    return new Cdp(ws);
  }
  async send(method: string, params: any = {}, sessionId?: string): Promise<any> {
    const id = ++this.id;
    const reply = new Promise<any>((ok) => this.waiting.set(id, ok));
    this.ws.send(JSON.stringify({ id, method, params, sessionId }));
    const r = await reply;
    if (r.error) throw new Error(`${method}: ${JSON.stringify(r.error)}`);
    return r.result;
  }
  close() { this.ws.close(); }
}

const sleep = (ms: number) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));

export async function browserCheck(root: string): Promise<{ problems: string[]; pages: number; requests: number; footer: string | null }> {
  const server = serveOutput(root);
  const origin = `http://127.0.0.1:${server.port}`;
  const profile = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), "wconf-browser-check-"));
  const chrome = Bun.spawn([CHROME, "--headless=new", "--remote-debugging-port=0", `--user-data-dir=${profile}`,
    "--no-first-run", "--no-default-browser-check", "--disable-extensions", "--disable-background-networking",
    "--disable-component-update", "--disable-sync", "about:blank"], { stdout: "ignore", stderr: "ignore" });
  const problems: string[] = [];
  let requests = 0;
  let footer: string | null = null;
  // The commit every repository link must name. A missing manifest is a refusal, not a skipped check.
  const manifest = join(root, "build-manifest.json");
  const commit: string | null = existsSync(manifest) ? JSON.parse(readFileSync(manifest, "utf8")).commit ?? null : null;
  if (!commit || !/^[0-9a-f]{40}$/.test(commit)) problems.push("no build commit in build-manifest.json, so repository links cannot be checked");
  try {
    let port = "";
    for (let i = 0; i < 100 && !port; i++) {
      const f = join(profile, "DevToolsActivePort");
      if (existsSync(f)) port = readFileSync(f, "utf8").split("\n")[0];
      else await sleep(100);
    }
    if (!port) throw new Error("Chrome did not open a DevTools port");
    const version = await (await fetch(`http://127.0.0.1:${port}/json/version`)).json();
    const cdp = await Cdp.connect(version.webSocketDebuggerUrl);
    const { targetId } = await cdp.send("Target.createTarget", { url: "about:blank" });
    const { sessionId } = await cdp.send("Target.attachToTarget", { targetId, flatten: true });
    const s = (m: string, p: any = {}) => cdp.send(m, p, sessionId);
    for (const d of ["Page", "Network", "Runtime", "Log"]) await s(`${d}.enable`);
    // Runs before the page's own scripts, and is not itself subject to the page's policy.
    await s("Page.addScriptToEvaluateOnNewDocument", {
      source: "window.__violations=[];document.addEventListener('securitypolicyviolation',e=>window.__violations.push(e.violatedDirective+' '+e.blockedURI));",
    });
    const evalJs = async (expr: string) => (await s("Runtime.evaluate", { expression: expr, returnByValue: true })).result.value;

    /**
     * Every check over one interval of events, the same for a page load and for
     * the client navigation. ⚠ The intervals are one continuous cursor over the
     * event stream: an event that arrived while an interval was being inspected
     * fell between two of them and was never read (`REVIEW-059` R3-8, measured).
     */
    let cursor = cdp.events.length;
    const inspect = async (label: string) => {
      const violations = await evalJs("window.__violations.splice(0)");
      const ev = cdp.events.slice(cursor);
      cursor = cdp.events.length;
      for (const e of ev.filter((e) => e.method === "Network.requestWillBeSent")) {
        requests++;
        const u = e.params.request.url as string;
        if (!u.startsWith(origin + "/")) problems.push(`${label}: a request to ${u}`);
      }
      for (const v of violations ?? []) problems.push(`${label}: CSP violation ${v}`);
      for (const e of ev.filter((e) => e.method === "Runtime.exceptionThrown")) problems.push(`${label}: exception ${e.params.exceptionDetails?.exception?.description ?? e.params.exceptionDetails?.text}`);
      for (const e of ev.filter((e) => e.method === "Log.entryAdded" && e.params.entry.level === "error")) {
        const t: string = e.params.entry.text ?? "";
        // Two same-origin 404s are expected: the verdict file before the gate
        // has written it, and the favicon a browser asks for unprompted.
        if (!(/404/.test(t) && /\/(gate-verdict\.json|favicon\.ico)$/.test(e.params.entry.url ?? ""))) problems.push(`${label}: console error ${t} (${e.params.entry.url ?? "no url"})`);
      }
      // ⚠ `Log.entryAdded` carries what the BROWSER logs; a page's own
      // `console.error` arrives only as `Runtime.consoleAPICalled`.
      for (const e of ev.filter((e) => e.method === "Runtime.consoleAPICalled" && ["error", "assert"].includes(e.params.type))) {
        problems.push(`${label}: console.${e.params.type} ${(e.params.args ?? []).map((a: any) => a.value ?? a.description ?? "").join(" ")}`);
      }
    };
    const squash = (t: string) => t.replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim();
    /** The D-H2 wording, present AND visible, on the page now loaded. */
    const dh2 = async (label: string) => {
      const shown = squash(await evalJs("document.body.innerText"));
      for (const para of DH2_WORDING) if (!shown.includes(squash(para))) problems.push(`${label}: the D-H2 sentence beginning ${JSON.stringify(para.slice(0, 40))} is not in the rendered text`);
      // ⚠ Measured on the TEXT, not on element boxes: an element under
      // `display:contents` has no box and its text is visible, and an element
      // whose box is fine can have its text clipped away (`REVIEW-059` R3-9).
      // The centre of the text's first rectangle is hit-tested after scrolling
      // it into view, which a clip, an ancestor's overflow or a covering
      // element defeats. Text coloured like its background is not detected.
      const seen = await evalJs(`(() => {
        const out = [];
        for (const root of document.querySelectorAll('[data-dh2-wording]')) {
          const walker = document.createTreeWalker(root, NodeFilter.SHOW_TEXT);
          for (let n = walker.nextNode(); n; n = walker.nextNode()) {
            if (!n.textContent.trim()) continue;
            const el = n.parentElement;
            let host = el;
            while (host && host.getClientRects().length === 0) host = host.parentElement;
            let opacity = 1;
            for (let e = el; e; e = e.parentElement) opacity *= Number(getComputedStyle(e).opacity);
            const range = document.createRange();
            range.selectNodeContents(n);
            const rects = () => [...range.getClientRects()].filter((r) => r.width > 0 && r.height > 0);
            let hit = false;
            if (rects().length) {
              window.scrollTo(0, Math.max(0, window.scrollY + rects()[0].top - window.innerHeight / 2));
              const r = rects()[0];
              const at = r ? document.elementFromPoint(r.left + r.width / 2, r.top + r.height / 2) : null;
              hit = !!at && (el.contains(at) || (host !== null && (host === at || host.contains(at))));
            }
            const visible = host ? host.checkVisibility({ opacityProperty: true, visibilityProperty: true, checkOpacity: true, checkVisibilityCSS: true }) : false;
            out.push({ text: n.textContent.trim().slice(0, 30), visible, opacity, rects: rects().length, hit, size: parseFloat(getComputedStyle(el).fontSize) });
          }
        }
        window.scrollTo(0, 0);
        return out;
      })()`);
      if (!seen?.length) problems.push(`${label}: no element carries the D-H2 wording`);
      for (const e of seen ?? []) {
        if (!e.visible || e.opacity < 0.5 || !e.rects || !e.hit || e.size < 8) problems.push(`${label}: D-H2 text ${JSON.stringify(e.text)} is not visible (checkVisibility ${e.visible}, opacity ${e.opacity.toFixed(2)}, ${e.rects} text rectangles, hit-test ${e.hit}, ${e.size}px)`);
      }
    };
    /**
     * Every URL attribute as the browser parsed it, resolved, and refused if it
     * names another origin the gate does not admit. ⚠ The gate decodes and
     * resolves too, and a browser's own parser is the reference it approximates
     * (`REVIEW-059` R3-3).
     */
    const attributes = async (label: string) => {
      const found = await evalJs(`(() => {
        const out = [];
        for (const el of document.querySelectorAll('[src],[href],[action],[formaction],[poster],[data],[srcset]')) {
          for (const a of ['src', 'href', 'action', 'formaction', 'poster', 'data', 'srcset']) {
            const v = el.getAttribute(a);
            if (v === null) continue;
            const candidates = a === 'srcset' ? v.split(',').map((c) => c.trim().split(/\\s+/)[0]).filter(Boolean) : [v];
            for (const c of candidates) {
              let u;
              try { u = new URL(c, location.href); } catch { out.push({ a, c, href: null }); continue; }
              if (u.origin !== location.origin) out.push({ a, c, href: u.href });
            }
          }
        }
        return out;
      })()`);
      for (const f of found ?? []) {
        if (f.href === null) problems.push(`${label}: an attribute URL does not parse: ${JSON.stringify(f.c)}`);
        else if (!ADMITTED_URLS.some(([re]) => re.test(f.href.replace(/\/$/, "")))) problems.push(`${label}: an attribute names another origin: ${f.href}`);
        else {
          const ref = repoLinkCommit(f.href);
          if (ref !== null && ref !== commit) problems.push(`${label}: a link into the repository names ${ref.slice(0, 12)}, not the build's commit: ${f.href}`);
        }
      }
    };

    for (const page of HOSTED_PAGES) {
      const before = cdp.events.length;
      await s("Page.navigate", { url: `${origin}/${page}` });
      for (let i = 0; i < 50; i++) { if (cdp.events.slice(before).some((e) => e.method === "Page.loadEventFired")) break; await sleep(100); }
      await sleep(1200);
      await inspect(`/${page}`);
      await attributes(`/${page}`);
      if ((LEDGER_PAGES as readonly string[]).includes(page)) await dh2(`/${page}`);
      const slot = await evalJs("document.querySelector('[data-gate-verdict]')?.textContent ?? null");
      if (page === "") footer = slot;
      // With a verdict file beside the pages, the footer must show the pass it records.
      if (slot !== null && existsSync(join(root, ".vercel/output/static/gate-verdict.json")) && !/^PASS, /.test(slot)) problems.push(`/${page}: a verdict file exists and the footer shows ${JSON.stringify(slot)}`);
      if (slot === null) problems.push(`/${page}: no gate-verdict slot`);
      else if (/^not loaded/.test(slot)) problems.push(`/${page}: the page's script never ran (the verdict slot still reads ${JSON.stringify(slot)})`);
    }

    // Client-side navigation: one link, no new document, the target renders,
    // and the interval after the click passes every check a page load does.
    // ⚠ This second load of the overview was cleared without being inspected,
    // so an error on it alone passed (`REVIEW-059` R2-6).
    await s("Page.navigate", { url: `${origin}/` });
    await sleep(1500);
    await inspect("/ loaded again, before the client navigation");
    const docsBefore = cdp.events.filter((e) => e.method === "Network.requestWillBeSent" && e.params.type === "Document").length;
    await evalJs("document.querySelector('a[href=\"/observer\"]').click()");
    await sleep(1200);
    await inspect("client navigation to /observer");
    const docsAfter = cdp.events.filter((e) => e.method === "Network.requestWillBeSent" && e.params.type === "Document").length;
    const path = await evalJs("location.pathname");
    const rendered = await evalJs("document.body.innerText.includes('Observer view: ')");
    if (path !== "/observer" || !rendered) problems.push(`client navigation to /observer did not render (path ${path}, rendered ${rendered})`);
    else {
      await dh2("client navigation to /observer");
      await attributes("client navigation to /observer");
    }
    if (docsAfter !== docsBefore) problems.push("client navigation loaded a new document; the router did not hydrate");
    // Whatever arrived during the last checks belongs to an interval too.
    await sleep(300);
    await inspect("after the last check");
    cdp.close();
  } finally {
    chrome.kill();
    server.stop(true);
    rmSync(profile, { recursive: true, force: true });
  }
  return { problems, pages: HOSTED_PAGES.length, requests, footer };
}

/**
 * The check's own controls, each a broken copy of a good output run through the
 * same `browserCheck()`. ⚠ The other-origin control removes the policy from its
 * copy, because with the policy in place the browser blocks the request and the
 * violation detector fires instead; this control is for the request detector.
 */
export async function selfTest(root: string): Promise<string[]> {
  const failed: string[] = [];
  const copy = () => {
    const d = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), "wconf-browser-self-test-"));
    cpSync(join(root, ".vercel"), join(d, ".vercel"), { recursive: true });
    cpSync(join(root, "build-manifest.json"), join(d, "build-manifest.json"));
    return d;
  };
  const dirs: string[] = [];
  const elsewhere = Bun.serve({ hostname: "127.0.0.1", port: 0, fetch: () => new Response("x") });
  try {
    const good = copy(); dirs.push(good);
    const g = await browserCheck(good);
    if (g.problems.length) failed.push(`the unbroken copy must pass, and reported: ${g.problems[0]}`);

    const other = copy(); dirs.push(other);
    const cfg = join(other, ".vercel/output/config.json");
    const c = JSON.parse(readFileSync(cfg, "utf8"));
    delete c.routes[0].headers["Content-Security-Policy"];
    writeFileSync(cfg, JSON.stringify(c));
    const idx = join(other, ".vercel/output/static/index.html");
    writeFileSync(idx, readFileSync(idx, "utf8").replace("</body>", `<img src="http://127.0.0.1:${elsewhere.port}/pixel.png"></body>`));
    if (!(await browserCheck(other)).problems.some((p) => p.includes(`a request to http://127.0.0.1:${elsewhere.port}/`))) failed.push("a request to another origin was not reported");

    // ⚠ The three cases `REVIEW-059` found passing: a page's own console
    // error, an exception after the client navigation, and the D-H2 wording
    // present in the HTML but hidden by the stylesheet.
    const asset = (d: string, re: RegExp) => join(d, ".vercel/output/static/assets", readdirSync(join(d, ".vercel/output/static/assets")).find((n) => re.test(n))!);
    // A script chunk every page loads, read from the pages rather than named.
    // ⚠ This chose `Verdict-*.js` until 2026-10-22, when a shared diagrams
    // module took that chunk's place and the self-test crashed on the name.
    const shared = (d: string) => {
      const sets = HOSTED_PAGES.map((p) => new Set([...readFileSync(join(d, ".vercel/output/static", p ? `${p}/index.html` : "index.html"), "utf8").matchAll(/\/assets\/([^"' )]+\.js)/g)].map((m) => m[1])));
      const common = [...sets[0]].filter((n) => sets.every((x) => x.has(n))).sort();
      if (!common.length) throw new Error("no script chunk is loaded by every page, so the self-test has nothing to modify");
      return join(d, ".vercel/output/static/assets", common[0]);
    };
    const logs = copy(); dirs.push(logs);
    const v1 = shared(logs);
    writeFileSync(v1, readFileSync(v1, "utf8") + '\nconsole.error("BROWSER_CHECK_CONTROL");\n');
    if (!(await browserCheck(logs)).problems.some((p) => p.includes("console.error BROWSER_CHECK_CONTROL"))) failed.push("a page's own console.error was not reported");
    const nav = copy(); dirs.push(nav);
    const v2 = shared(nav);
    writeFileSync(v2, readFileSync(v2, "utf8") + "\ndocument.addEventListener('click',e=>{if(e.target.closest('a[href=\"/observer\"]'))setTimeout(()=>{throw Error('BROWSER_CHECK_NAV')},0)});\n");
    if (!(await browserCheck(nav)).problems.some((p) => p.startsWith("client navigation to /observer: exception") && p.includes("BROWSER_CHECK_NAV"))) failed.push("an exception after the client navigation was not reported");
    const hidden = copy(); dirs.push(hidden);
    const css = asset(hidden, /\.css$/);
    writeFileSync(css, readFileSync(css, "utf8") + "\n[data-dh2-wording]{display:none}\n");
    if (!(await browserCheck(hidden)).problems.some((p) => p.startsWith("/observer: the D-H2 sentence"))) failed.push("D-H2 wording hidden by the stylesheet was not reported");
    // ⚠ Round two's examples and the next in each class (`REVIEW-059` R2-6, R2-7).
    for (const [name, rule] of [
      ["transparent", "[data-dh2-wording]{opacity:0}"],
      ["one paragraph nearly transparent", "[data-dh2-wording] p{opacity:0.05}"],
      ["one paragraph at zero size", "[data-dh2-wording] p{font-size:0}"],
    ] as const) {
      const d = copy(); dirs.push(d);
      const c = asset(d, /\.css$/);
      writeFileSync(c, readFileSync(c, "utf8") + `\n${rule}\n`);
      const r = await browserCheck(d);
      if (!r.problems.some((p) => p.startsWith("/observer: D-H2 text") || p.startsWith("/observer: the D-H2 sentence"))) failed.push(`D-H2 wording made invisible (${name}) was not reported`);
      if (!r.problems.some((p) => p.startsWith("client navigation to /observer: D-H2") || p.startsWith("client navigation to /observer: the D-H2"))) failed.push(`D-H2 wording made invisible (${name}) was not reported after the client navigation`);
    }
    // ⚠ Round three's cases (`REVIEW-059` R3-3, R3-8, R3-9), including one
    // that must NOT be reported: visible wording under `display:contents`.
    for (const [name, rule] of [
      ["clipped", "[data-dh2-wording]{clip-path:inset(50%)}"],
      ["clipped by an ancestor's overflow", "section:has(>[data-dh2-wording]){overflow:hidden;max-height:0}"],
    ] as const) {
      const d = copy(); dirs.push(d);
      const c = asset(d, /\.css$/);
      writeFileSync(c, readFileSync(c, "utf8") + `\n${rule}\n`);
      if (!(await browserCheck(d)).problems.some((p) => p.startsWith("/observer: D-H2 text"))) failed.push(`D-H2 wording ${name} was not reported`);
    }
    const contents = copy(); dirs.push(contents);
    const cc = asset(contents, /\.css$/);
    writeFileSync(cc, readFileSync(cc, "utf8") + "\n[data-dh2-wording], [data-dh2-wording] p {display:contents}\n");
    const rc = await browserCheck(contents);
    if (rc.problems.some((p) => p.includes("D-H2"))) failed.push(`visible wording under display:contents was reported: ${rc.problems.find((p) => p.includes("D-H2"))}`);
    const gap = copy(); dirs.push(gap);
    const v4 = shared(gap);
    writeFileSync(v4, readFileSync(v4, "utf8") + "\nif(location.pathname==='/'){const n=Number(sessionStorage.getItem('bc-gap')||0);sessionStorage.setItem('bc-gap',String(n+1));if(n===1){const o=window.__violations.splice;window.__violations.splice=function(...a){if(location.pathname==='/')console.error('BROWSER_CHECK_INSPECT_GAP');return o.apply(this,a)}}}\n");
    if (!(await browserCheck(gap)).problems.some((p) => p.includes("BROWSER_CHECK_INSPECT_GAP"))) failed.push("an error emitted during an inspection was not reported");
    const link = copy(); dirs.push(link);
    const li = join(link, ".vercel/output/static/index.html");
    writeFileSync(li, readFileSync(li, "utf8").replace("</body>", '<a href="&sol;&sol;tracker.example.net/x">x</a></body>'));
    if (!(await browserCheck(link)).problems.some((p) => p.startsWith("/: an attribute names another origin:") && p.includes("//tracker.example.net/x"))) failed.push("an encoded off-site link was not reported");

    // ⚠ A link a script assembles after load: no file holds the URL, so only the rendered page can show it.
    const built = copy(); dirs.push(built);
    const v5 = shared(built);
    writeFileSync(v5, readFileSync(v5, "utf8") + "\nsetTimeout(()=>{const a=document.querySelector('a[href*=\"/blob/\"]');if(a)a.setAttribute('href',['https://github.com','nandanito','xrpl-confidential-positions','blob','0'.repeat(40),'CLAIMS.md'].join('/'))},300);\n");
    if (!(await browserCheck(built)).problems.some((p) => p.startsWith("/how-it-works: a link into the repository names 000000000000"))) failed.push("a repository link assembled at run time at another commit was not reported");

    const second = copy(); dirs.push(second);
    const v3 = shared(second);
    writeFileSync(v3, readFileSync(v3, "utf8") + "\nif(location.pathname==='/'){const n=Number(sessionStorage.getItem('bc-home')||0);sessionStorage.setItem('bc-home',String(n+1));if(n===1)console.error('BROWSER_CHECK_SECOND_HOME')}\n");
    if (!(await browserCheck(second)).problems.some((p) => p.startsWith("/ loaded again") && p.includes("BROWSER_CHECK_SECOND_HOME"))) failed.push("an error on the second load of the overview was not reported");

    const dead = copy(); dirs.push(dead);
    const wal = join(dead, ".vercel/output/static/wallet/index.html");
    writeFileSync(wal, readFileSync(wal, "utf8").replace(/<script type="module"[^>]*><\/script>/, ""));
    if (!(await browserCheck(dead)).problems.some((p) => p.startsWith("/wallet: the page's script never ran"))) failed.push("a page whose script never ran was not reported");
  } finally {
    elsewhere.stop(true);
    for (const d of dirs) rmSync(d, { recursive: true, force: true });
  }
  return failed;
}

if (import.meta.main) {
  if (process.argv[2] === "--self-test") {
    const failed = await selfTest(process.argv[3] ?? "");
    for (const f of failed) console.log(`  CONTROL FAILED: ${f}`);
    console.log(`browser-check self-test: verdict=${failed.length ? "CONTROL-FAILED" : "PASS"} exit=${failed.length ? 2 : 0}`);
    process.exit(failed.length ? 2 : 0);
  }
  const root = process.argv[2];
  if (!root) throw new Error("usage: bun run web/hosted/browser-check.ts <output root>");
  const r = await browserCheck(root);
  console.log(`browser-check: ${r.pages} pages, ${r.requests} requests observed, all to the page's own origin unless listed below`);
  console.log(`  the overview's footer shows: ${r.footer}`);
  for (const p of r.problems) console.log(`  PROBLEM: ${p}`);
  console.log(`browser-check: verdict=${r.problems.length ? "FAIL" : "PASS"} exit=${r.problems.length ? 1 : 0}`);
  process.exit(r.problems.length ? 1 : 0);
}

exec
/bin/zsh -lc "command -v bun; cat review-input/rebuilt/config.json; ls -la review-input; sed -n '1,150p' tools/check-web-hosted.ts; rg -n 'export.*runChecks|8b|function runChecks|\\.claude|\\.wconf|homedir|WCONF' tools/check-web-hosted.ts; python3 - <<'PY'

for p in pages:
 t=(pathlib.Path('review-input/rebuilt/static')/p/'index.html').read_text()

 print('/'+p, sorted(a));print('scripts',re.findall(r'<script["'^>]*>'"',t))
print('common', sorted(set.intersection(*s)))

/**
 * check-web-hosted — architecture §6 step 9's gate: the HOSTED part of
 * `check-web-readonly`, run over the UPLOADED OUTPUT and its routing
 * configuration, never over the source (`ADR-0004`, "What phase 2 step 4's gate
 * must check").
 *
 *   bun run tools/check-web-readonly.ts --hosted <output root> --commit <sha>
 *
 * `<output root>` holds `.vercel/output/`. On a pass it writes
 * `.vercel/output/static/gate-verdict.json`, which the page's footer reads: a
 * digest of every other file, so a reader can tell what the gate passed.
 *
 * ⚠ **Gates in this repository have false-passed before they worked**
 * (`CLAIMS.md` E-165, E-182, E-226). So every run first copies the real output,
 * breaks the copy one way per control, and runs the SAME `runChecks()` over it;
 * each control names the check that must fire, and the unbroken copy must pass.
 * A control that fails stops the run with `CONTROL-FAILED`.
 *
 * ⚠ **It cannot check egress.** It reads files; a visitor's requests are not in
 * them (`ADR-0004`). `web/hosted/browser-check.ts` observes one browser's
 * requests, which is evidence about that browser and not a proof. The full list
 * of what this gate does not check is printed on every run.
 *
 * The last line is always `check-web-hosted: verdict=<WORD> exit=<N>`.
 */

import { createHash, randomBytes } from "node:crypto";

import { tmpdir } from "node:os";

// ------------------------------------------------------------------ the output

/** Every file under `.vercel/output`, by path relative to it. Symlinks refused. */
export function readOutput(root: string): Map<string, Buffer> {
  const base = join(root, ".vercel", "output");
  if (!existsSync(base)) throw new Error(`${base} does not exist`);
  const files = new Map<string, Buffer>();
  const walk = (rel: string) => {
    for (const name of readdirSync(join(base, rel)).sort()) {
      const r = rel ? `${rel}/${name}` : name;
      const st = lstatSync(join(base, r));
      if (st.isSymbolicLink()) throw new Error(`a symlink at ${r}: the uploader may follow it`);
      if (st.isDirectory()) walk(r);
      else if (st.isFile()) files.set(r, readFileSync(join(base, r)));
      else throw new Error(`${r} is not a regular file`);
    }
  };
  walk("");
  return files;
}

const VERDICT = "static/gate-verdict.json";
const text = (b: Buffer) => b.toString("utf8");
const sha256 = (b: Buffer | string) => createHash("sha256").update(b).digest("hex");

/**
 * React escapes these in text; the wording is compared after undoing it. ⚠
 * Numeric references are decoded too, because an attribute written as
 * `&#47;&#47;host` is `//host` to a browser and passed the URL check while it
 * was not (`REVIEW-059` R2-3).
 */
const NAMED: Record<string, string> = { quot: '"', lt: "<", gt: ">", amp: "&", apos: "'", sol: "/", colon: ":", period: ".", Tab: "\t", NewLine: "\n", nbsp: "\u00a0" };
export function decodeEntities(s: string): string {
  // ⚠ ONE pass. Sequential replacements decoded `&#x26;#47;` twice, and an
  // out-of-range reference threw (`REVIEW-059` R3-4). A numeric reference may
  // omit its semicolon, as a browser allows; an unknown name is left as written.
  return s.replace(/&(?:#[xX]([0-9a-fA-F]+);?|#(\d+);?|([A-Za-z]+);)/g, (m, hex, dec, name) => {
    if (hex !== undefined || dec !== undefined) {
      const cp = hex !== undefined ? parseInt(hex, 16) : Number(dec);
      return cp === 0 || cp > 0x10ffff || (cp >= 0xd800 && cp <= 0xdfff) ? "\ufffd" : String.fromCodePoint(cp);
    }
    return Object.hasOwn(NAMED, name) ? NAMED[name] : m;
  });
}

/**
 * The inner HTML of every element whose opening tag matches `open`, with nested
 * elements of the same name balanced. A regex to the first closing tag would
 * stop inside a nested `div`.
 */
export function elementsInner(html: string, tag: string, open: RegExp): string[] {
  const out: string[] = [];
  const any = new RegExp(`<(/?)${tag}\\b[^>]*>`, "gi");
  for (const m of html.matchAll(new RegExp(open.source, open.flags.includes("g") ? open.flags : open.flags + "g"))) {
    const start = m.index! + m[0].length;
    any.lastIndex = start;
    let depth = 1, end = -1;
    for (let t = any.exec(html); t; t = any.exec(html)) {
      depth += t[1] ? -1 : 1;
      if (depth === 0) { end = t.index; break; }
    }
    if (end >= 0) out.push(html.slice(start, end));
  }
  return out;
}

/** HTML read as text: block elements spaced, inline elements removed with nothing in their place. */
export function blockText(html: string): string {
  return decodeEntities(
    html.replace(/<!--[\s\S]*?-->/g, "")
      .replace(/<\/?(?:p|div|li|br|tr|td|th|h[1-6]|section|footer|header|ul|ol|table|thead|tbody|dl|dt|dd)\b[^>]*>/gi, " ")
      .replace(/<[^>]+>/g, ""),
  ).replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim();
}

/** A page's text as a reader sees it: no comments, no tags, entities undone. */
export function pageText(h: string): string {
  return decodeEntities(h.replace(/<script[\s\S]*?<\/script>/gi, " ").replace(/<!--[\s\S]*?-->/g, "").replace(/<[^>]+>/g, " ")).replace(/\s+/g, " ");
}

import type { ReactNode } from "react";

const STYLE: Record<string, string> = {
  yes: "bg-emerald-100 text-emerald-900 dark:bg-emerald-950 dark:text-emerald-200",
  no: "bg-red-100 text-red-900 dark:bg-red-950 dark:text-red-200",
  conditional: "bg-amber-100 text-amber-900 dark:bg-amber-950 dark:text-amber-200",
  unverified: "bg-slate-200 text-slate-800 dark:bg-slate-800 dark:text-slate-200",
};

const LABEL: Record<string, string> = {
  yes: "yes",
  no: "no",
  conditional: "conditional",
  unverified: "⚠ unverified",
};

export function Verdict({ v }: { v: string }) {
  return (
    <span className={`inline-block rounded px-2 py-0.5 text-xs font-semibold ${STYLE[v] ?? STYLE.unverified}`}>
      {LABEL[v] ?? v}
    </span>
  );
}

export function Panel({ title, tone = "plain", children }: { title: string; tone?: "plain" | "warn"; children: ReactNode }) {
  const border = tone === "warn" ? "border-[var(--color-warn)]" : "border-black/10 dark:border-white/15";
  return (
    <section className={`border ${border} rounded-lg p-5 mb-6`}>
      <h2 className="font-semibold mb-3">{title}</h2>
      {children}
    </section>
  );
}

/**
 * The wallet page: prove-and-contrast.
 *

/**
 * Prove-and-contrast. ⚠ The contrast is the lesson: a browser wallet cannot
 * hold a confidential position, and the reasons are structural rather than
 * missing features. ⚠ They are scoped to the wallets examined, not to every
 * possible wallet (`CLAIMS.md` E-329, E-330).
 */
export function WalletView({ rows, namedGaps }: { rows: any[]; namedGaps: { walletCodec: string } }) {
  const wallet = rows.find((r: any) => r.id === "browser-wallet");

  return (
    <>
      <Panel title="A browser wallet cannot hold a confidential position">
        <p className="text-sm leading-relaxed mb-4">
          The three wallets examined (Xaman, GemWallet and Crossmark, on 2026-08-28) cannot, and the
          reasons are structural, not missing features. A wallet that kept the ElGamal key inside the
          extension and offered a prove-and-sign operation could; none of those examined does.
        </p>
        <ol className="text-sm space-y-3 list-decimal pl-5">
          <li>
            A confidential transaction needs two secrets: the ElGamal secret, to decrypt the balance
            and build the proof, and the XRPL account key, to sign. A wallet supplies only the second.
          </li>
          <li>
            Under the ruled key generation, the ElGamal secret is derived from the master seed. A
            derived-key account has no other way to get it.
          </li>
          <li>
            A wallet extension exists in order not to release seed material. That is the product, not
            a gap in its API.
          </li>
        </ol>
        <p className="text-sm mt-4">
          So a page cannot assemble the transaction through these wallets. They are not refusing a
          permission; they hold nothing they are built to give a page.
        </p>
      </Panel>

      <Panel title="What a wallet can do">
        <div className="text-sm space-y-3">
          <div className="flex gap-3 items-start">
            <Verdict v={wallet.publicTransactions.verdict} />
            <div>
              <div className="font-medium">Ordinary XRPL transactions</div>
              <p className="opacity-80">{wallet.publicTransactions.detail}</p>
              <p className="text-xs opacity-50 mt-1">{wallet.publicTransactions.citation}</p>
            </div>
          </div>
          <div className="flex gap-3 items-start">
            <Verdict v="yes" />
            <div>
              <div className="font-medium">Prove control of an account</div>
              <p className="opacity-80">
                Enough to show that account&rsquo;s observer view, which anyone with the address can
                read anyway.
              </p>
            </div>
          </div>
        </div>
      </Panel>

      <Panel title="What this page does not claim" tone="warn">
        <p className="text-sm">{namedGaps.walletCodec}</p>
        <p className="text-xs opacity-60 mt-3">CLAIMS.md E-206.</p>
      </Panel>
    </>
  );
}

226:Docs: *"the version counter is reset to 0."* `ConfidentialMPTClawback.cpp:150` calls `incrementConfidentialVersion`. The documentation is wrong, and it is wrong in a way that breaks integrations: a wallet predicting a post-clawback version of 0 binds proofs to the wrong value and fails with `tecBAD_PROOF`.
232:**F-6 — Grade A, verified.** Spec §11 and §11.4 say clawed-back funds are burned; the closing sentence of §11.2 says they move to the issuer's public reserve. `ConfidentialMPTClawback.cpp:170–179` decrements both `sfConfidentialOutstandingAmount` and `sfOutstandingAmount` with no credit to any account. Funds burn. The §11.2 sentence is the error.
304:- **Clawback burns the holder's entire balance** and cannot model partial recovery. It is a sanctions instrument, not a repayment mechanism.
    return [send, merged];
  }

  /**
   * Step 9 — retire or resize. The Dedicated Account converts value back out.
   *
   * ⚠ **Each ConvertBack publishes its OWN amount in the clear** — `MPTAmount`
   * is a plaintext field of every one of them (XLS-0096 §10.3), so retiring in
   * tranches publishes **every tranche size**, not just the net movement in
   * `sfConfidentialOutstandingAmount`. That is `CLAIMS.md` **F-24**, and the
   * walkthrough said the opposite until 2026-08-25.
   */
  async retire(amount: number): Promise<Movement> {
    const have = this.book.expected(this.cfg.dedicated.address).spending;
    if (amount > have) {
      throw new FacilityRefusal(
        "REFUSED_INSUFFICIENT_CONFIDENTIAL_BALANCE",
        `retiring ${amount} exceeds the Dedicated Account's tracked balance of ${have}`,
      );
    }
    const m = await this.confidential(this.cfg.dedicated, {
      operation: "ConfidentialMPTConvertBack",
      amount,
      what: `retire ${amount}`,
      amountIsPublic: true,
    });
    this.book.record({
      kind: "convert-back",
      account: this.cfg.dedicated.address,
      amount,
      hash: m.hash,
    });
    return m;
  }

  /**
   * Every confidential transaction: signed by `signer/`, submitted from here
   * with `tapFailHard`, and **not recorded in the book until it validates**.
   */
  private async confidential(
    p: Party,

export function runChecks(files: Map<string, Buffer>, want: Expect): Finding[] {
  const f: Finding[] = [];
  const add = (check: string, detail: string) => f.push({ check, detail });
  const paths = [...files.keys()].filter((p) => p !== VERDICT);
  const html = new Map(paths.filter((p) => p.endsWith(".html")).map((p) => [p, text(files.get(p)!)]));

  // 1. Inventory and provenance.
  for (const p of paths) if (!ADMITTED.some((re) => re.test(p))) add("inventory", `${p} is not admitted by the provenance rule`);
  for (const p of HOSTED_PAGES) if (!files.has(PAGE_HTML(p))) add("inventory", `the page /${p} is missing`);

  // 2. No source maps.
  for (const p of paths) if (/sourceMappingURL|\.map$/.test(p) || /[#@]\s*sourceMappingURL=/.test(text(files.get(p)!))) add("no-source-maps", `${p} carries or references a source map`);

  // 3. No server code: no function, no middleware, routes to files only.
  for (const p of paths) if (p.startsWith("functions/") || p.includes("/functions/")) add("no-server-code", `${p} is a function`);
  let config: any = null;
  try { config = JSON.parse(text(files.get("config.json") ?? Buffer.from("null"))); } catch { add("no-server-code", "config.json is not JSON"); }
  if (!config || config.version !== 3) add("no-server-code", "config.json is missing or not Build Output API version 3");
  else {
    for (const k of Object.keys(config)) if (k !== "version" && k !== "routes") add("no-server-code", `config.json has a top-level key ${k}`);
    for (const [i, r] of (config.routes ?? []).entries()) {
      for (const k of Object.keys(r)) if (!["src", "dest", "headers", "continue", "handle"].includes(k)) add("no-server-code", `a route has the key ${k}`);
      // ⚠ Every reader of the policy (this gate, the browser check, the live
      // comparison) reads the FIRST route's headers. A later route's headers
      // would be read by none of them, so only the first may set any
      // (`REVIEW-059` S-1).
      if (i > 0 && r.headers !== undefined) add("csp", `route ${i} sets headers; only the first route may, because it is the only one any check reads`);
      if (r.handle !== undefined && r.handle !== "filesystem") add("no-server-code", `a route hands off to ${r.handle}`);
      if (r.dest !== undefined && (typeof r.dest !== "string" || !files.has(`static${r.dest}`))) add("no-server-code", `a route's dest ${JSON.stringify(r.dest)} is not a file in the output`);
    }
  }

  // 4. No socket client, signer path, decryption routine, secret name or build variable.
  const NEEDLES = [...SOCKET_NEEDLES, ...SECRET_NEEDLES, "WebAssembly", ".wasm", "ProjectivePoint", "mptool", "mpt_", "elgamal_", "VITE_", "WCONF_", "eval(", "new Function"];
  for (const p of paths) {
    if (p === "static/third-party-licenses.txt") continue;
    const t = text(files.get(p)!);
    for (const n of NEEDLES) if (t.includes(n)) add("no-socket-secret-or-decryption", `${p} contains ${JSON.stringify(n)}`);
    for (const api of ["new WebSocket", "EventSource(", "sendBeacon", "XMLHttpRequest"]) if (t.includes(api)) add("no-other-connection", `${p} uses ${api}`);
  }

  // 5. Absolute URLs, against the admitted list.
  for (const p of paths) {
    if (p === "static/third-party-licenses.txt") continue;
    for (const m of text(files.get(p)!).matchAll(/\bhttps?:\/\/[A-Za-z0-9.:/#_-]+/g)) {
      const u = m[0].replace(/[.:]+$/, "");
      if (!ADMITTED_URLS.some(([re]) => re.test(u))) add("urls", `${p} names ${u}`);
    }
  }
  // ⚠ A scheme-relative address (`src="//host/x.js"`) has no `https:` for the
  // pattern above to match (`REVIEW-059` R1-2's table), and an encoded one
  // (`&#47;&#47;host`, `https&#58;//host`) has neither (R2-3). So every
  // URL-bearing attribute is decoded first, and each `srcset` candidate is read.
  const URL_ATTRS = /\b(src|href|action|formaction|srcset|poster|data|background|xlink:href)\s*=\s*(?:"([^"]*)"|'([^']*)'|([^\s>]+))/gi;
  for (const [p, h] of html) {
    for (const m of h.matchAll(URL_ATTRS)) {
      const value = decodeEntities(m[2] ?? m[3] ?? m[4] ?? "").trim();
      const candidates = m[1].toLowerCase() === "srcset" ? value.split(",").map((c) => c.trim().split(/\s+/)[0]) : [value];
      // ⚠ Resolved, not prefix-tested: a tab between the slashes or a
      // backslash is `//` to a URL parser (`REVIEW-059` R3-3).
      for (const c of candidates) {
        const cleaned = c.replace(/[\t\n\r]/g, "");
        let u: URL;
        try { u = new URL(cleaned, SAME_ORIGIN); } catch { add("urls", `${p} has an attribute URL that does not parse: ${JSON.stringify(c)}`); continue; }
        if (u.origin === new URL(SAME_ORIGIN).origin) continue;
        if (/^[\\/]{2}/.test(cleaned)) add("urls", `${p} names the scheme-relative ${c}`);
        else if (!ADMITTED_URLS.some(([re]) => re.test(cleaned.replace(/[.:]+$/, "")) || re.test(u.href.replace(/\/$/, "")))) add("urls", `${p} names ${c} in an attribute`);
      }
    }
  }

  // 6. The Content Security Policy, as config.json delivers it.
  const first = config?.routes?.[0];
  const csp: string | undefined = first?.src === "/(.*)" && first?.continue === true ? first?.headers?.["Content-Security-Policy"] : undefined;
  if (!csp) add("csp", "the first route does not set a Content-Security-Policy on every path");
  else {
    // ⚠ A browser enforces the FIRST of two directives with one name and
    // ignores the second; a Map keeps the LAST. So `connect-src *;` placed
    // before the real one passed this check while the page permitted a
    // connection anywhere (`REVIEW-059` R1-1, measured). A repeated name, in
    // any case, is refused before anything else is read.
    const parsed = csp.split(";").map((d) => d.trim()).filter(Boolean).map((d) => { const [k, ...v] = d.split(/\s+/); return [k, v] as const; });
    const seen = new Set<string>();
    for (const [k] of parsed) { if (seen.has(k.toLowerCase())) add("csp", `the directive ${k} appears twice; a browser enforces the first`); seen.add(k.toLowerCase()); }
    const dirs = new Map(parsed);
    const exact: Record<string, string[]> = {
      "default-src": ["'self'"], "style-src": ["'self'"], "img-src": ["'self'"], "font-src": ["'self'"],
      "connect-src": ["'self'"], "object-src": ["'none'"], "base-uri": ["'none'"], "form-action": ["'none'"], "frame-ancestors": ["'none'"],
    };
    for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(exact)) if (JSON.stringify(dirs.get(k) ?? null) !== JSON.stringify(v)) add("csp", `${k} is ${JSON.stringify(dirs.get(k) ?? null)}, not ${v.join(" ")}`);
    for (const k of dirs.keys()) if (!(k in exact) && k !== "script-src") add("csp", `an unexpected directive ${k}`);

    const allowed = new Set(script.slice(1));
    for (const tok of allowed) if (!/^'sha256-[A-Za-z0-9+/]+=*'$/.test(tok)) add("csp", `script-src admits ${tok}`);
    const needed = new Set([...html.values()].flatMap((h) => inlineScripts(h).map(scriptHash)));
    for (const h of needed) if (!allowed.has(h)) add("csp", `an inline script's hash ${h} is not admitted, so the page would not run`);
    for (const h of allowed) if (!needed.has(h)) add("csp", `script-src admits ${h}, which no page carries`);
    const names = Object.keys(first.headers ?? {});
    for (const n of names) if (!(n in EXPECTED_HEADERS)) add("csp", `the first route sets ${n}, which is not one of the expected headers`);
    const lower = names.map((n) => n.toLowerCase());
    if (new Set(lower).size !== lower.length) add("csp", "the first route sets one header name twice in different case");
    for (const [h, v] of Object.entries(EXPECTED_HEADERS)) if (v !== null && first.headers?.[h] !== v) add("csp", `${h} is not ${v}`);
  }

| Tx | Type | Delegable | ZKProof | Proof obligation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ConfidentialMPTConvert` | 85 | **No** | 64 B, optional | Schnorr PoK, only when registering the holder key. Otherwise the disclosed `BlindingFactor` lets validators verify deterministically with no ZKP. |
| `ConfidentialMPTMergeInbox` | 86 | Yes | none | Proof-free. Value is known to ledger state. |
| `ConfidentialMPTConvertBack` | 87 | Yes | 816 B | 128 B sigma (balance ownership, key linkage) + 688 B single Bulletproof |
| `ConfidentialMPTSend` | 88 | Yes | **946 B**, fixed | 192 B sigma (ciphertext consistency across sender/receiver/issuer/auditor, amount linkage, balance linkage) + 754 B aggregated Bulletproof over two 64-bit values |
| `ConfidentialMPTClawback` | 89 | Yes | 64 B | Sigma proof that `sfIssuerEncryptedBalance` encrypts the stated plaintext |

**~~Byte values are Grade B pending GAP-6.~~ Grade A as of 2026-08-16.** `Protocol.h:466–471` verifies the *structure* — `kEcSendProofLength = kEcSendSigmaProofLength + kEcDoubleBulletproofLength`, enforced at preflight. The macros it resolves to are now read at mpt-crypto `0.4.0-rc4`: `SECP256K1_COMPACT_STANDARD_PROOF_SIZE 192`, `kMPT_DOUBLE_BULLETPROOF_SIZE 754`, `kMPT_SINGLE_BULLETPROOF_SIZE 688`, `SECP256K1_COMPACT_CONVERTBACK_PROOF_SIZE 128`, `SECP256K1_COMPACT_CLAWBACK_PROOF_SIZE 64`, `kMPT_SCHNORR_PROOF_SIZE 64`. Every value in the table above is confirmed. The header additionally derives each sigma size from its scalar count — send is (e, z_m, z_r, z_b, z_rho, z_sk) ∈ Z_q⁶ = 192 B — so the numbers are internally cross-checked as well.
